# Joshua White, six true-education playlists: per-talk notes

Joshua White, A Thinking Generation Ministries (early-childhood education researcher, "11 years in this work" as of 2023). Written 2026-10-13. These are third-party talks: everything below is his material (or another speaker's), reported as data in our own summary words. Nothing here is the owner's wording.

- **Raw transcripts** (git-ignored, never committed): `tmp/content-series/education/transcripts/white/<playlist>/NN-<slug>.txt` in the warm source, plus `<videoid>.json` with per-caption timestamps and `index.tsv`. Each `.txt` groups the auto-captions into ~30 s lines stamped `[h:mm:ss]`.
- **Tool.** `ytt --json` fetched all 49 videos (31 in A–C, 18 in D–F); the `yt-dlp` fallback was not needed. Playlists D–F were added later on 2026-10-13.
- **EGW check.** Every quotation's status is in `verification.md` (721 White rows: 685 VERIFIED, 27 DIFFERS, 9 NOT FOUND; A–C 460, D–F 261). Statuses are marked inline here. DIFFERS and NOT FOUND items are flagged where they occur.
- **Conventions.** Times are `h:mm:ss` into the video (about ±30 s, from the line stamps). "Needs a primary source" means he names nothing checkable. Captions mishear names; the notes use the corrected forms (for example "Djokovic"/"Jacob" = Jochebed, "mileination" = myelination, "Leonard Saxs" = Leonard Sax, "Shiers" = Shyers, "YouTube Pine" = probably Uchee Pines). "SDP" is S. N. Haskell, _The Story of Daniel the Prophet_ (1901), the pioneer source of the series' Daniel booklet.

## The six playlists

D, E and F follow C below, each with its own talk table; their dedupe against A–C is stated per talk ("delta to …").

Resolved with `yt-dlp --flat-playlist` on 2026-10-13. A and B resolve with the short ids as given.

**D–F in one line.** D (3 talks, Autumn Leaves NZ, January 2018), E (8 unique talks, 2021–2023, A Thinking Generation Ministries' own playlist) and F (7 talks, Uchee Pines Institute, 2016 and 2018) are all Joshua White. Full talks: D2, D3, E1, F5; the rest are deltas.

**Speakers.** 29 of the 31 videos in A–C are Joshua White. Jennifer White gives none of them: speakers were identified from each opening and from YouTube's mid-video frames, where the Zoom tile labels show the camera account, not the speaker. "Mothers in Israel" (C8), the likeliest candidate, is Joshua White on camera throughout. The two other speakers (C3, C7) are kept apart at the end of this file and in their own sub-table in `verification.md`.

**Dedupe.** Three talks repeat across B and C: Daniel Chapter Zero (B2 full, C10 delta), Not Recognizing Jesus (C13 full, B4 delta) and Called to Leave / Time to Leave (B8 full, C12 delta). Inside the playlists, talks overlap by theme:

- A6 0:30–0:57 is announced as "Daniel chapter zero" and condenses B2.
- A5 and B1 teach the "Deuteronomy method" that C6 gives in full.
- A1, B6 and C1 open with the same frame (education = redemption, Ed 13.1 and 30.2, the leaning demonstration).
- A2, B7 and C4 cover start age.
- A7, C2 and C5 cover media.

Repeated quotations are marked "dup" in `verification.md`.

**Running cautions** (detail under each talk):

- **An insider audience.** He speaks to Adventist insiders: "the Spirit of Prophecy", "remnant", Sabbath school, Ellen White by name, Battle Creek, Sutherland and Madison. B7's whole frame is denominational history (1844, 1863, 1872, 1888, the Blair Bill). None of it is spoken in the series.
- **Brain, eye, hormone and media science used as proof** of the counsel (A2, A3, A7, C2, C4, C5). Most of it is unsourced, and several claims look wrong (flagged per talk). The series rule stands: present it as counsel, not as brain science.
- **A Reformation → Jesuits → Prussia → United States lineage** (B7, C1, C2), milder than Ritsema's but the same skeleton. Steer around it as for Ritsema.
- **Haskell read as if it were EGW.** He reads Haskell's _Story of Daniel_ several times without naming him (A6, C13), including SDP 49.1, "From what homes will come the Daniels…".
- **Rhetoric that condemns families' choices:** "socialization is a lie" (A2), the "insanity" line and "send our children to Caesar" (A8), video games (C5), and university as "extremely dangerous" (C14). He is milder than Ritsema overall: moving the desk home "only changed the location" (A4, B7), and the 1904 interview he reads lets parents choose the public school (A4).
- **Fiction:** C8 opens with an imaginative Jochebed story he did not write, and A7 extends "light reading" to true stories read for entertainment. See `white-review.md` §4.3.

## A · True Education Event, Bogenhofen, Austria 2026

---

### A1 · Back to Basics (nLgQNHK18Og) · 1:05:19 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/01-back-to-basics.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- True education is "redemption through knowing God resulting in transformation". Education and redemption are one (Ed 30.2), and "to know God" (John 17:3; COL 114.2) is its core.
- Knowing God comes in four stages: through the parents (infancy), through His works (childhood, outdoors), through His word (from about 8–10), and through fellowship with Him in service (youth). The goal is harmonious physical, mental and spiritual development, with every subject made practical "missionary training".
- Noah's ark is the frame: he followed God's exact plan, built it with his sons, and his faith was seen in what he did.

**Outline**

- 0:00:00–0:02:17 Welcome, prayer, the ministry (Mal 4:6) and the theme "Preparing an Ark".
- 0:02:54–0:09:51 Heb 11:7: Noah prepared an ark "to the saving of his house"; PP 98.1 on "parental fidelity". The sons helped build it. Built faith, not preached faith, condemned the world. God was designer and Noah builder (PP 92.3). Noah did not trust God about the crisis while picking his own preparation.
- 0:09:51–0:12:44 Success in education depends on fidelity to the Creator's plan (Ed 50.4). Audience definitions; his formula: redemption, knowing God, transformation.
- 0:12:44–0:16:39 Redemption = training for heaven (Ed 30.2; DA 330.2). John 17:3 / COL 114.2: "This is true education". CG 494.2 contrasts "to know God" with "the world's standard"; the first lesson is the will of God.
- 0:16:39–0:19:28 Stage 1, infancy: knowing God through mom and dad. Parents "stand in the place of God" (AH 293.1). Deut 6:6–7 as a method for every age that starts with the parent's own heart.
- 0:20:02–0:28:35 Stage 2, childhood: knowing God through His works. Not "books about nature" but nature (CT 80.1; CG 300.3–300.4; Ed 100.2–101.1). In the first 6–7 years the stress falls on the physical (CG 300.3; Ed 195.1; CG 103.4). **Live demo at 0:25:46:** two men lean on him ("spiritual" and "mental" resting on "physical"); he steps away and they fall.
- 0:28:35–0:34:17 Stage 3, about 8–10: knowing God through His word. The Bible is the first textbook when mental study begins, "not math and science" (CT 108.3; Ed 124.2; MH 401.4; Ed 191.4). "Meditation all the day" (Ps 119:97) is the baseline, not a ceiling.
- 0:34:17–0:37:39 Stage 4, youth: knowing God through service (Ed 264.3; SC 79.1, 80.1; MH 395.1, 409.2).
- 0:37:39–0:45:19 Transformation = harmonious development (CT 37.1; Ed 13.1). Physical: chores (folding clothes, firewood), trade (CT 307.1; Ed 218.2; Paul), agriculture (Ed 219.1; Sutherland; CT 534.1).
- 0:45:19–0:57:11 Mental: thinkers, not reflectors (Ed 17.2). "When will I ever use this?" trains learning without doing, which is spiritually dangerous (James 2:19). Subjects made useful: math through budgeting, science through health, history as God's story, geography for mission, calculus "maybe later" (MH 449.5; MH 402.2).
- 0:57:11–1:05:19 Worldly education vs "seek ye first" (CT 64.2–64.3). Einstein quip; 11MR 339.1. **One-suitcase illustration:** we take only character to heaven (COL 332.3; Mark 8:36).

**Bible texts**

- 0:02:17 Mal 4:6 (paraphrased).
- 0:02:54, 0:06:24 Heb 11:7 (quoted).
- 0:04:02 Gen 7:1 (inside PP 98.1).
- 0:14:24 John 17:3 (quoted).
- 0:18:56 Deut 6:6–7 (quoted/paraphrased).
- 0:33:08 Ps 119:97 (quoted).
- 0:41:00 Paul the tentmaker (Acts 18:3, alluded).
- 0:49:16 James 2:19 (paraphrased).
- 0:58:18 Matt 6:33, Prov 9:10 (inside CT 64.3).
- 0:59:26 2 Thess 1:10 (inside 11MR 339.1).
- 1:03:26 Mark 8:36 (quoted).
- 0:34:50 the Great Commission (alluded).

**EGW quotes** (all VERIFIED unless marked; detail in `verification.md`)

- 0:04:02 PP 98.1 ("What encouragement to parental fidelity").
- 0:05:08 "Let parents beware…" = ST Nov 10, 1881 par. 8. It reads as if from PP but is a Signs article.
- 0:07:31 PP 92.3. 0:09:17 PP 97.3. 0:10:22 Ed 50.4. 0:12:44 Ed 30.2. 0:13:17 DA 330.2. 0:14:24 COL 114.2. 0:15:32 CG 494.2.
- 0:16:06 "first great lesson in true education" = AUGleaner Jan 27, 1909. FE 414.2 has "all education".
- 0:16:39 "true education is to know and to do the will of God" = 13LtMs Ms 135, 1898.
- 0:17:15 AH 293.1 (= PP 308.2). 0:20:36 CT 80.1 (also FE 21.2). 0:21:44 CG 300.4. 0:22:18 Ed 100.2. 0:23:27 Ed 100.3 + 101.1. 0:24:37 CG 300.3. 0:25:11 Ed 195.1. 0:27:28 CG 103.4.
- 0:30:20 CT 108.3. 0:31:26 Ed 124.2. 0:32:30 MH 401.4 (CG 296.2). 0:33:08 Ed 191.4. 0:34:50 Ed 264.3. 0:35:25 SC 79.1, SC 80.1. 0:35:57 MH 395.1. 0:37:05 MH 409.2. 0:37:39 CT 37.1. 0:38:11 Ed 13.1.
- 0:40:26 7BC 912.3. 0:40:58 CT 307.1. 0:42:03 Ed 218.2. 0:42:35 Ed 219.1.
- 0:44:15 "Eden… not only Adam's dwelling but his schoolroom" = GCDB Mar 6, 1899 par. 36 (attribution note in verif). 0:44:15 CT 534.1.
- 0:46:25 Ed 17.2. 0:47:00 MH 449.5. 0:56:37 MH 402.2 (**context:** "use" there means imparting knowledge to others). 0:57:11 CT 64.2. 0:58:18 CT 64.3. 0:59:26 11MR 339.1. 1:02:17 COL 332.3.

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:43:07 E. A. Sutherland, _Living Fountains or Broken Cisterns_: the Eden-garden paragraph. Not in the local corpus; needs a primary source (the 1900 book).
- 0:58:51 "Albert Einstein": "Education is what remains after one has forgotten what one has learned in school." Einstein quoted this in 1936 ("On Education") as the saying of "a wit". It is not his own line.
- 0:53:47 He "Googled" the least useful subject: history. Anecdotal.

**Strongest arguments**

- The three-man demonstration (0:25:46): spiritual and mental both rest on physical (Ed 195.1). Visual and memorable.
- "A book about nature isn't nature" (0:20:02). Ed 100.2's child who perceives "the Presence" before he can read.
- "Learning without applying trains hearing without doing" (0:47:35–0:49:16). Years of "you'll use it someday" carry over into sermons we admire and never practice. MH 449.5: "It is not enough even to have knowledge."
- Start with the application and let it pull in the subject (0:51:30): budgeting before arithmetic drills.
- One suitcase to heaven (1:00:01–1:02:52): only character goes (COL 332.3).
- Noah's sons built the ark with him (0:05:08). The family was invested before the crisis.

**History / statistics claims**

- 0:42:35 Gardening research: more self-confidence, patience, "better test scores". Needs a primary source.
- 0:21:12 "About 8 for girls, about 10 for boys" is his gloss on "eight or ten". The text gives no sex split.

**Cautions for the series**

- He calls her writings "the Spirit of Prophecy" and names books and Sutherland ("the great educator"). The school farm text (CT 534.1) is from the Madison school testimony, which is institutional.
- 0:29:12–0:30:52 "According to Spirit of Prophecy… at 8 we start with Bible training", not academics. CT 108.3 gives no age, so the age link is his inference.
- He makes Moses and Paul missionary-training models. Fine.
- "Calculus maybe not" is a light jab. Harmless.
- Stories:
  - the parent who feared a child "too spiritual" (personal, true by his account);
  - Romania and Belgium travel times (true, audience);
  - the suitcase (illustration).
- No fiction. Nothing on Daniel 1 or public schools here.

**Series mapping**

- no. 1 (What is school for?): Ed 30.2 + DA 330.2 ("redemption… trained for heaven"); COL 114.2 (John 17:3 "This is true education"); CT 64.2–64.3 (two kinds of education); COL 332.3 (one-suitcase payoff).
- no. 3 (Head, hands, heart): Ed 195.1 with the leaning-men demo; Ed 13.1.
- no. 4 (Teaching and saving are one): Ed 30.2; DA 330.2; CG 494.2 ("to know God… or the world's standard?").
- no. 9 (Your child's first teacher): AH 293.1 / PP 308.2. The child's first picture of God is the parent. That is his gloss; the text is about parental authority.
- no. 10 (When should kids start?): CG 300.3 ("first six or seven years… physical training rather than the intellect"); CG 300.4; CT 80.1.
- no. 11 (Garden best classroom) and no. 40 (Nature, the second book): Ed 100.2–101.1 ("In no other way can the foundation… be so firmly and surely laid").
- no. 2 (Thinkers): Ed 17.2.
- no. 31 (Trade): CT 307.1; Ed 218.2; 7BC 912.3. no. 27 (Paul): needed his trade as a missionary, not as a Sanhedrin member.
- no. 39 (Bible as schoolbook): Ed 124.2; CT 108.3; Ed 191.4.
- no. 81 / 82 (Bread before algebra / Higher math, no checkbook): "graduate and can't clean a bathroom"; budgeting-first math.
- no. 86 (Serve now): Ed 264.3; SC 79.1–80.1; MH 395.1.
- **NEW candidate: "Learning you never use".** Promise: does school train children to hear and not do? KJV: James 1:22–25; Matt 7:24–27; James 2:19. EGW: MH 449.5; MH 402.2 (in context, imparting); 8T 311.4.
- **NEW candidate: "Noah's sons built the ark".** Promise: a family that works on God's plan together is ready when the crisis comes. KJV: Heb 11:7; Gen 6:22; 7:1. EGW: PP 92.3, 97.3, 98.1; ST Nov 10, 1881 par. 8.

### A2 · Early Growth, Late Fruit: How to Lay a Good Foundation in Childhood (OSev4eaaSo8) · 1:30:43 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/02-early-growth-late-fruit.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- The counsel "parents the only teachers until eight or ten, nature the textbook, the open air the schoolroom" is backed by development: brain, eyes, movement, speech, emotion, social life, memory and reading. Early academics do harm, and late starters catch up and pass early starters.
- The counsel's real point is positive. The early years are for character, and that work takes all of a parent's time.
- Spiritual fruit should come late too. There were no "junior Levites", and God's servants (Joseph, Moses, Joshua, David, Elisha, Jesus at 30) grew in obscurity. Children should not be put on display.

**Outline**

- 0:01:10–0:06:40 Fruit-tree grafting: rootstock, scion, about 3 years to bond. A first-year fruiting spur breaks the graft. Weakened for life (true horticulture used as an object lesson).
- 0:06:40–0:10:46 CG/MH 380.2 (early habits outweigh later training). FE 21.2 + CT 80.1. A Q&A point: "only textbook" vs "most familiar textbook". A "scientific article": no evidence that earlier is better.
- 0:11:20–0:21:54 Brain: myelination schedule; limbic system to 4½; "gestalt" to 7 (girls) and 9 (boys); logic areas after. Forcing early routes learning through "lower" systems (Healy, _Endangered Minds_). The word-problem "blank stare". Learn by doing, then paper.
- 0:22:27–0:35:59 Eyes: binocular focus to 7–9; myopia from near work plus too little time outdoors. US ~50%, East Asia ~90%, Seoul 96%, Africa 10–15%; the Alaska Inuit study; retinal dopamine; 3 hours at 10,000 lux vs a classroom's ~500. Anecdote: a friend's daughter outdoors 2 h a day stopped progressing. Claim: glasses worsen myopia, which "can heal".
- 0:35:59–0:42:15 "Sit still, be quiet": movement → BDNF → myelination; "free as lambs" (CT 79.1), with parents nearby like the ewe. Outer speech; Hannaford: silent reading is ineffective before 7.
- 0:42:15–0:47:31 Emotional security as a survival need (the lost-hiker "time to brush up on algebra" joke). Raymond Moore, _School Can Wait_. Socialization: "a lie"; Shyers and Smedley studies; mixed ages vs same-age peers.
- 0:47:31–0:50:48 Memory: unschooled peoples (Aboriginal song cycles, herds). His Tanzania Maasai cattle anecdote.
- 0:50:48–0:59:04 Reading: language first; talk, answer questions, interesting books. RIA 5 vs 7 study (the late group catches up by 9 and is ahead at 11); Montessori advantage gone by 12; a "zero reading instruction" district study.
- 0:59:04–1:04:42 ADHD: a Stanford study on delayed entry (−73%); 300 late starters; a staircase example of math repetition vs a 20-minute lesson at the right age.
- 1:05:14–1:07:32 Turn: the counsel is about doing character work, not just about avoiding academics.
- 1:07:32–1:21:54 Spiritual development is also not to be rushed. Levites 25/30; Joseph 30, Moses 80, Joshua 60+, David 30, Solomon early 20s, Elisha poured water, Samuel (an exception), Jesus 30. DA 74.3; OHC 57.4; Ed 237.3 (don't foster love of praise). "Up front" programs and stage fright; children preaching at the end (7T 26.4 paraphrase).
- 1:21:54–1:30:43 Music lessons later (8–10); FLB 242.5. Youth go out with experienced workers (Ev 109.1, 684.1). He has seen young missionaries "lose their way"; whole families serving works better. Closing: Mark 4:28, corn needs water early and dryness late (Ed 106.3–107.2). "Consider moving to an area that makes it easier."

**Bible texts**

- 0:32:40 Prov 26:2 "the curse causeless" (paraphrased, in an anecdote).
- 1:08:43 Num 4:3; 8:24 (Levites at 30 / 25, paraphrased).
- 1:10:25 Gen 41:46 (quoted ref).
- 1:11:00 Moses at 80 (Acts 7:23, 30; Ex 7:7, alluded).
- 1:12:07 Joshua as spy, then 40 years (Num 13–14, alluded).
- 1:12:39 David 30 (2 Sam 5:4, alluded).
- 1:13:13 Solomon "a little child" (1 Kgs 3:7, paraphrased).
- 1:13:50 Elisha "poured water on the hands of Elijah" (2 Kgs 3:11, quoted).
- 1:14:26 Samuel (1 Sam 2–3, alluded).
- 1:15:01 Jesus 30 (Luke 3:23).
- 1:16:42 "grew and waxed strong in spirit" (Luke 2:40, in OHC 57.4).
- 1:26:33 Mark 4:28 (in Ed 106.3).

**EGW quotes** (all VERIFIED)

- 0:06:40 MH 380.2 (= CG 184.3).
- 0:07:16 FE 21.2 (+ CT 80.1 wording; dup A1).
- 0:37:44 CT 79.1.
- 1:16:09 DA 74.3.
- 1:16:42 OHC 57.4 (Lt 17, 1878).
- 1:17:52 Ed 237.3.
- 1:21:54 FLB 242.5 (Lt 6a, 1890).
- 1:23:05 Ev 109.1.
- 1:23:40 Ev 684.1.
- 1:26:33 Ed 106.3 + 107.2.
- Paraphrases, not counted:
  - 1:20:40 children preaching at the end (7T 26.4; GC 366.3);
  - 1:10:58 Joseph's home training (Ed 52);
  - 1:12:07 young men under older workers (Ev 683.2; GW 101.1);
  - 1:14:26 Eli raised Samuel (PP 573).

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:10:12 A "scientific article" summary (no evidence that earlier is better; play; damage from early literacy). It is not named; it resembles Whitebread/Bingham (TACTYC 2012). Needs a primary source.
- 0:18:31, 0:21:19 Jane M. Healy, _Endangered Minds_ (1990): two quotations. Checkable book; page not given.
- 0:41:08 Carla Hannaford (_Smart Moves_): silent reading ineffective before 7. Needs a page.
- 0:45:50 Raymond Moore, _School Can Wait_ (named); "a quote from Harvard" (not named).
- 0:46:26 Larry Shyers and Thomas Smedley socialization studies (1992 theses). Checkable.
- 0:50:14 The "primitive peoples" memory passage: unnamed book. Needs a primary source.
- 0:54:37 RIA 5 vs 7 study. Matches Suggate, Schaughency & Reese (2013, New Zealand). Checkable.
- 0:57:25 Montessori vs public school at age 12; 0:57:58 the two-district kindergarten study; 1:00:15 "300 individuals" starting at 8+. All need a primary source.
- 0:59:40 "Stanford University" study on delaying kindergarten, 73% less ADHD behavior. Dee & Sievertsen (Danish data); roughly right as stated for inattention/hyperactivity at age 7.

**Strongest arguments**

- The grafted tree that fruits in year one: delight now, weakness for life (0:03:22–0:06:40). A clean visual allegory. Pair it with Ed 107.2's "precocious maturity".
- "The counsel is not mainly about what not to do; it's about what to do: character, full-time" (1:05:46–1:06:54).
- Thirty years of obscurity: there are a few verses on Jesus' childhood and half the New Testament on His ministry, and DA 74.3 says the silence itself teaches (1:15:33–1:17:15).
- Ed 237.3: don't "repeat before them their clever sayings". Parents often want the display more than the child does (1:17:52–1:18:59).
- Language is the foundation for reading: talk with children and answer their questions; many teach themselves when ready (0:51:53–0:54:04).
- Young workers go "in company with a worker of experience" (Ev 109.1).

**History / statistics claims** (all need a primary source unless noted)

- Myelination stage ages and the "two-year" boy/girl gap. Left/right hemisphere lists.
- France's compulsory age is 3: correct since 2019. "Scandinavia 7": partly dated.
- Eye focus refined by 7–9, foveal by 11–12.
- Myopia: US ~50%, Finland ~50%, East Asia 90%, Seoul 96% (Jung et al. 2012, Seoul 19-year-old men: real), Africa 10–15%.
- **Alaska: wrong as framed.** "When the US bought it from Russia it became a state" mixes 1867 and 1959. The Barrow Inuit study (Young et al. 1969: children ~58%, parents and grandparents near 0) is real.
- Retinal dopamine; 3 h a day at 10,000 lux; classroom ~500 lux; average under ½ h outdoors.
- **"Glasses cause myopia to progress"** and **"the eye can heal"**: look wrong or contested (full-correction vs under-correction trials went the other way). Medical-advice territory.
- BDNF is "required for myelination"; outer speech internalizes by about 8.
- Unschooled peoples have "the best memories"; earliest-reading countries have "the lowest literacy".
- Homeschoolers "three times better socialized" (Shyers/Smedley).
- ADHD −73% with a one-year delay (see above).

**Cautions for the series**

- **Brain science as proof.** Most of the talk's evidence is neuro/eye/hormone research; series no. 10 is to "present as counsel, not as brain science". Use at most one lead, and never say "almost permanent organizational damage" or "learning disability" as caused by early school.
- **"Socialization is a lie… I said lie"** (0:45:50) and "putting them in a group of equal age peers" yields poor socialization. This is the same register as Ritsema §4.2/4.4. The series keeps no. 87/85 to principles: CT 177.1 "sometimes a hindrance".
- **Start age split by sex** (8 girls / 10 boys) is his, not the text's. FE 21.2 and CT 80.1 say "eight or ten" with no split.
- **"No junior Levites" overstates.** Under David the Levites served "from twenty years old and above" (1 Chr 23:24, 27), and Samuel "ministered unto the Lord, being a child" (1 Sam 2:18). He concedes Samuel. Also Josiah, Jeremiah's "I am a child" and Timothy. The pattern is real (Luke 3:23; Gen 41:46), but it is a pattern, not a law.
- **Tension with no. 86** ("You learn to swim in the water: serve now"). White says don't push children into public roles, and that youth serve under experienced workers (Ev 109.1, 684.1). The series can hold both: youth serve now, alongside, not on display.
- Children preaching at the end: his paraphrase says it is miraculous "because there is no option for the adults". 7T 26.4 does not say that; GC 366.3 (Sweden, 1843–44) shows children preaching under a power beyond their own, where the law silenced adult preachers. Denominational history; steer around.
- "Consider moving to an area that makes it easier" (1:29:19): advice to homeschoolers under state rules. Not series material.
- Stories:
  - the friend's daughter's myopia (second-hand, true by his account);
  - Maasai cattle (second-hand);
  - the pear spur from a forum (true);
  - the lost hiker (hypothetical illustration);
  - the grafted tree and the corn (object lessons, true horticulture).
- No fiction.

**Series mapping**

- no. 10 (Start age): FE 21.2; CT 79.1 ("free as lambs… lay the foundation for sound constitutions"); CT 80.1; MH 380.2. Plus his turn: the counsel's aim is character work.
- no. 70 (Too much, too soon): Ed 107.2 ("Children should not be forced into a precocious maturity"); the grafting allegory; Mark 4:28 (Ed 106.3).
- no. 35 (Why kids can't sit still): CT 79.1; "sit still and be quiet" as the two classroom commands (health claims are leads only).
- no. 41 (Readers are made at home) and no. 54 (A hundred questions): talk, answer, ask back.
- no. 42 (Who gives the credit?): Ed 237.3, whole paragraph.
- no. 17 (Did Jesus go to school?): DA 74.3; OHC 57.4 (Lt 17, 1878: "comparative obscurity, but of the highest importance").
- no. 25 (Elisha): 2 Kgs 3:11 "poured water on the hands of Elijah".
- no. 23 (Moses): "tried to bear fruit too soon".
- no. 87 (Socialization) and no. 85 (Age-sorting): mixed ages; leads only.
- no. 86 (Serve now): Ev 109.1 and 684.1 as the counterweight.
- **NEW candidate: "Thirty quiet years".** Promise: should children be put up front? KJV: Luke 3:23; 2:51–52; Gen 41:46; 2 Kgs 3:11; 1 Kgs 3:7. EGW: DA 74.3; OHC 57.4; Ed 237.3; Ed 107.2.
- **NEW candidate (or fold into no. 70): "The tree that fruits too early"**, the grafting allegory. KJV: Mark 4:28; Eccl 3:1. EGW: Ed 106.3–107.2.

### A3 · How We Can Raise Our Boys to Be Godly Men (02y2jYTt9Ec) · 1:08:06 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/03-raising-boys.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Manhood is in crisis because of culture, education, a failure to raise boys right, and "an intentional attack by Satan". God's purpose for men is godly leadership: head, protector, priest, "house-band".
- Boys differ from girls in brain, senses, development and emotional needs. School treats "boys like defective girls", and medicating their energy suppresses the drive God meant for manhood.
- Remedies: later and more active learning, plenty of real hard work, intentional emotional connection (especially from mothers, on the boy's terms), and men who mentor boys into using "strength in the service of others".

**Outline**

- 0:00:00–0:04:30 "Boys will be girls". WWI volunteers (Britain, underage enlistees, "nearly 2 million"). The "1943 lied about age / 2023 lie about gender" line.
- 0:05:07–0:11:25 God's purpose for men:
  - AH 215.1 ("house-band");
  - 1T 547.1 (lawmaker, sterner virtues, priest);
  - AH 212.1 (father confesses the family's sins);
  - CME 38.1–38.4 (young men as light-bearers);
  - GW 290.2 / TM 407.2 ("men like Caleb");
  - Ed 57.3 ("men", read as males);
  - CD 407.3 as a joke.
- 0:12:00–0:12:34 Male religiosity statistics.
- 0:13:07–0:25:39 Attack 1, gender sameness:
  - brain differences; toy studies (12 cultures, then monkeys);
  - two visual systems; children's drawings (his Mexico photo, a friend's children);
  - hearing (girls hear ~3× louder), smell;
  - vocabulary 40 vs 90 words at 18 months; Sax's "below the childhood average" story;
  - mutual balancing (NOT FOUND as a quote).
- 0:25:39–0:36:31 Attack 2, school:
  - boys' grade, discipline, LD, ADHD, suspension and dropout shares;
  - brain halfway at 11 vs 15, maturity at 22 vs 30; Sax on "too much too soon";
  - "treating boys like defective girls"; ADHD as a stress response.
- 0:36:31–0:42:44 Work: RH June 17, 1880 ("nothing so good to keep boys from being ruined… as plenty of work"); 28% of parents assign chores; AH 90.2 (habit of enjoying useful labor); real, hard, outdoor work; "create a lifestyle in which your children are needed" (his blog). Play with them too.
- 0:42:44–0:49:29 Attack 3, chemicals: ADHD medications (dopamine, "damage the motivational center"); sleep loss; endocrine disruptors ("male hormone levels dropped 50% in 50 years"); plastics; media dopamine vs the reward of work.
- 0:49:29–1:02:52 Attack 5, lack of connection:
  - boys are more emotionally needy but show it differently;
  - still-face sex differences; Schore quotations on right-brain regulation;
  - fathers attend to girls' sadness and boys' anger (so sons learn "my anger is noticed, my sadness is not");
  - unconnected boys become "more alone" and turn to games or rebellion;
  - the oil-change story (mother connects with her son).
- 1:02:52–1:06:22 Attack 6, collapse of the social image of manhood: role models matter but are not the strongest factor; rites of passage (Maasai lion); Titus 2 mentoring; Sax: "use your strength in the service of others".
- 1:06:22–1:08:06 MH 396.2 ("God's word does not repress activity, but guides it aright"). Prayer.

**Bible texts**

- 0:05:39 Eph 5:23 (inside AH 215.1).
- 0:09:43 Matt 21:28 "Son, go work today in my vineyard" (inside CME 38.4).
- 0:10:17 Caleb (Num 14:24, inside TM 407.2).
- 0:13:07 "male and female" (alluded).
- 1:04:40 Titus 2 (older and younger men; alluded, no verse).
- Few direct Bible readings in this talk.

**EGW quotes**

- VERIFIED:
  - 0:05:39 AH 215.1 (**context:** the paragraph is headed "Exercise authority with humility" and warns husbands against dwelling on headship);
  - 0:06:47 1T 547.1;
  - 0:07:54 AH 212.1 (paraphrase);
  - 0:09:10 CME 38.1–38.4 (Ms 134, 1898; temperance appeal);
  - 0:10:17 GW 290.2 and TM 407.2 (both about gospel workers);
  - 0:10:52 Ed 57.3;
  - 0:11:25 CD 407.3;
  - 0:37:03 RH June 17, 1880 par. 5;
  - 0:38:43 AH 90.2 (= 3T 336.2; "labor", not "work"; **context:** written to a young woman about readiness for marriage);
  - 1:06:22 MH 396.2.
- **NOT FOUND:** 0:21:09 "the mother helps soften her boys, the father balances the girls, sisters balance brothers… the wife softens the husband". Nearest: AH 35.5 (= 3T 80–81, "What an influence a sister may have over brothers!").

**Other sources as spoken**

- Dr. Leonard Sax (named, no book): the 18-month vocabulary story; the "too much too soon / dumb group" quotation; "use your strength in the service of others". Probably _Boys Adrift_ (2007) and _Why Gender Matters_ (2005). Needs pages.
- Toy-preference studies across 12 cultures (Alexander & Hines 2002 vervets; Hassett et al. 2008 rhesus). Children's drawings (Iijima et al. 2001). Still-face sex differences (Weinberg et al. 1999). Allan Schore ("All Our Sons", 2017). Fathers' attention to emotions (Chaplin, Cole & Zahn-Waxler 2005). Relative-age ADHD (Morrow et al. 2012 / Elder 2010). He names none of these; they are identifiable, so check before use.
- An unnamed "doctor" on teen chores. The 28% chores figure (Braun Research 2014 survey). An unnamed "statement I read" (1943/2023).

**Strongest arguments**

- MH 396.2 closing: God's word guides boys' drive; it does not repress it. "The indomitable will… untiring perseverance" are to be directed, not discouraged. Fits no. 66.
- RH June 17, 1880: "nothing so good to keep boys from being ruined… as plenty of work". Real work, not invented chores: "create a lifestyle in which your children are needed"; depend on the garden.
- AH 90.2: "The habit of enjoying useful labor, once formed, will never be lost." And "Your son is not going to wake up on his 12th birthday and decide he wants to scrub the toilet."
- Connection on the child's terms: the mother who joins her son changing the oil (1:01:13–1:02:52). Ties to Deut 6:7 "when thou walkest by the way".
- "My anger is noticed; my sadness is not" (0:58:24): what boys learn from where parents put their attention.
- 1T 547.1: the father as "house-band", with energy, integrity, honesty and practical usefulness.

**History / statistics claims** (all need a primary source; several look wrong)

- WWI: Britain's volunteers, underage enlistment, "nearly 2 million in a couple of years". Roughly right (about 2.4 million volunteered by the end of 1915).
- Religiosity: 12% more women attend and believe; a third more women pray daily; 30% / 28% / 50% figures. Pew-type data, unnamed.
- Hearing "3× louder" in females and the two-visual-systems account: Sax's claims, disputed in the literature. Smell: "double", corrected on the spot to "a third more".
- Vocabulary 40 vs 90 at 18 months, "average 65".
- Boys: 70% of lowest grades, 90% of discipline problems, 3/4 of learning-disabled students, 3× ADHD, 9× hyperactivity, 70% of suspensions, 80% of dropouts, "millions on Ritalin". Popular-book figures, unsourced.
- **Brain maturity 22 (women) vs 30 (men): looks wrong.** The common estimate is mid-20s with a small sex gap.
- **"Male hormone levels dropped 50% in 50 years": looks overstated.** Travison et al. 2007 found about 1%/year at population level.
- ADHD medications "damage the motivational center" (nucleus accumbens) and "create an addiction": contested medical claim.
- Plastics act like female hormones: early puberty in girls, later maturity and lower testosterone in boys. Partly supported for some compounds; needs sources.

**Cautions for the series**

- **Culture-war register:** "boys will be girls", "feminizing our boys", the 1943/2023 transgender joke (0:03:55), and "Satan… wants to lower those hormone levels". The series speaks to every family; leave all of it out.
- **Medical claims about ADHD medication** are given as fact to parents. Never speak them; the series should not give medication advice.
- **Gender-science claims** are leads at best. Several are Sax's contested ones (hearing, vision). The drawing and toy studies are real but should not carry doctrine.
- **AH 215.1 used without its frame.** The paragraph's point is humility in headship ("Christ's authority is exercised in wisdom, in all kindness and gentleness"). Any use must keep it.
- **Ed 57.3 read as "males".** In 1903 usage "men" is generic. Don't narrow it on camera.
- "As a church we need to…" and the mentoring call are mild, but church-addressed.
- Stories:
  - WWI volunteers (true history);
  - the Mexico drawing and the friend's children (personal, true);
  - Sax's patient (second-hand from Sax);
  - the oil-change mother (second-hand, true by his account);
  - the Maasai lion hunt (a real but now-discouraged custom; he flags it as extreme).
- No fiction.

**Series mapping**

- no. 74 (Greatest want of the world): Ed 57.3. Also GW 290.2 "Men of stamina are wanted".
- no. 67 (Caleb's other spirit): TM 407.2 "men like Caleb, who will do and dare".
- no. 66 (Direct a strong will): MH 396.2, whole paragraph.
- no. 36 (The sluggard's field) and no. 37 (Chores are curriculum): RH June 17, 1880 par. 5; AH 90.2; "a lifestyle in which your children are needed"; the 28% figure (lead only).
- no. 38 (Pay for chores?): the quoted doctor says "unpaid… no need for allowances" (not EGW).
- no. 35 (Why kids can't sit still) and no. 10 (Start age): boys need later starts and active learning. Leads only.
- no. 9 / no. 13 (fathers' part): 1T 547.1; AH 212.1 (morning and evening worship, confessing the family's sins); AH 215.1 with its humility frame.
- **NEW candidate: "Boys are not broken girls".** Promise: why do so many boys struggle at school, and what do they need instead? KJV: Prov 20:29 ("The glory of young men is their strength"); Ps 144:12; Luke 2:52. EGW: MH 396.2; RH June 17, 1880; AH 90.2. Science only as unnamed, sourced leads.
- **NEW candidate: "Connect on their ground".** Promise: the way into a child's heart may be through his work, not a talk. KJV: Deut 6:7; Mal 4:6; Col 3:21. EGW: Ed 187.1 (example and spirit); AH 90.2 (doing useful work with them). The oil-change story is second-hand, so retell it as an illustration.
- **NEW candidate: "Older men, younger men".** Promise: who teaches a boy to be a man? KJV: Titus 2:2, 6–7; 2 Kgs 3:11. EGW: Ev 109.1; Ev 684.1; MH 396.2.

### A4 · Following God's Curriculum (riVziw3ah4M) · 1:02:38 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/04-gods-curriculum.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- God's people are "peculiar", so their education must differ from the world's. State curricula plus a Bible class is "false education plus Bible".
- God's curriculum is constant from Eden through the Fall to the patriarchs:
  - classroom: garden and field;
  - activity: useful work;
  - subject: the words and works of God;
  - unit: the family;
  - teacher: God, then the parents as His representatives (Ed 20–33).
- The Fall was a failure of education: wanting "more" than God gave, and wanting to know evil. Today this means a family lifestyle, country living, real work, agriculture, nature, Scripture, and fewer subjects.

**Outline**

- 0:01:11–0:06:57 Purpose: prepare for heaven. Peculiar people: Deut 14:2; 1 Pet 2:9; Deut 28:13; CT 89.1. Homeschoolers who buy the state curriculum. The "chocolate coating on a pagan pill" quip.
- 0:06:57–0:09:43 The Eden model (Ed 20.1; 33.1; 21.2). A table: classroom, activities, subject, unit, teacher.
- 0:09:43–0:15:23 The Fall as an education failure:
  - Satan offered "more" (Ed 24.1); FE 414.2 ("science through man's interpretation… a false education"); Ed 25.3 (the mind confused by mixing);
  - "children need exposure to evil" is Satan's original lie (Ed 23.2; Isa 7:15);
  - the bank-teller counterfeit illustration; a mother who wants her children to "experiment with evil".
- 0:15:23–0:18:44 After the Fall: the curse (Ed 26.1–26.2), nature still the lesson book. Christ the great teacher, parents His representatives (Ed 33.2).
- 0:18:44–0:21:31 The patriarchs (Ed 33.3): fields and hills, labor, study and meditation. Home = school.
- 0:21:31–0:25:30 Today: the family as the unit, the parents as teachers. "Grading worksheets at the kitchen table is not true education." Deut 6:6–7: first the parent's heart (Ed 187.1), then all-day teaching. "True education is a lifestyle involving the entire family."
- 0:25:30–0:28:55 Where to live:
  - Cain's city as rebellion (Gen 4);
  - the patriarchs in the country;
  - "95% of education comes from experience";
  - CL 12.5 (Ms 76, 1905); CL 9.4 ("at the peril of their soul's salvation").
- 0:28:55–0:32:50 Useful work (LS 87.1; plenty of work, dup); agriculture (Eden schoolroom, dup; Ed 219.1–219.2; CT 534.1, dup); his own childhood garden plot.
- 0:32:50–0:40:13 Nature, not books about nature (Ed 100.2–100.3, 101.1; Prov 6:6; Job 12:7–8). **His forest-and-bird testimony** (0:35:40–0:37:25). Israel's three sources (FE 95.5), extended to "the Spirit of Prophecy".
- 0:40:48–0:43:39 Jesus' conditions (MH 365.3, "12 subjects on page 365"). Ed 77.2: useful work, Scripture, nature and the experiences of life. A plug for his mother's book.
- 0:43:39–0:56:34 Twenty subjects "from the Spirit of Prophecy":
  - eternal realities (CT 56.3); character (CT 61.2); "the great truths of our Seventh-day Adventist message";
  - health (CT 84.1); agriculture; trades (FE 425.1; CT 307.1); physical development; missionary training;
  - Bible; nature; voice culture and music; language (for mission and translation); money; home duties;
  - spelling with writing; math made practical; history with prophecy (Wylie, _Great Controversy_; the soap-opera anecdote about his mom); geography for mission; true science.
- 0:56:34–1:02:38 Cut subjects: the 1904 Taylor interview (6MR 369.2–369.3, 372.3–372.4) and 6T 130.1. "Our ability to understand God's will is linked to our willingness to follow it"; put your foot in the Jordan (Josh 3).

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:06:23 The "chocolate coating" line (unattributed; reads as a quotation).
- 0:27:13 "95% of their education is going to come from the experiences of life". Needs a primary source.

**Strongest arguments**

- **Eden → Fall → patriarchs, one table** (0:07:29–0:21:31). The same five slots (classroom, activity, subject, unit, teacher) survive the Fall; only the teacher changes, and parents become God's representatives (Ed 33.2). This is the clearest frame for no. 7 / no. 79.
- **"Do kids need to see evil to resist it?"** That was the serpent's pitch (Ed 23.2: the knowledge of evil "was in love withheld"). Isa 7:15: "that he may know to refuse the evil, and choose the good". Pair it with Rom 16:19 (not used by him): "wise unto that which is good, and simple concerning evil".
- **Satan's offer was "more"** (Ed 24.1). "God's plan isn't enough" is the same temptation, and mingling true and false "confused" the mind (Ed 25.3).
- **"False education + Bible ≠ true education"**; moving the desk home only "changed the location" (0:16:57, 0:24:55).
- **MH 365.3, the conditions the Father chose for His Son:** secluded home, honest labor, simplicity, hardship, economy, service, study at His mother's side, nature, communion. A ready-made episode list.
- **The forest-and-bird testimony** (0:36:17–0:37:25): a true, personal and gentle way to say nature teaches the heart, not just the head.
- **Fewer subjects:** 6T 130.1, "so much time lost for eternity".

**History / statistics claims**

- 0:26:06 Cain built the city "so God doesn't have to protect me". Gen 4:17 gives no motive; this is his gloss (PP was not checked for it).
- 0:27:13 "95%". Needs a primary source.
- 0:13:41 Isa 7:15 read as "not a diet" but as upbringing. An interpretation; it holds in context of good-vs-evil choosing.
- 0:14:14 Bankers study only real bills. A popular illustration; treat as illustration, not fact.
- 0:46:29 A show of hands on state-of-the-dead sermons. Audience poll.
- 0:41:21 "12 subjects on page 365 = 12 months, 365 days" is offered as a joke.

**Cautions for the series**

- **Denominational content:**
  - "the great truths of our Seventh-day Adventist message", the state of the dead, "a prophet for our time… the Spirit of Prophecy" (FE 95.5 extended);
  - Wylie and _Great Controversy_, "testimonies about the American Civil War";
  - Elder Taylor and the church school (Ms 7, 1904), with Battle Creek in its context;
  - CT 89.1's "one college in the land";
  - the Madison school farm (CT 534.1).
  - Use the principles only.
- **Cities:** CL 9.4 says "those who remain in them unnecessarily do so at the peril of their soul's salvation". The series should keep "unnecessarily" and not condemn city families. He himself adds "The Lord understands… don't give up". Fits Ritsema-review §4.2.
- **Homeschool-only frame:** "most of you are homeschooling", "state curriculum". Most of it still transfers to every family (the family unit, the parent's heart first, real work, nature).
- **The Taylor interview** is a stenographic report of a conversation about a church school. "If they prefer to send their children to the public school, let them send them" is permissive in tone. It supports the series' restraint (Ritsema-review §4.2), not a condemnation.
- Exposure to evil: the mother who wants children to "experiment with evil". Keep this as a principle without mocking parents.
- Stories:
  - the forest and the birds (personal testimony, true);
  - his garden plot (personal);
  - his mom and Wylie (personal);
  - bankers and counterfeits (stock illustration).
- No fiction.
- **Ritsema overlap:** none on Daniel 1. His public-school stance is milder than Ritsema's. Age-grading is not raised here.

**Series mapping**

- no. 7 (The first school had no walls): Ed 20.1 (the fourfold model), Ed 21.2, Ed 33.1, plus the five-slot table carried through Ed 26.2 and Ed 33.3.
- no. 79 (Was school God's first plan?): Ed 33.2 ("The family was the school, and the parents were the teachers"); Ed 33.3 (patriarchs).
- no. 8 (Why God gave Adam a job): Ed 21.2 ("useful occupation was appointed them as a blessing"); LS 87.1 ("Upon this their happiness depends").
- no. 4 (Not just school plus a Bible class): "false education plus Bible" (the chocolate-coating line is unsourced, so don't quote it); CT 61.2.
- no. 6 (Knowledge that puffs up) and no. 5: Ed 24.1; Ed 25.3; FE 414.2.
- no. 12 (Sit, walk, lie down): Ed 187.1 ("only the weight of influence given it by our own example and spirit").
- no. 11 / no. 40 (Nature): Ed 100.2–100.3; Prov 6:6; Job 12:7–8; the bird testimony.
- no. 17 (Did Jesus go to school?): MH 365.3; Ed 77.2.
- no. 31 / 81 / 82: FE 425.1; CT 307.1; money and home duties as subjects.
- no. 70 (Too much, too soon): 6T 130.1; 6MR 372.4 ("Just cut off some of those studies").
- no. 76 / no. 1: CT 56.3 ("the superiority of the future life… impressed upon the mind of every learner").
- **NEW candidate: "Do kids need to see evil to resist it?"** Promise: the oldest argument in education, and why God withheld the knowledge of evil in love. KJV: Gen 2:17; Isa 7:15; Rom 16:19; Phil 4:8. EGW: Ed 23.2; Ed 24.1; Ed 25.3.
- **NEW candidate: "Less is more: cutting subjects".** Promise: what if the best curriculum has fewer subjects? KJV: Eccl 12:12; Luke 10:41–42. EGW: 6T 130.1; MH 449.5 (dup A1); 6MR 372.4 (paraphrase only; denominational setting).
- **NEW candidate: "Where you live is part of the lesson".** Promise: does the place a family lives teach its children? KJV: Gen 13:12; Gen 4:17; Ed 33.3's "fields and hills". EGW: Ed 33.3; CL 9.4 ("unnecessarily"). Handle gently.

### A5 · Together We Build: Keeping the Next Generation (IoD_6QBqk5I) · 1:11:46 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/05-together-we-build.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- A generation is kept the way Noah kept his sons: by involving them in the work and spending time with them, not by information alone.
- Parents must be educated first. God's answer to unready parents at the Exodus was to train the parents (the wilderness, Deuteronomy), not to replace them.
- True education is "connection and relationship to win the heart". Spending time with a child _is_ education, good or bad.

**Outline**

- 0:01:45 "80% are leaving"; Judg 2:7–10, "another generation … which knew not the LORD".
- 0:02:52 Noah kept his children by building the ark with them; Methuselah's line helped (1SP 65.2); "invested everything" (PP 95.1), plus an imagined last-nails scene.
- 0:06:18 Review: Eden school (Ed 20.1), family school (Ed 33.1), parents as Christ's representatives (Ed 33.2), patriarchs in the country (Ed 33.3).
- 0:09:07 Ed 34.1: few Israelites were ready to train their children; "the parents themselves needed instruction". "Give me the curriculum" is the wrong mindset: the parent needs the education first.
- 0:13:41 God's response: forty years of training parents. The school of the prophets came ~400 years later "to help the family, not replace" it.
- 0:15:19 Deuteronomy as a book of education on the border of the promised land; Deut 6:6–7 unpacked ("in thine heart" = lived; "diligently" = intentional; sit/walk/lie/rise = all the time, through relationship). Ed 41.1, Ed 187.1.
- 0:19:28 Daily Bible study as the parent's "key to success"; reading list (Ed "five times", AH, CT, FE, 6T …).
- 0:27:24 Ed 250.2: associated in work, study, worship, recreation; parents as "teachers and companions". Dishes with a toddler; study together (CT 436.1, misattributed to COL); worship together, against age-split classes (his book _Gather the Children_); play.
- 0:34:13 Parents "stand in the place of God" (PP 308.2), so they represent His character. God's character includes wanting to be with us; Jesus "made us His career".
- 0:40:30 Jesus' school: Ed 84.3 (the family of Jesus), Ed 86.2 (four disciples incl. Judas), Ed 80.1, DA 359.2 ("told Him all things").
- 0:45:00 Relationship science: attachment, conscience, resisting temptation (no named study).
- 0:47:49 Judg 2:10 again: they lacked the _works_ (not taught; PP 592.2) and _the LORD_ ("know" = relationship). Israel had information; it lacked the Deuteronomy manner.
- 0:53:27 Counseling pattern: well-instructed youth "rebel" because they never experienced connection; FE 156.1 (young hearts yearn for sympathy). "Teenage rebellion … is a plea for relationship."
- 0:57:31 Practical: spend time; toddlers at the dishes; "me time" = before they wake (Mark 1:35 example); DA 250.2 ("only life can beget life").
- 1:03:15 Closing story: two mothers at a picnic (the one who went down to the river with her boys); "not luck … a return on investment"; 1 John 4:19; "never too late" (Jesus' disciples were adults).

**Bible texts**

- 0:02:20, 0:47:49 Judg 2:7–10 (quoted 2:7, 2:10).
- 0:16:27 Deut 6:6–7 (quoted, then unpacked phrase by phrase).
- 0:14:13 Exodus–Numbers, wilderness (alluded). 0:31:24 assemblies "even the babies" (alluded; cf. Joel 2:16, Deut 31:12).
- 0:43:18 "I'll never leave you or forsake you" (Heb 13:5, paraphrased); "I'm with you always" (Matt 28:20).
- 0:43:51 Mark 6:30–31 (via DA). 1:00:21 Mark 1:35 (alluded). 1:08:29 1 John 4:19 (quoted). 1:02:38 "my ear is always open" (alluded).

**EGW quotes** (all in `verification.md`)

- 0:04:01 Methuselah and sons helped build the ark — 1SP 65.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:05:44 "invested everything" — PP 95.1 — VERIFIED (core line; the bank-account scene is his own).
- 0:06:18 Eden school; family school — Ed 20.1, 33.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:06:53 "ordained that men and women should be His representatives" — Ed 33.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:08:01 "conditions most favorable for the development of character … fields and hills" — Ed 33.3 — VERIFIED.
- 0:09:07 "This was the method … The parents themselves needed instruction" — Ed 34.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:18:55 "Not as a dry theory …" — Ed 41.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:21:43 "As a preparation for teaching His precepts … weight of influence" — Ed 187.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:27:24 "associated in work and study, in worship and recreation … teachers and companions" — Ed 250.2 — VERIFIED (context: the Sabbath).
- 0:29:39 "Let us learn together" (said to be COL) — CT 436.1 — DIFFERS (wrong book; "Let us climb together").
- 0:35:51 "parents shall stand in the place of God" — PP 308.2 (= AH 293.1) — VERIFIED.
- 0:39:57 "set aside their cold reserve … a child among their children" — FE 18.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:40:30 "formed the family of Jesus …" — Ed 84.3 — VERIFIED.
- 0:41:37 the four (Peter, James, John, Judas) — Ed 86.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase; see note).
- 0:43:18 "discerned the infinite possibilities" — Ed 80.1 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:43:51 "The disciples came to Jesus and told Him all things …" — DA 359.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:49:27 "mighty works … often recounted in the homes of Israel" — PP 592.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:52:54 "binding the hearts of the children to the parents" — CT 158.3 / AH 220.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:56:16 "Young hearts yearn for sympathy and tenderness …" — FE 156.1 (= CE 169.1) — VERIFIED.
- 1:00:57 "not the highest work of education to communicate knowledge merely …" — DA 250.2 (= 1MCP 187.3) — VERIFIED (context: the disciples' daily contact with Christ).

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:01:45 "according to some statistics 80% are leaving" — needs a primary source.
- 0:45:33–0:47:16 "a scientific article" on relationship and early conscience, secure children resisting temptation, relationship moderating moral discourse, infant attachment predicting later relationships — needs a primary source (plausible leads: Kochanska's conscience studies; Laible & Thompson; attachment literature).
- 0:30:50 his own book _Gather the Children_ (age-segregated worship).
- 0:27:24 Rachel Nobella's talk that morning (event speaker; not in this playlist).

**Strongest arguments**

- **"Give me the curriculum" is backwards**: Ed 34.1 says Israel's parents "themselves needed instruction". God's fix was forty years of training parents, not a substitute institution (0:09:07–0:15:19).
- **Two things the forgetting generation lacked** (Judg 2:10): the works (not recounted; PP 592.2) and the LORD (no relationship). Israel was saturated with religious information and still lost its children (0:48:54–0:52:22).
- **Teachers _and companions_** (Ed 250.2): the companion half is the forgotten one. A companion has no ulterior motive (0:28:00–0:33:38).
- **Parents stand in God's place** (PP 308.2), so children form their first picture of God from time spent with parents. Every unhurried walk shows "a God who delighted to walk with his children" (0:34:13, 1:02:05).
- **DA 359.2**: the disciples "told Him all things" because of an intimate relationship. A parent who wants that from a teenager builds it early (0:43:51).
- **"Rebellion" as a plea for connection** with FE 156.1: a strong Q, if kept to the source's claim and not his counseling generalization (0:56:16).

**History / statistics claims**

- "80% are leaving" — needs a primary source (likely a denominational retention figure; varies widely by study).
- "School of the prophets was not for another 400 years" — roughly consistent with Exodus → Samuel; a lead.
- 0:45:00 "brain development": children cannot grasp abstract relationship at 2–6 and learn it only by experience — needs a primary source.
- 0:58:03 "We encourage our toddlers to be independent and wonder why our teenagers don't want to talk to us" — rhetoric, no source.

**Cautions for the series**

- Denominational: "spirit of prophecy" (×4), Sabbath school, "as a church", the reading list of EGW compilations, "Rachel Nobella's talk". Keep all out of speech.
- Fiction/imagination: Noah's last-nails and bank-account scene (0:04:36–0:05:44) is his imagination, framed "I imagine". If retold, frame it as such, or drop it.
- True stories (his observation): the two mothers at the picnic and the caterpillar (1:03:15–1:08:29). Usable only as his story, credited, or retold as the owner's own.
- Brain-science and attachment claims (0:45:00–0:47:16): no study named. Series rule: counsel, not brain science.
- Ed 86.2: he says Jesus entered "an even closer relationship" with four, Judas among them. The text says the four "were to act a leading part" and were "the objects of Christ's greatest solicitude and the recipients of His most frequent and careful instruction". Speak the text, not "closer relationship".
- Age-segregated worship: "not a single example … in the Bible" (0:31:24) — the assembly texts (Deut 31:12; Joel 2:16; 2 Kgs 23:2) support whole-family gatherings, but Neh 8:2 has "all that could hear with understanding", which some read as an age threshold. Don't state "not one example" as fact. Relates to no. 85.
- "You don't [get me time]. Plain and simple." (0:59:47) — may land as condemning tired parents; soften.
- Gender: "primarily upon the mother in the early years as the father's the breadwinner" (0:29:07) — series avoids gender assumptions; if used, quote a source, not his summary.

**Series mapping**

- No. 9 (first teacher): Ed 33.2 (He "ordained that men and women should be His representatives. The family was the school, and the parents were the teachers"); PP 308.2 (parents "stand in the place of God to their children" in the earlier years).
- No. 12 (sit, walk, lie down): Ed 187.1 ("God commands that they be hidden in the hearts of the parents … we ourselves must be interested in it"); Ed 41.1 ("Not as a dry theory"); his reading of "diligently" = intentional, "sit … rise up" = all day, carried by relationship rather than lecture.
- No. 14 (the generation that forgot): Judg 2:10's two halves (the works, the LORD); PP 592.2 (works "often recounted in the homes of Israel"); his line: Israel had the information but lost the manner.
- No. 79 (God's first plan): Ed 34.1 ("The parents themselves needed instruction and discipline"), so God trained parents rather than substituting a school.
- No. 37 (chores): Ed 250.2 ("associated in work"); the 18-month-old at the dishes: "change your focus from getting the dishes done to winning the heart".
- No. 54/55 (questions; "Yes, and let me help"): the caterpillar (look at your boy's find); FE 18.2 ("sometimes even being a child among children").
- No. 73 (teacher is the lesson): Ed 41.1; DA 250.2 ("It is only life that can beget life").
- No. 80 (Jesus ran a school): Ed 84.3; Ed 86.2 (four given "most frequent and careful instruction", Judas among them); DA 359.2.
- No. 85 (age-sorting): whole-family assemblies (Deut 31:12; Joel 2:16), with the Neh 8:2 caveat.
- **NEW: "Who needs the education first?"** (Q) — Promise: before the children, God schooled the parents for forty years. KJV: Deut 4:9; Deut 6:6–7; Ps 119:11. EGW: Ed 34.1; Ed 276.3 (education will not accomplish all it should "until the importance of the parents' work is fully recognized, and they receive a training"); Ed 187.1.
- **NEW: "Teacher or companion?"** (Q) — Promise: the half of parenting that wins the heart. KJV: Mark 6:30; 1 John 4:19; Mal 4:6. EGW: Ed 250.2; FE 18.2; DA 359.2; MH 388.2 ("Young children love companionship … They yearn for sympathy and tenderness").
- **NEW: "Rebellion, or a plea for connection?"** (Q) — Promise: what a teenager who "rebels" may actually be looking for. KJV: Mal 4:6; 1 John 4:19; Judg 2:10. EGW: FE 156.1; CT 158.3 ("bind the hearts of the little ones to them by silken cords of love").

### A6 · Education for the Crisis (hany-oLj6iw) · 1:02:58 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/06-education-for-the-crisis.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- A crisis is coming in which children may be separated from parents and must stand alone. Prepare them for it rather than trying only to prevent it.
- Every Bible youth who stood (Noah's sons, Isaac, Joseph, Moses, Samuel, Elisha, David, the captive maid, Daniel) had the same preparation: a home school, parents as teachers, country life and useful work, the word and works of God, and temperance.
- The parents' urgency comes from knowing time is short. Hezekiah's prophecy → Josiah's reading of the law → Daniel's parents ("Daniel chapter zero").

**Outline**

- 0:01:45 Dan 12:1 time of trouble; "answer … separately and alone" (5T 463.2). Shift: prepare, don't only prevent.
- 0:03:27 Review: Eden school (Ed 20.1), family school (Ed 33.1–33.3), parents as representatives.
- 0:06:18 Pattern survey: Noah (PP 98.1), Isaac (Gen 18:19; PP 152.2), Joseph (Ed ch.), Moses (PP 243.4–244.1, "about twelve years old"), Samuel (CG 197.1), Elisha (Ed 58.2), David, the captive maid (PK 245.1).
- 0:14:11 Daniel: "dare to be a Daniel"; parallels with the last generation (prophetic time, remnant, health, worship test, no guile).
- 0:15:18 FE 95.3–96.1 read whole: home and school one; figures and symbols "almost from infancy"; Moses, Samuel, David, Daniel "before the scenes of the captivity".
- 0:20:20 Three elements: "words of revelation" (Bible), "scenes of nature", "voices of the prophets" (his gloss: the spirit of prophecy). A prophet's role is to apply the word and prepare for the crisis.
- 0:23:44 PK 428.1; YI May 21, 1903 (pars. 5, 9, 6–7); Ed 262.2: Daniel's home training made him strong.
- 0:26:30 Temperance as "the means of their preservation": 1MCP 333.2; MYP 242.2; CT 158.3. Daniel accepted the name and the university, drew the line at diet. "Not a tofu pork roast."
- 0:30:27 Time is short: "Daniel chapter zero" in 2 Kgs 20 (Hezekiah's sign, Babylon's envoys, the prophecy of sons as eunuchs). Haskell, SDP 16.1–16.3: Hephzibah or Mary?
- 0:37:17 Manasseh's 55 years: urgency fades. Josiah: three steps of reform — the word (Deuteronomy; PK 396.2), the prophet (Huldah), educating families (2 Kgs 23:1–2, "both small and great").
- 0:47:07 Chronology: law read ~621 BC; Daniel taken 605 at 15–16 (12LtMs Ms 122, 1897) and in service at 18 (4T 570.1), so born ~620, a year after the reading. His parents heard Deuteronomy.
- 0:51:40 Imagined appeal by Josiah ("I imagine … 'Wait, wait'").
- 0:53:21 Haskell SDP 19.3–19.4 (unattributed): Daniel's godly mother taught the scrolls; "holy mothers … by word and song".
- 0:54:28 Recap: none of them knew how long they had. A present-day Romanian family whose son, 8, was interrogated by social services.
- 0:56:44 Haskell SDP 20.2: "Now can be seen the results of home training … children of the home school." Daniel interprets where Babylon's professors could not; Ps 119:99–100.
- 1:00:16 Haskell SDP 49.1 (unattributed): "From what homes will come the Daniels …?" Close: CG 64.3 ("Who is sufficient for these things?").

**Bible texts**

- 0:02:18 Dan 12:1 (paraphrased). 0:07:58 Gen 18:19 (quoted). 0:14:44 Rev 14:5 (alluded, "no guile"). 0:16:58 Phil 4:8 (alluded).
- 0:31:38–0:36:10 2 Kgs 20:1–3, 6, 11, 13–18 (narrated; 20:14–18 read). Babylon's interest: 2 Chr 32:31 fits ("to enquire of the wonder").
- 0:37:53 2 Kgs 21 (Manasseh, Amon). 0:40:10 2 Kgs 22:8–20 (narrated). 0:45:52 2 Kgs 23:1–2 (read). 0:46:26 Joel 2:16 (alluded).
- 0:49:22 Dan 1:5 (three years). 0:58:26 1 Cor 1:20 (quoted). 0:59:40 Ps 119:99–100 (quoted). 1:00:16 Heb 11:25 (in Haskell).
- 0:12:29 1 Kgs 19:18–19 (Elisha, the 7,000; alluded). 0:13:04 2 Kgs 5:2–3 (alluded).

**EGW quotes**

- 0:02:18 "answer separately and alone" — 5T 463.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:04:01 review — Ed 20.1, 33.1–33.3 — dup (A5).
- 0:07:26 Noah's family saved as a reward for faithfulness — PP 98.1 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:08:32 Isaac "a sharer in the faith of his father" — PP 152.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:10:11 "It was with deep gratitude that Jochebed …"; "about twelve" — PP 243.4–244.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:11:19 "During the first three years of the life of Samuel …" — CG 197.1 (= RH Sept 8, 1904) — VERIFIED.
- 0:12:29 Elisha's family among the 7,000 — Ed 58.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:13:04 "strong witness to the power of early home training" — PK 245.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:15:18 "God commanded the Hebrews … the home and the school were one …" through "… before the scenes of captivity" — FE 95.3–96.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:23:44 "instruction … in the homes of their parents made them strong in faith" — PK 428.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:24:17 "not after the order of the worldly schools" — YI May 21, 1903 par. 5 — VERIFIED.

- 0:28:46 "clear minds to discern Satan's devices" — 1MCP 333.2 (= MYP 236.3) — VERIFIED.
- 0:28:46 "Daniel's clearness of mind … plainness of his diet" — MYP 242.2 (= YI July 9, 1903) — DIFFERS (wording).
- 0:29:18 "a sacred duty … content with plain and simple food" — CT 158.3 (= CG 141.2) — VERIFIED.
- 0:41:18 "he trembled for the future" — PK 396.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:48:48 "Daniel was but eighteen years old …" — 4T 570.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:48:48 "about fifteen or sixteen years old" — 12LtMs, Ms 122, 1897 par. 25 (= CTr 173.2) — VERIFIED.
- 1:00:51 "Who is sufficient for these things?" — CG 64.3 (= 4T 198.2) — VERIFIED.

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:36:45 Haskell, _The Story of Daniel the Prophet_ (named): SDP 16.1 + 16.3 (Manasseh; "Hephzibah or Mary"). Verified, pioneer.
- 0:53:21 Same book, **unnamed** at this point: SDP 19.3 + end of 19.4 (godly mother, parchment scrolls, "holy mothers … by word and song"). Spliced; verified, pioneer.
- 0:56:44 Haskell (named): SDP 20.2. Verified; "fulfilled his purpose" for "promise".
- 1:00:16 Haskell, **unnamed**: SDP 49.1 ("From what homes will come the Daniels and the Meshachs?"). A listener would take it for EGW.
- 0:55:35 "the Kjukaru [caption] family testimony" earlier in the week: Romanian parents Sebastian and Andrea; their 8-year-old interrogated by social services — needs a primary source; a private family.

**Strongest arguments**

- **The pattern of the youth who stood**: home teachers, country, useful work, word and works, temperance — Noah's sons to Daniel (0:06:18–0:29:54). A strong Episode spine using Bible texts plus PK 245.1, PP 243.4–244.1, CG 197.1, PK 428.1.
- **"If you knew you only had twelve years …"** Jochebed ("about twelve", PP 244.1), Hannah (~three years), Daniel's parents (~fifteen). None knew; each made the years count (0:10:45, 0:11:19, 0:54:28).
- **The captive maid** (2 Kgs 5:2–3; PK 245.1): one sentence in Naaman's house is "a strong witness to the power of early home training". A clean Question.
- **Josiah's reform in three steps**: return to the book, inquire of the LORD, then gather "all the people, both small and great" (2 Kgs 22–23). Educating families is the third step (0:40:10–0:46:26).
- **Daniel accepted a new name and a foreign syllabus but not defilement** (0:27:36–0:28:46): fits the series' Daniel 1 reading (no. 19); see Cautions on diet.
- **Ps 119:99** as the payoff: "I have more understanding than all my teachers: for thy testimonies are my meditation" (0:59:40).

**History / statistics claims**

- Dates (0:47:07–0:50:01): Hezekiah healed 702 BC; Manasseh 686; Amon 642; Josiah 640; temple restoration ~621; Daniel captive 605; Daniel born ~620 — his calculation, standard-chronology leads. Series rule: no dates for Daniel's captivity in speech. Note the source he leans on (Haskell, SDP 19.3) dates the first siege 607 BC and calls Daniel "not over eighteen" then, which conflicts with his 605/15 reconstruction.
- "Babylonians worshiped the sun", so the sign drew them (0:33:18) — partly a lead (Shamash); Scripture says only that they came "to enquire of the wonder" (2 Chr 32:31).
- Manasseh "longest reigning king in Judah", 55 years (2 Kgs 21:1) — correct per the text.
- "Babylon was the educational center of the world … the University of Babylon" (0:58:26) — rhetoric; "university" is anachronistic.

**Cautions for the series**

- Denominational/prophecy: Daniel's parallels with "the last generation" (remnant, "health message", worship test, 144,000) (0:14:44); "voices of the prophets" = "the spirit of prophecy" and "do we have a prophet?" (0:20:54–0:23:10); image-of-the-beast decree (Haskell, 1:00:16). All out of speech.
- Prophecy applied to schooling and fear: "What if my children are taken from me?" and the social-services testimony (0:02:53, 0:56:07). Don't build a short on state-removal fear.
- Health: "God's diet is not a healthy replica of the world … no tofu pork roast"; "content with plain food" with the broccoli illustration (0:28:46–0:30:27). The series keeps Daniel's diet to Scripture's account, no health claims (no. 20).
- Imagined scenes: Josiah's appeal speech (0:51:40, "I imagine"); Babylonian envoys knocking on the door (0:33:18). Fine as framed imagination, not as narrative fact.
- Haskell is read twice without attribution (0:53:21, 1:00:16); the "homeschool" wording is Haskell's ("home school"), not EGW's. Credit Haskell in any description; don't attribute to EGW.
- "Children of the homeschool … homeschooled in an education based on God's word" (0:57:51–0:58:26): sets Daniel up as a homeschool model. Scripture (Dan 1:4–5, 17) also has him inside Babylon's three-year program. This matches the series' Daniel 1 reading (`ritsema-review.md` §4.1): settled at home, then tested inside the school.
- Moses "grew up in the country" (0:09:37; "lowly cabin home in Goshen", FE 96.1) — sourced, but only to age ~12.

**Series mapping**

- No. 15 (Hannah's three years): CG 197.1 ("During the first three years of the life of Samuel … By every familiar object surrounding him she sought to lead his thoughts up to the Creator").
- No. 16 (held in trust): PP 243.4 ("All this rendered her more diligent and careful in his instruction"); PP 244.1 ("about twelve years old"; "The lessons learned at his mother's side could not be forgotten").
- No. 18 (Manasseh or Jesus): SDP 16.1–16.3 is the source the series' Daniel booklet already uses; add 2 Kgs 20:17–18 as the prophecy the parents ignored.
- No. 19 (Daniel's education): PK 428.1; YI May 21, 1903 par. 5 ("not after the order of the worldly schools"), par. 9; FE 96.1; Ed 262.2; 4T 570.1 (eighteen when brought into the court; "ages" safe, dates not).
- No. 20 (ten-day test): YI May 21, 1903 pars. 6–7 (parents trained him "to habits of strict temperance … such an early education was … the means of their preservation"); PK 482.2. Keep it textual.
- No. 22 (Joseph), 25 (Elisha): Ed 58.2 ("his father's household were among the number who had not bowed the knee to Baal").
- No. 14 (generation that forgot): Manasseh's 55 years as how urgency fades between generations.
- No. 13 ("When thy son asketh"): FE 95.3–95.4 (works "recounted with eloquence and reverential awe"; "by the use of figures and symbols … almost from infancy").
- **NEW: "The girl in Naaman's house"** (Q) — Promise: one sentence from a captive child shows what her home had given her. KJV: 2 Kgs 5:2–3. EGW: PK 245.1 ("a strong witness to the power of early home training"; "There is no higher trust than that committed to fathers and mothers").
- **NEW: "Daniel's parents heard the law read"** (E) — Promise: a king's warning, a found book, and the parents of four boys who would stand in Babylon. KJV: 2 Kgs 20:17–18; 22:8, 11, 19–20; 23:1–2; Dan 1:6. EGW: PK 396.2; PK 428.1; FE 96.1. Note: the year link (law read ~621, Daniel born ~620) is his inference and needs care; the claim "his parents were there" is speculation (all the people were summoned).
- **NEW: "Prepare them to stand, not only to be sheltered"** (Q) — Promise: you may not be there when your child has to stand. KJV: Dan 1:8; 3:17–18; Ps 119:99. EGW: 5T 463.2 (many "called to speak before councils … perhaps separately and alone"); CG 64.3. Keep the frame on character, not on state persecution.
