### A7 · Distracted Builders: The Attack of Media (VKR975XNrCA) · 1:14:41 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/07-distracted-builders.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- The ark is not being built because the builders are distracted. Screens harm us "regardless of what they are being used for", even for Bible reading.
- Satan's aim is to put media in the place of God: in the reward of study, in spare moments, in the first thought of the morning, and in relationships.
- Escape by replacement, not by bare restriction: something better, and above all time together.

**Outline**

- 0:01:05 AI-made image of distracted ark builders; workplace distraction statistics (720 hours/yr; 250 phone checks/day; 23 minutes to refocus; clinicians texting in surgery).
- 0:05:03 1 Pet 5:8 (roaring lion; his friend's lion photo in Tanzania); 2 Cor 2:11 "ignorant of his devices".
- 0:07:26 1. Unnatural dopamine: natural vs artificial; Kardaras (_Glow Kids_): screen addicts harder to treat than heroin addicts; video-game designers and blood pressure; notifications and novelty; receptor down-regulation; "level 452" on a flight. Spiritual dopamine: meditation +65%; GW 251.2 ("will most assuredly be rewarded"). Chores feel boring after easy dopamine.
- 0:19:10 Practical: tool, not toy; "no place for media as entertainment"; notifications off; a plan for tired evenings.
- 0:20:52 2. Eye damage: near work, blue light, retina; an Indonesian surgeon's account; circadian rhythm; no screens after lunch or 5 hours before bed; print it; screen work in the mornings; a daily cut-off.
- 0:27:05 3. Attention: noise, color, movement; scene cuts force attention; Christakis ("reality can just become boring"); Mr. Rogers in real time; Dunckley ("non-screen activities as boring … a red flag").
- 0:32:47 4. Multitasking. 5. Digital dementia: South Korea 15%; UCLA 14% of 18–39; phone numbers and GPS.
- 0:36:44 Concordance-and-notebook study vs Bible-app study; paper-vs-screen research list; digital texts can vanish.
- 0:43:38 6. Anterior cingulate shrinkage with media multitasking ("center of the free will"); SC 90.1.
- 0:46:24 7. Light reading: 1SAT 352.3; ST June 13, 1906 with "YouTube shorts" substituted; 4T 498.2–498.3; CT 337.1; CT 281.2.
- 0:50:19 8. Spare moments: Ed 191.4 (keep the Bible always with you); a printed Bible as witness on a plane; time math (18-year-old to 90: 312 of 334 free months on media); €1,200/month in time at €20/hour.
- 0:59:40 First thought: Lt 19, 1895; phones "sleep" in a central place; airplane mode until after time with God.
- 1:01:27 Relationship replacement: phones release relationship chemicals; children get phones at the age they most need parents.
- 1:03:07 Nadab and Abihu: mingling sacred and common in our morning time and in church.
- 1:05:26 Six escapes: accountability partner; a 28-day media fast; something better; a schedule without idle time; fresh air, exercise, plain diet (spices "raise the animal passions"); the Lord's help. Parents' own example.
- 1:08:49 Closing stories: the 12-year-old at his fourth media seminar ("my dad … is too busy to go with me"); the student who swapped her laptop for a notebook.

**Bible texts**

- 0:05:03 1 Pet 5:8 (quoted). 0:06:50 2 Cor 2:11 (paraphrased). 0:13:29 Gen 1 "it was good" (alluded). 1:03:07 Lev 10:1–2 (narrated, "strange fire"). 1:01:27 "from the mouths of babes" (Ps 8:2, alluded in passing).

**EGW quotes**

- 0:17:23 "The diligent, humble student … will most assuredly be rewarded" — GW 251.2 — VERIFIED (context: ministers' study).
- 0:45:51 "If God's word were studied as it should be … breadth of mind …" — SC 90.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:47:34 "We have no time to spend in light reading …" — 1SAT 352.3 (Ms 27, 1906) — VERIFIED (context: magazines).
- 0:48:40 "Question your own experience as to the influence of light reading …" — ST June 13, 1906 par. 3 (cf. MYP 273.1, "exciting stories") — VERIFIED; he openly swaps in "YouTube shorts".
- 0:49:12 "There is a work before you to dispose of your light reading … Light reading fascinates the mind …" — 4T 498.2 + 498.3 — DIFFERS (splice; addressed to one woman, framed as "Parents are told").
- 0:49:12 "It is Satan's policy to fill the mind with a desire for worldly amusement …" — CT 337.1 (= AH 521.1) — VERIFIED (compressed).
- 0:49:47 "Amusements are doing more to counteract the working of the Holy Spirit …" — CT 281.2 — VERIFIED (context: teachers' exercise).
- 0:50:19 "Let the student keep his Bible always with him …" — Ed 191.4 — VERIFIED.
- 0:59:40 "Your last thought at night, your first thought in the morning …" — Lt 19, 1895 (OHC 116.2 = 2MCP 666.4) — VERIFIED.
- 1:07:41 spices and "animal passions" — bare allusion (cf. CD 341.2); not tabled.

**Other sources as spoken**

- Nicholas Kardaras, _Glow Kids_ (2016; named) — heroin vs screen quote; game-design and blood-pressure claim — checkable book; quote needs page.
- Dimitri Christakis, Seattle Children's (named) — "reality can just become boring" — needs a primary source (a quote, likely from an interview).
- Victoria Dunckley (named; "children's media use") — "non-screen activities as boring … red flag" — likely _Reset Your Child's Brain_ (2015); needs page.
- TED talk "The hidden cost of social media" on YouTube (named) — life-in-months chart — needs a primary source.
- Unnamed studies: meditation +65% dopamine; paper vs screen (retention, comprehension, notes, prefrontal vs parietal activation); media multitasking and anterior cingulate gray matter; South Korea and UCLA "digital dementia"; spicy diets and pornography — all need primary sources.

**Strongest arguments**

- **Spare moments** (Ed 191.4): the Bible "always with him … waiting at a railway station" set against the phone that fills every spare moment. The printed Bible on a plane as an unspoken witness (0:50:19–0:53:12). Clean Q, no science needed.
- **"Can you, after such reading, open the Bible …?"** (ST June 13, 1906; MYP 273.1). He asks it of short-form video. Strong, and it stays on the text if the owner says "Ask yourself what an hour of scrolling does to the next hour of reading" without the neuroscience (0:48:40).
- **First thought** (Lt 19, 1895): phones that "sleep" in the kitchen; airplane mode until after prayer (0:59:40–1:00:47). Practical and text-anchored; pair with Ps 5:3 or Mark 1:35.
- **The boy at his fourth seminar** (1:08:49–1:11:34): taking away without replacing; "my dad … is too busy to go with me". Good second-hand story for a parenting short; it lands on relationship, not rules.
- **Concordance-and-notebook vs app** (0:36:44): a felt contrast; keep it as experience, not as brain research.

**History / statistics claims** (every one needs a primary source unless marked)

- 720 work hours/year distracted ("90 work days"); 250 phone checks/day ("once every three minutes"); 23 minutes to return to a task (Gloria Mark's work is the likely origin; a lead).
- "Half of technicians have admitted to texting during a procedure" — probably a perfusionist survey (Smith et al., 2011); needs a primary source.
- Kardaras: easier to treat heroin addicts than screen addicts — source named (book/interview); an opinion, not data.
- Game testers' blood pressure must hit "180 over 140" — needs a primary source; looks wrong.
- Meditation raises dopamine 65% — likely Kjaer et al. 2002 (yoga nidra, PET); a lead.
- Blue light damages the retina; an Indonesian surgeon says retinas look "torn apart" from screens — needs a primary source; the surgeon story looks wrong as stated (retinal tears/detachment are not established screen effects).
- "Digital dementia": found first in South Korea in ~15% of young people; UCLA 14% of 18–39 with memory complaints who "actually had changes in their brain" — the UCLA/Gallup figure was self-reported memory complaints; the brain-change claim looks wrong.
- Paper vs screen: retention, comprehension, overconfidence (leads: Delgado et al. 2018 meta-analysis; Mueller & Oppenheimer 2014 on laptop notes). "Paper activates prefrontal and cingulate; screen activates parietal (emotional)" — needs a primary source; the "parietal = emotional" gloss looks wrong.
- Media multitasking and smaller anterior cingulate gray matter — likely Loh & Kanai 2014 (correlational); "center of the free will" is his gloss.
- 312 of 334 remaining free months spent on media (TED chart) — needs a primary source; looks wrong (implies ~93% of free time).
- "28 days for the brain to heal"; phones release "the same exact chemicals" as time with a person; spicy diets linked to pornography and media addiction — needs a primary source; the spice claim looks wrong.

**Cautions for the series**

- Health and brain-science claims dominate the talk (dopamine receptors, blue light, digital dementia, ACC, spices). Series rule: no health or brain-science claims in speech (cf. no. 69: state only the credited booklet's claim, no statistics).
- "There is no place for media as entertainment in our lives" and "parents ask how much time … that's not the question" (0:19:44, 1:09:23) — absolute; frame as counsel.
- Fiction: he extends "light reading" from novels to "even good and true stories" read for entertainment (0:46:59). This is stricter than the series' allegory allowance (no. 68, §5); note it, don't adopt.
- Nadab and Abihu applied to phone use in church (1:03:44) — a strong typological move; risky as doctrine. If used, frame as a question.
- Denominational: "spirit of prophecy", "I'm using it for spirit of prophecy" (0:07:26), carrying "the entire spirit of prophecy library" (0:42:26).
- "Digital is easy to change … with the click of a button it all disappears" when "the attention of the world is going to be focused on our beliefs" (0:41:18–0:42:26) — an end-time censorship frame; leave out.
- AI-made illustration (0:01:05): if a similar image is used, label it "Illustration".
- True vs second-hand stories: the man at "level 452" (his own), the 12-year-old (a friend's story, second-hand), the laptop student (his own). Mr. Rogers' restaurant episode is from memory ("I don't remember all the details").

**Series mapping**

- No. 69 (screens and the reading child): Ed 191.4; ST June 13, 1906 ("Can you, after spending time in such reading, open the Bible, and read with interest the words of life?"); the boy who wanted a bike, a garden and a fort, if his father would come.
- No. 68 (novels): 1SAT 352.3 ("We have no time to spend in light reading"); 4T 498.3 ("Light reading fascinates the mind and makes the reading of God's word uninteresting"); MYP 273.1. Note his extension to true stories (stricter than the series).
- No. 34 (recreation or amusement?): CT 337.1 (Satan fills the mind with amusement "that they may have no time to ask themselves the question, How is it with my soul?"); CT 281.2 (amusements counteract the Holy Spirit; context: teachers who left useful work for games).
- No. 37 (chores): his observation that children used to easy rewards find chores boring; keep it as observation, with no dopamine language.
- No. 39 (the Bible as a schoolbook): SC 90.1 ("breadth of mind, a nobility of character, and a stability of purpose"); GW 251.2.
- No. 72 (evenings at home): screens off in the evening and phones sleeping outside bedrooms; Lt 19, 1895.
- No. 41/83 (readers; paper): concordance-and-notebook study — as experience only.
- **NEW: "Your spare moments"** (Q) — Promise: the minutes in line and on the bus are an education, one way or the other. KJV: Eph 5:16; Ps 119:11; Deut 6:7. EGW: Ed 191.4 ("Let the student keep his Bible always with him … waiting at a railway station").
- **NEW: "The first thought of the morning"** (Q) — Promise: who gets the first minute of your child's day, and yours? KJV: Ps 5:3; Mark 1:35. EGW: Lt 19, 1895 (OHC 116.2: "Your last thought at night, your first thought in the morning, should be of Him").
- **NEW: "Take away, then replace"** (Q) — Promise: why cutting screen time fails without a parent to fill it. KJV: Rom 12:21; Prov 22:6. EGW: FE 18.2 (enter their sports; "a child among children"); CT 337.1. Story lead: the boy at his parents' fourth media seminar (credit White's friend).

Transcript: `bogenhofen-2026/08-another-ark.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Noah's ark was God's design, not human wisdom, and it saved his family. Today's "ark" is the family as a refuge, built by God's plan and not by copying other people's programs.
- Five points: look to God, not man; live your faith; prioritize the family; expect opposition; invest everything.

**Outline**

- 0:00:43 1 Cor 1:27: strength through what the world calls weak. PP 92.3: "Human wisdom could not have devised …"
- 0:02:30 Noah's plans came from God, not from YouTube, a homeschool convention or a WhatsApp group; God uses what is in our hand.
- 0:06:00 Point 1, look to God: Ed 187.1 (dup); Ed 276.3 (parents need training); churches no longer run parenting classes; Titus 2 (older train younger). Story: the mother told to "study the book Education" who returned years later saying it saved her marriage.
- 0:11:33 Point 2, live your faith: PP 95.1 ("every blow struck upon the ark was a witness"). Obedience as the hedge: CG 233.3; 1BC 1105.4; TDG 109.2; PK 677.3 (Isa 58:12); FE 501.2. Legalism is a matter of motive.
- 0:15:25 Point 3, prioritize the family: don't call parents away to church work; AH 32.2; PP 579.1; MH 350.2. "Work in the family is ministry."
- 0:18:46 Early years: CG 103.5; Hagen castle foundations; "first seven years" research; "Give me a child until he is seven"; AH 267.1.
- 0:20:58 The home as refuge: emotional (make home happy; FE 156.1 dup), from media, and from social media as the place teenagers now take their struggles.
- 0:24:52 Refuge from schools: Russell 1952 on Fichte and on early indoctrination; Baucham ("send our children to Caesar"); "insanity"; CT 205.1; Ms 5, 1909; CG 304.3–305.1; Isa 26:20; Ms 155, 1903 + Lt 70, 1906 (Battle Creek). John the Baptist: DA 101.3.
- 0:31:02 Motherhood is ministry: PP 572.3; PK 219.1; ST Dec 11, 1901; AH 231.3; his own "mothers, do you want …" litany; MYP 330.1 (crowns at the judgment).
- 0:34:56 Point 4, expect opposition: a study of successful Christian parents (three traits); the mother who learned to "like being weird"; PP 95.3, 96.1, 95.2, 97.1 (Noah called a fanatic; opposition from professed worshipers); lone faithful (Enoch … Elijah); Luther paraphrase.
- 0:41:11 What the world calls weak: country living, "God's diet", homeschooling ("the research shows … nonsense"), dependence on God, the wife as homemaker.
- 0:44:05 Point 5, invest everything (PP 95.1 dup). ST Dec 19, 1878; CG 33.3; Ms 81, 1910 (named "Ellen White").
- 0:47:30 2 Chr 15:1–7: "Be ye strong … your work shall be rewarded." Commitment prayer.

**Other sources as spoken**

- 0:25:25 Bertrand Russell, 1952 (named, no title): "advances in physiology and psychology will give governments much more control over individual mentality …"; Fichte "laid it down that education should aim at destroying free will …"; "the influence of home is an obstacle"; indoctrination must begin "before the age of ten". Source: _The Impact of Science on Society_ (1951/1952), ch. 2 — checkable; correctly framed as Russell's report of Fichte.
- 0:27:05 "the late Voddie Baucham": "We cannot continue to send our children to Caesar for their education and be surprised when they come home as Romans" — widely attributed to Baucham; needs a primary source (book/sermon).
- 0:35:33 "They did some study on successful Christian parents" (three traits: parenting as primary job, didn't leave discipling to church/school, accepted being countercultural) — needs a primary source (likely George Barna, _Revolutionary Parenting_, 2007).
- 0:40:04 Martin Luther paraphrase ("on God's word I'm in the majority") — needs a primary source.
- 0:41:11 "Give me a child until he is seven" ("it's been said") — a traditional Jesuit maxim, attribution uncertain; needs a primary source.

**Strongest arguments**

- **"Human wisdom could not have devised"** (PP 92.3) set beside 1 Cor 1:27: God's plan looks weak and is strong. Clean opener for a short on why parents should look first to God's word, not to programs (0:00:43–0:06:00).
- **"Every blow struck upon the ark was a witness"** (PP 95.1): Noah's works made his preaching credible; children respect what parents live (0:11:33–0:12:38).
- **The family is the sermon** (AH 32.2; PP 579.1; MH 350.2): "No work entrusted to human beings involves greater or more far-reaching results than does the work of fathers and mothers" (0:15:59–0:17:41).
- **John the Baptist's school** (DA 101.3; Luke 1:80): the forerunner was kept out of the rabbinical schools and sent to the desert "that he might learn of nature and nature's God". The series lacks John (0:30:28).
- **Motherhood as the highest calling** (AH 231.3; PK 219.1; MYP 330.1): "An angel could not ask for a higher mission"; crowns laid down at the judgment with "She made me all I am" (0:31:02–0:34:22).
- **Expect to look foolish** (PP 95.3–97.1): the loudest critics of Noah were professed worshipers; "the men of renown … would understand the matter". Good Q on being countercultural (0:34:56–0:41:11).

**History / statistics claims**

- Parenting classes in US churches 30–40 years ago and none for "a couple of decades" — anecdote; needs a primary source.
- "The research shows … the foundation is laid in the first seven years" (spirituality, cause-and-effect, conscience, character) — needs a primary source.
- Fichte "helped design the modern Prussian-based educational system" — interpretive; the Russell quotations are real (named, checkable).
- "Homeschooling … the research shows this is all nonsense … strengthens the mind like nothing else" (0:42:20) — needs a primary source; overstated.
- "Research … recognizing the benefits of God's diet" (0:42:20) — needs a primary source.
- "Noah … 120 years" (0:29:22) vs "119 years" in A5 (0:05:10) — loose.

**Cautions for the series**

- **Public-school rhetoric**, the series' sharpest disagreement point (`ritsema-review.md` §4.2): CT 205.1, Ms 5, 1909 and CG 304.3–305.1 read as present commands; Baucham's Caesar line; "the classic definition of insanity … we keep sending them through this system" (0:27:05–0:29:22); "Russell … why the pressure to get the children out of the home earlier" (0:26:33). What the primary texts say: each passage's alternative is the church school plus the parents' own teaching (CT 205.1 "schools where they will obtain an education based on a Scriptural foundation"; Ms 5, 1909 "For years we have been establishing schools"; Ms 100, 1902 par. 28 continues "do not rest satisfied merely with having them study the Word in the church school. Teach the Scriptures to your children yourselves"). The series speaks to every family; keep the home-teaching half and leave the school verdicts in the description at most.
- **Institutional name**: he reads "Battle Creek" aloud and splices two documents (Ms 155, 1903 is about the Battle Creek _Sanitarium_). Out of speech.
- **Conspiratorial frame**: "attack … through education … seeking to destroy the family … I could show you quotes … our goal is to destroy the family" (0:24:52–0:25:25), Fichte/Prussia lineage. Same §5 rule as Ritsema: quote documents, impute no plot.
- **Church-role rhetoric**: "don't call parents away … to activities in the church"; "dispensing with ministry outside the family … when your children are young" (0:15:25–0:18:13); "I want to challenge us as a church" (0:07:06). Denominational/institutional; keep only the principle (MH 350.2).
- **Gender**: "the wife as the helpmate, the homemaker"; "Let's have strong women … the mother in the home is the strength of society" (0:43:27). Series avoids gender assumptions; the EGW mother texts can stand on their own if the owner wants them.
- **His own composition read as if quoted** (0:33:17 "most influential position attainable", "the world could be without kings, but it could not be without mothers"): NOT FOUND in the corpus; don't attribute.
- "Successful Christian parents" study, homeschool research, "first seven years", Jesuit maxim: no sources.
- Names "Ellen White" (0:46:26); "spirit of prophecy" throughout.
- True stories: the mother who studied _Education_ (his own, years apart), the "weird" family who lost friends (his own). Usable only credited or retold as the owner's own.

**Series mapping**

- No. 9 (first teacher) / no. 79: Ed 276.3 ("Never will education accomplish all that it might and should accomplish until the importance of the parents' work is fully recognized, and they receive a training"); Ms 100, 1902 par. 28 ("Teach the Scriptures to your children yourselves when you sit down, when you go out …").
- No. 10 (start age) / no. 15: CG 103.5 (= FE 156.2: happiness and the welfare of society "depend largely upon the physical and moral education which your children receive in the first years of their life"); AH 267.1 ("The labor due your child during its early years will admit of no neglect").

- No. 15 (Hannah): PP 572.3 ("opportunities of inestimable worth, interests infinitely precious, are committed to every mother").
- No. 14 (generation that forgot) / no. 74: PP 579.1 (the ill-regulated family "accumulates in a tide of evil that affects families, communities, and governments"); AH 32.2.
- No. 28 ("unlearned and ignorant men") / no. 43: PP 97.1 ("if there were any truth in what Noah had said, the men of renown—the wise, the prudent, the great men—would understand the matter"); 1 Cor 1:27.
- No. 77 (Taught of the Lord, finale): 2 Chr 15:7 ("Be ye strong therefore, and let not your hands be weak: for your work shall be rewarded"); Ms 81, 1910 par. 39.
- **NEW: "John the Baptist's school"** (Q) — Promise: the man who prepared the way for Jesus was kept out of the best schools of his day, on purpose. KJV: Luke 1:80; Matt 3:4; Luke 7:28. EGW: DA 101.3 ("God did not send him to the teachers of theology … He called him to the desert, that he might learn of nature and nature's God"). Fits Part III.
- **NEW: "The highest mission"** (Q or E) — Promise: what the mother at home is actually doing, by heaven's measure. KJV: Prov 31:28; 2 Tim 1:5; Titus 2:4. EGW: AH 231.3 ("An angel could not ask for a higher mission"); PK 219.1; MYP 330.1 ("She made me all I am through the grace of God"); ST Dec 11, 1901. Keep it as honor for the work, not a rule for every mother.

EGW: PP 92.3; PP 95.1 ("Every blow struck upon the ark was a witness"); PP 96.1 ("It was not multitudes or majorities that were on the side of right"); PP 97.1.

### B1 · Turning Hearts (O8tHAp0UrhA) · 1:00:05 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/01-turning-hearts.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Judges 2:10's generation "knew not the LORD" not because nobody taught them but because the teaching lost its method: God's method is example plus all-day relationship (Deut 6:6–7), not information.
- When Israel's parents were unfit to teach, God did not replace the family; he trained the parents (wilderness, Sinai, Deuteronomy). Parents stand in God's place, so they must represent a God who gave up everything to be with his children.

**Outline**

- 0:00:03 Christ's method of reaching people (MH 143.3); Judg 2:7–10, the strongest generation followed by one that knew neither the Lord nor his works.
- 0:02:50 Review of Eden's plan: Ed 20.1, 33.1–33.3 (family school, God the teacher; after the Fall, parents as Christ's representatives); those who left God built cities.
- 0:05:41 Aside: Cain built the first city (Gen 4:17) as defiance of "a fugitive and a vagabond" (his reading).
- 0:07:56 Ed 34.1: the parents themselves needed instruction. God's answer was to train parents (manna, Sinai, tabernacle), not to start schools; the schools of the prophets came about 400 years later. Deuteronomy is a "refresher course" given on the border of Canaan.
- 0:14:04 Deut 6:6–7 in two steps: "in thine heart" = example (Ed 41.1, Ed 187.1); "teach them diligently … sittest … walkest … liest down … risest up" = relationship, all day, anywhere.
- 0:23:05 "Parents shall stand in the place of God" (AH 293.1 = PP 308.2): the child's picture of God is the parent. God's character includes wanting to be with his children; "Jesus didn't set aside his career; he made us his career."
- 0:28:11 Jesus' family school with the Twelve (Ed 84.3); he let them enter his work and fail, and they told him their failures (DA 359.2); Ed 80.1.
- 0:30:29 Children grasp the concrete before the abstract, so the parent bond is the springboard to a bond with God; secular research on conscience, resistance to temptation and moral talk.
- 0:33:52 What failed in Judges: PP 592.2 says the works of God were to be recounted in the homes; Israel was saturated with religion, but busy parents (conquering Canaan) relied on services rather than relationship.
- 0:38:19 "True education is not information alone … is transformation … is relationship … takes time." FE 156.1: unmet longing goes to media or harmful relationships; older "fathers and mothers in Israel" should take youth in.
- 0:46:14 Practice: keep toddlers with you, wash dishes and cook together; "spending time with your child is education."
- 0:50:47 Two mothers at a seminar picnic: one scolds, one goes down to the creek with her boys; "not luck, a return on investment"; "you can be a friend and a parent." 1 John 4:19. The four disciples nearest Jesus included Judas (Ed 86.2). It is never too late: a teacher who silently washes dishes beside troubled teens.

**Bible texts**

- 0:01:10 Judg 2:7–10 (quoted).
- 0:05:41 Gen 4:12, 17 (paraphrased; Cain's city named for his son).
- 0:14:04, 0:19:06 Deut 6:6–7 (quoted; "diligently" discussed).
- 0:30:29, 0:49:37 Heb 13:5 / Matt 28:20 (alluded: "never leave thee"; "with you always"); Ps 34:15 / 1 Pet 3:12 (alluded: "ear open to your prayer").
- 0:55:20 1 John 4:19 (quoted).
- Ex–Num wilderness, Sinai, tabernacle (alluded); Mal 4:6 behind the title (not read).

**EGW quotes** (all in `verification.md`)

- 0:00:03 "only one method … Christ's method" — MH 143.3 — VERIFIED (allusion).
- 0:02:50 Eden "model for man throughout all aftertime"; schoolroom / lesson book / instructor / students — Ed 20.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:03:26 "a family school" — Ed 33.1 — VERIFIED. 0:03:59 "ordained that men and women should be His representatives" — Ed 33.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:05:08 patriarchs, cities vs fields and hills — Ed 33.3 — VERIFIED. 0:07:56 "few prepared … parents themselves needed instruction" — Ed 34.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:16:53 "Not as a dry theory …" read straight into "In order to interest our children in the Bible …" — Ed 41.1 + Ed 187.1 — **DIFFERS** (two paragraphs 146 pages apart read as one).
- 0:24:16 "parents shall stand in the place of God to their children" — AH 293.1 = PP 308.2 — VERIFIED (source context: the fifth commandment and authority).
- 0:27:38 parents "set aside dignity and reserve to be a child among children" — 3T 134.2 = FE 18.2 = CT 76.3 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:28:11 "formed the family of Jesus … house, table, closet, field" — Ed 84.3 — VERIFIED. 0:29:22 disciples told him their failures — DA 359.2 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:30:29 "discerned infinite possibilities … awakened hope through trust" — Ed 80.1 — VERIFIED (compressed).
- 0:34:25 "not to be delegated to another … stranger lips … recounted in the homes of Israel" — PP 592.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:40:37 "Young hearts yearn for sympathy and tenderness …" — FE 156.1 = CE 169.1 — **DIFFERS** (drops "may": "sources that may endanger").
- 0:53:03 "Love cannot be commanded"; "love awakens love" — DA 22.1 — VERIFIED (paraphrase).
- 0:56:26 four disciples in especially close relation, one Judas — Ed 86.2 (+ Ed 92.1) — VERIFIED; Jesus' words "if there's one hope of reaching his heart …" are White's imagined speech.
- Allusions only, not rows: "binding the hearts of the children to the parents" (0:38:53); "fathers and mothers in Israel" (0:41:44).

**Other sources as spoken**

- Unnamed "scientific research" on relationship and conscience, emotionally secure children in a "resistance to temptation task", and parent–child relationship quality moderating moral discourse (0:32:11–0:33:15) — needs a primary source (sounds like Kochanska / Laible & Thompson; he names no study).
- "Child development" claim that children need concrete experience before abstraction (0:31:02) — general; needs a source if spoken.

**Strongest arguments**

- God's response to unprepared parents was to train the parents, not to bypass the family (Ed 34.1 with Deuteronomy as the "refresher course").
- Deut 6:6–7 read as two steps: example first ("in thine heart"), then relationship ("when thou sittest … walkest …"). Clean, Bible-only, every-Christian.
- Judges 2:10: a religion-saturated nation still lost its children; the gap was method, not information (PP 592.2).
- "Love cannot be commanded; only love awakens love" (DA 22.1) with 1 John 4:19.
- Jesus kept the most troubled disciple closest (Ed 86.2, Ed 92.1): misbehavior as a plea for connection.

**History / statistics claims**

- Schools of the prophets began about 400 years after the Exodus — roughly right (Samuel's day); fine as Bible chronology, not a statistic.
- "4,673 times" the same story — rhetorical.
- Counselling anecdotes about youth from good homes who struggle — his experience; not data.

**Cautions for the series**

- Cain's city as rebellion against God's "vagabond" sentence, and "God's people always lived in the hills" — his interpretation; Ed 33.3 says only that those who departed built cities.
- "This is the juncture I believe we're at as a church" (0:29:30), "Spirit of Prophecy" named throughout — denominational framing.
- "Parents stand in the place of God" — EGW's wording concerns authority under the fifth commandment; White widens it to "the child's image of God is the parent". Usable, but say "represent".
- Health/psychology: "media chemically develops an artificial relationship" (0:40:05) — needs a source; don't speak.
- Stories: the two mothers (his own observation, true), the family that took youth in for 20 years and the dish-washing teacher (second-hand, true as told), the swimming-disciple stock photo (image, not a story). None is fiction.
- "Jesus' me time was early morning" (Mark 1:35 would support it; he cites nothing).

**Series mapping**

- **14 The generation that forgot**: Judg 2:7–10 + PP 592.2 ("not to be delegated to another … recounted in the homes of Israel"); his turn: the gap was method, not information.
- **12 When you sit, walk, and lie down**: Deut 6:6–7 two steps (example, relationship); Ed 187.1 ("weight of influence given it by our own example").
- **73 The teacher must be the lesson**: Ed 41.1 ("Not as a dry theory … must themselves practice its principles").
- **9 Your child's first teacher**: Ed 33.2 (parents as Christ's representatives); AH 293.1 = PP 308.2.
- **79 Was school God's first plan?**: Ed 34.1 — God trained Israel's parents in the wilderness rather than founding schools; schools of the prophets came centuries later.
- **80 Jesus ran a school**: Ed 84.3 (family of Jesus); Ed 86.2 (the four, including Judas); DA 359.2 (they told him their failures).
- **37 Chores are curriculum**: toddler at the sink, cooking together — the point is relationship, not output.
- **87 What about socialization?**: FE 156.1 — unmet longing for sympathy is sought elsewhere; peers win because they get the time.
- **New: "Love can't be commanded"** — why rules without relationship lose children. KJV 1 John 4:19; Deut 6:6–7; Judg 2:10. EGW DA 22.1, FE 156.1, 3T 134.2.
- **New: "Jesus kept Judas close"** — the hardest child gets the most of you. KJV Mark 3:14; John 13:1. EGW Ed 86.2, Ed 92.1.

### B2 · Daniel Chapter Zero (V4JRCj47ueI) · 59:09 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/02-daniel-chapter-zero.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Daniel's success in Daniel 1 was prepared in "Daniel chapter zero": a home education in God's word, His works and the living prophets, given by parents who had been awakened by Josiah's reading of the law.
- God's formula for crisis preparation never changes: the home is the setting, the parents are the teachers, the Bible and nature are the material, and the fruit is spiritual strength. The best preparation is connection with the source of wisdom.

**Outline**

- 0:00:03 A crisis is coming (Dan 12:1). Who wants their children prepared?
- 0:01:10 Eden's school (Ed 20.1). The content was God's word and works, the unit was the family (Ed 33.1), and after the Fall it was "adapted": parents became Christ's representatives (Ed 33.2). Patriarchs: labor, study and meditation in the fields (Ed 33.3).
- 0:07:50 Bible youth who faced a crisis, each trained at home:
  - Noah's sons built the ark with him (PP 98.1).
  - Isaac's obedience was not blind: "a sharer in Abraham's faith" (PP 152.2).
  - Joseph (Ed 52.1–3).
  - Moses: Jochebed had about 12 years (PP 243.4). "If you knew you only had 12 years…?"
  - Hannah had 3 years (CG 197.1).
  - Elisha, a rich farmer's son who still plowed.
  - David.
  - Naaman's maid (PK 245.1).
  - Esther and Mordecai: a guardian who saw the need.
- 0:19:44 Daniel and his friends as a type of the last remnant: diet, worship, prophetic time, "no fault" (Dan 6:4; Rev 14:5).
- 0:20:53 How Israel's homes taught (FE 95.3–95.5): from infancy, by figures and symbols. "Such was the training… of Daniel" (FE 96.1). His triad: words of revelation, scenes of nature, voices of the prophets.
- 0:27:39 2 Kings 20: Hezekiah's healing and the sun going back; the Babylonian envoys; he "showed his treasures" and not his God. Isaiah's prophecy that the king's sons would become eunuchs in Babylon. Hephzibah or Mary? (SDP 16.1–16.3.)
- 0:34:25 Manasseh's 55 years let the urgency fade. Josiah's reform has three steps: return to the word (2 Kgs 22), inquire of the prophet (Huldah: "too late"), then educate the families (2 Kgs 23:1–2, "small and great").
- 0:42:57 Chronology: Hezekiah healed 702 BC, the law read in 621 BC, Daniel taken in 605 BC at 15 (4T 570.1 gives 18 on entering the court; CTr 173.2 gives "15 or 16" when carried away). So the reading of the law came about a year before Daniel's birth, and his parents were probably in the crowd.
- 0:49:13 Daniel's godly mother taught him the scrolls. "This education was not gained in the schools of the time" (SDP 19.3–19.4).
- 0:50:58 Daniel 1: "Now can be seen the results of the home training" (SDP 20.2); Ed 55.1; SDP 26.1.
- 0:53:11 Babylon was the world's university, yet four homeschooled slaves came out "ten times" wiser, and Daniel read the dream its professors could not, because he was connected to the source of wisdom.
- 0:56:01 The final test: "From what homes will come the Daniels?" (SDP 49.1). The promise to parents (4T 198.2). Appeal.

**Bible texts**

- 0:00:37 Dan 12:1, quoted.
- 0:08:58 Gen 7:1, quoted inside PP 98.1.
- 0:10:07 Gen 18:19 ("I know him, that he will command his children…"), quoted.
- 0:11:18 Job 13:15, quoted.
- 0:16:55 1 Kgs 19:19 (twelve yoke), paraphrased.
- 0:18:38 Esth 4:14, alluded.
- 0:20:53 Dan 6:4 and Rev 14:5, alluded.
- 0:28:13 2 Kgs 20:1–6, 8–11, 14–18, read or paraphrased.
- 0:36:42 2 Kgs 22:8–20 (the book found, Huldah), paraphrased.
- 0:37:16 Joel 2:13 ("rend your heart…"), alluded.
- 0:41:48 2 Kgs 23:1–2, read.
- 0:46:57 Deut 6:6–7, quoted.
- 0:45:10 Dan 1:5 (three years), cited.
- 0:52:07 Dan 1:4, quoted within SDP 20.2.
- 0:54:20 1 Cor 1:20, quoted.
- 0:54:20 Dan 1:20 and Dan 2, paraphrased.
- 0:56:01 Heb 11:25, quoted within SDP 49.1.

**EGW quotes** (all in `verification.md`)

| Time    | Quote                                                                          | Ref                 | Status   |

| 0:13:31 | "It was with deep gratitude that she entered upon her now safe and happy task" | PP 243.4            | VERIFIED |
| 0:15:46 | "During the first three years of the life of Samuel…"                          | CG 197.1            | VERIFIED |
| 0:18:04 | captive maid, "a strong witness to the power of early home training"           | PK 245.1            | VERIFIED |

| 0:45:10 | "about fifteen or sixteen years old"                                           | CTr 173.2 = UL 83.2 | VERIFIED |
| 0:52:39 | "faithfully instructed in the principles of the word of God"                   | Ed 55.1             | VERIFIED |
| 0:56:35 | "Parents may well inquire, Who is sufficient for these things?"                | 4T 198.2 = CG 64.3  | VERIFIED |

**Other sources as spoken**

- Haskell, _Story of Daniel the Prophet_, named "Haskell". He reads:
  - SDP 16.1, 16.3 (Hephzibah or Mary);
  - SDP 19.3–19.4 (Daniel's godly mother);
  - SDP 20.2 ("children of the home school");
  - SDP 26.1 ("Where are the parents…").
- SDP 49.1 is read without attribution at 0:56:01.
- His own book, _Gather the Children_, at 0:42:21.
- "Someone pointed out to me" that Hezekiah's treasure was his god (0:30:31).

**Strongest arguments** (good shorts)

- **"If you only had twelve years."** Jochebed (PP 243.4–244.1) and Hannah's three years (CG 197.1): would it change your parenting, and do you know how long you have? This is the strongest hook in the batch.
- **Isaac's obedience was not blind.** He could have run, yet he "yielded a willing submission… a sharer in Abraham's faith" (PP 152.2). The goal is children who share our faith, not mere compliance.
- **Noah built the ark with his sons.** Involve children in the family's real work, not side activities (PP 98.1; Gen 7:1; Heb 11:7).
- **The parents in the crowd.** 2 Kings 23:2 says "all the people, both small and great" heard the law, and that generation's children faced Babylon. Tell it as possibility, not as fact (see Cautions).
- **The world's best university met four home-taught captives** and lost (Dan 1:4, 17, 20; Dan 2). This fits no. 19 and no. 21 as Scripture tells it.
- **"Children of the home school"** (SDP 20.2): Dan 1:4 lists the qualities they were selected for, so their learning came with them into Babylon.

**History / statistics claims**

- The dates are Hezekiah healed 702 BC, Manasseh 686, Amon 642, Josiah 640, the law read in 621, and Daniel captive in 605. These match standard Thiele-type reckonings, but each needs a primary source.
  - "The law was read one year before Daniel's birth" depends on taking his age as 15. That is an inference.
  - Haskell himself has Daniel "not over eighteen" at "607 B.C." (SDP 19.3), which conflicts with the 605/15 reckoning.
  - The series already bans speaking Daniel's captivity dates (series.md §5).
- "Babylon was the educational center of the world… the king granted the degrees." Needs a primary source.
- Elisha's twelve yoke as "the latest tractor" is a joke. Ed 58.3 does call him "the son of a wealthy farmer". White says that Elisha himself was the wealthy farmer, a small slip.

**Cautions for the series**

- **Prophecy applications.**
  - Daniel is "a representative of the 144,000".
  - Cyrus is "a type of Christ".
  - "Spiritual Babylon rules the world".
  - "The decree to worship the image of the beast" (SDP 49.1).
  - The "health message".

  All of these are denominational framing. Keep none of them in speech.

- **"Voices of the prophets" read as the "spirit of prophecy".** He reads FE 95.5 ("the voices of the prophets, heard throughout the land") as Bible + nature + EGW. In the text the phrase means Israel's own prophets. The series can keep "word and works of God" (Ed 33.1) and drop the third leg.
- **Imagined scenes told as narrative.** These are:
  - the Babylonian envoys' dialogue;
  - Josiah "pushing aside the scribe" to plead with the parents;
  - Daniel's parents in the crowd;
  - "at least five" faithful mothers (Daniel's, his three friends', Ezekiel's);
  - "Daniel's parents probably were killed".

  He flags some of them with "I just imagine". These are not allegory but conjecture, so if the series uses the story, mark each as possibility.

- **Rhetoric about schooling.**
  - 0:15:14: "countless youth who are ruined" when homeschooling parents "send them off to education" (later schooling).
  - 0:50:22: "Are we finding that in the schools of our time?"

  Both condemn families' choices. Restate them as principle.

- **The Daniel 1 reading.** He keeps the series' half (they were educated before captivity, and God was the source of their wisdom). He does not call Babylon's course indoctrination in this talk. Compare C12 0:44:04, below.

**Series mapping**

Existing topics:

- **No. 7:** Ed 20.1 (already listed); Ed 33.1 "Theirs, in the truest sense, was a family school".
- **No. 79:** Ed 33.2–33.3 (patriarchal schools were family schools; "those who departed from God built for themselves cities").
- **No. 13:** FE 95.3–95.4 (God's mighty works "recounted with eloquence and reverential awe"; "almost from infancy… by figures and symbols"). PP 592.2 is the parallel.
- **No. 15:** CG 197.1 (= RH Sept 8, 1904 par. 6): "During the first three years… by every familiar object surrounding him she sought to lead his thoughts up to the Creator". This is a different text from the PP 572.2 the series has, and it is stronger for a short.
- **No. 16:** PP 243.4 (she knew she had little time, so she was "more diligent and careful"); PP 244.1 ("could not be forgotten. They were a shield from the pride, the infidelity, and the vice…").
- **No. 18:** SDP 16.1–16.3 (already the Daniel booklet; confirms the wording).
- **No. 19:** FE 96.1 ("Such was the training… of Daniel, before the scenes of the captivity"); SDP 19.3–19.4 (his mother taught him the scrolls); SDP 20.2 ("children of the home school"); 2 Kgs 20:16–18; 2 Kgs 23:1–2. Keep it free of the age and date arithmetic.
- **No. 21:** Dan 1:4 (the entry qualities) beside Dan 1:20, and SDP 20.2.
- **No. 22:** Ed 52.1–52.3 (the night stories under the Syrian stars; "Joseph remembered his father's God").
- **No. 25:** Ed 58.3 ("the son of a wealthy farmer… took up the work that lay nearest").
- **No. 12:** Deut 6:6–7 framed as "these words… imagine Daniel's parents hearing them" (2 Kgs 23:2).
- **No. 77:** 4T 198.2 ("God alone is their sufficiency… by prayer, by study of the Bible… repaid a hundredfold").

Candidate new topics:

- **"If you only had twelve years"** (Q). _Promise:_ two mothers knew their time was short and made it count. Would it change yours?
  - KJV: Ex 2:9–10; 1 Sam 1:22, 27–28; Ps 90:12.
  - EGW: PP 243.4, 244.1; CG 197.1.
- **"Not blind obedience"** (Q). _Promise:_ Isaac could have run; why didn't he?
  - KJV: Gen 22:7–10; Gen 18:19.
  - EGW: PP 152.2.
- **"Build the ark together"** (Q). _Promise:_ the sons who helped build were the sons who went in.
  - KJV: Gen 6:22; 7:1; Heb 11:7.
  - EGW: PP 98.1.
- **"A captive girl's testimony"** (Q). _Promise:_ what early home training looks like far from home.
  - KJV: 2 Kgs 5:2–3.
  - EGW: PK 245.1.
- **"The generation that heard the law read"** (E). _Promise:_ a king learned the crisis could not be stopped and put everything into preparing the children who would face it.
  - KJV: 2 Kgs 22:13, 18–20; 23:1–2; Dan 1:3–6.
  - EGW: FE 95.3; SDP 19.3.
  - Tell it as Scripture plus clearly labeled possibility.

### B3 · Learning to Depend (iUE7v3hMp4Q) · 41:57 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/03-learning-to-depend.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Bible lives succeed by dependence on God and fail without it; worldly education trains self-reliance, so the well-schooled (Paul, Moses) needed re-education.
- Re-education and right first education follow one program: a changed environment (nature, solitude), Scripture study, communion with God, then work that keeps exposing our weakness.

**Outline**

- 0:00:03 "How do I get re-educated?" 2 Cor 12:9.
- 0:01:46 Paul: his education made him a persecutor; blind at Damascus; to Arabia (Ed 65.3, AA 125.3, AA 126.1 "in such communion is found the highest education").
- 0:04:02 Step 1, environment: associations (misquote, AH 466.2), "by beholding"; country living is about what we behold, not prepping. Paul learned from God, not from the apostles (Gal 1:17); careful aside on YouTube sermons.
- 0:10:45 Step 4, the work: "in the water men learn to swim" (Ed 268.3). Athens: Paul met logic with logic and failed; "men today talk of higher education, but this is all human" (13LtMs Ms 114, 1898).
- 0:14:38 Jesus needed no re-education, and his program was the same (Ed 77.2; MH 365.3's list of conditions, "you're looking for a curriculum, here it is"); Haskell: Jesus never entered the schools of his day.
- 0:18:41 John the Baptist: rabbinical schools would have unfitted him (DA 101.3).
- 0:19:47 Moses: peerless (PP 245.4) yet "not prepared" (PP 247.3). Abraham and Jacob "helped God"; Joseph waited.
- 0:25:23 Law of force vs law of love (Ed 65.2); mountain solitudes (Ed 63.2, 63.6, 64.1); starlight casting shadows; "God can do more with you in a week of dependence than a life of your own strength."
- 0:29:22 _Special Testimonies on Education_, "Speedy Preparation for the Work": Moses thought Egypt qualified him (SpTEd 116.1 = CT 407.1); humbled "at the very height of his human glory" (FE 344.1); learned shepherding (SpTEd 116.2); men disqualify themselves in men's schools (FE 344.3, CT 410.1–2).
- 0:37:43 "In this work is a constant education" (Ev 466.1). E. M. Bounds, "men are God's method"; a "Charles Wesley" line.

**Bible texts**

- 0:01:13, 0:09:49 (B5) 2 Cor 12:9 (quoted).
- 0:04:35 2 Cor 3:18 ("by beholding we become changed", alluded).
- 0:06:15 Gal 1:16–17 (alluded: no contact with the apostles).
- 0:27:08 Ps 8:4 (quoted fragment).
- Acts 9 (Damascus), Acts 17 (Athens), Gen 16, Gen 27, Gen 37–41, Ex 2:11–15 (alluded); John 8:44, John 5:30, 2 Pet 1:4, Acts 22:3, Ex 33:11 inside EGW passages.

**EGW quotes**

- 0:03:26 "nature's solitudes became a school … emptied his soul of prejudices" — Ed 65.3 — VERIFIED.
- 0:04:35 "If I could lift my voice to all the parents in the land, I would warn them about the danger of associations" — **DIFFERS**: nearest AH 466.2 (= 5T 544–545), "Could my voice reach the parents all through the land, I would warn them not to yield to the desires of their children in choosing their companions or associates."
- 0:07:54 Paul in the desert — AA 125.3 — VERIFIED. 0:09:02 "highest education" — AA 126.1 — VERIFIED.

- 0:15:13 "magnifying of things small … Heaven-appointed sources" — Ed 77.2 — VERIFIED. 0:16:20 "Ministry of Healing page 365" conditions of Jesus' early life — MH 365.3 (= AH 132.2) — VERIFIED.

- 0:25:23 law of force, forty years — Ed 65.2 — VERIFIED. 0:26:32 mountain solitudes — Ed 63.2 — VERIFIED. 0:27:42 dependence, "not the period too long" — Ed 63.6 + 64.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:29:54 "Moses supposed …" — SpTEd 116.1 = CT 407.1 — VERIFIED. 0:30:58 "very height of his human glory" — FE 344.1 — VERIFIED. 0:32:06 shepherd lessons, "submerged in the Holy One" — SpTEd 116.2 = FE 343.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:34:24 "place themselves in such positions as will completely disqualify them" — FE 344.3 — VERIFIED. 0:34:57 "forgotten the sound of the Teacher's voice" — CT 410.1 — VERIFIED. 0:36:04 "meekness, lowliness of heart, and distrust of self" — CT 410.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:37:43 "In this work is a constant education … discovers the weakness" — Ev 466.1 (Letter 133, 1898) — VERIFIED.
- Allusion only: "Spirit of Prophecy says an angel instructed Joseph through dreams" (0:24:18) — not searched to a ref; don't use.

**Other sources as spoken**

- Haskell, "Jesus never entered the schools of his day because of the mixture of the truth of God with heathen philosophy …" — S. N. Haskell, _The Story of Daniel the Prophet_ (1901), SDP 191.2: first sentence verified; the rest of the local paragraph is garbled OCR, so "sat at his mother's knee … crucified the Lord of life" could not be checked.
- E. M. Bounds, "men are God's method …" — _Power Through Prayer_ (1907), ch. 1; not in the corpus; he openly adds "or great educations".
- "Charles Wesley": "Give me 100 men who love only God … shake the gates of hell" — usually **John** Wesley (letter to Alexander Mather, 1777: "Give me one hundred preachers who fear nothing but sin, and desire nothing but God …"). Misattributed and reworded.

**Strongest arguments**

- The desert school: Moses, John, Paul and Jesus were all formed away from the schools of their day, in solitude and nature (Ed 63–65, DA 101.3, Ed 77.2, Luke 1:80, Gal 1:17).
- Moses: peerless in Egypt's learning, yet "not prepared"; forty years to unlearn the law of force (PP 245.4, PP 247.3, Ed 65.2).
- "Highest education" is communion of the finite with the Infinite (AA 126.1), not a degree.
- Jesus' curriculum list (MH 365.3) as a practical home checklist.
- Paul at Athens: brilliance met brilliance and failed; power from a higher source (1898 Ms 114).

**History / statistics claims**

- Moses had "10 years of worldly education" (B6 repeats it) — his estimate; PP 243–244 says his mother kept him until "about twelve years old".
- Starlight bright enough to cast a shadow — personal observation; harmless.

**Cautions for the series**

- "Ellen White writes" (0:04:35) and "Spirit of Prophecy" on camera — owner rule.
- The associations quote is a reconstruction (DIFFERS); its real context is parents yielding to children's choice of companions.
- Country-living aside ("prepping for the end of time", 0:05:10) — denominational/eschatological frame.
- "God's own schools" contrast can tip into condemning all formal training; he guards it himself (0:35:31 "we do need training … along the right lines").
- The Wesley misattribution and the unverified half of the Haskell quote.

**Series mapping**

- **23 Moses: forty years to learn, forty to unlearn**: PP 245.4; PP 247.3; Ed 65.2; Ed 63.2, 63.6, 64.1; SpTEd 116.1–116.2; FE 344.1.
- **17 Did Jesus go to school?**: Ed 77.2 ("the schools of His time … He did not seek"); MH 365.3; Haskell SDP 191.2 (pioneer).
- **27 Paul the tentmaker** (or a Paul re-education beat): Ed 65.3; AA 125.3, 126.1; 13LtMs Ms 114, 1898 (Athens).
- **86 You learn to swim in the water**: Ed 268.3; Ev 466.1 ("in this work is a constant education").
- **47 "Lord, what wilt thou have me to do?"**: CT 410.1 (absent from Christ's school, "forgotten the sound of the Teacher's voice").
- **New: "The desert school"** — why God's great leaders were trained in solitude, not seminaries. KJV Luke 1:80; Gal 1:17; Acts 7:30. EGW DA 101.3, Ed 63.2, Ed 65.2–65.3, AA 126.1.

### B4 · Not Recognizing Jesus (bq8Shcrx5kA) · 43:08 · speaker: Joshua White (delta to C13)

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/04-not-recognizing-jesus.txt` (ytt --json)

B4 is about 85% the same as C13, in the same order and with the same readings. Only the new material is listed here.

**New in B4**

- **0:00:03** He opens from the previous day's talk: "Our greatest need is to know our need."
- **0:03:28** "All sin has its root in the exaltation of the human wisdom above faith in the divine" (NOT FOUND; see verification).
- **0:05:45** He reads DA 35.2's "every heathen religion" first as "some heathen religions" and then corrects himself.
- **0:09:15** The Greek counterfeit system: sacrifices, priests, "a spirit of prophecy which was not true prophets", sacred feasts that became the games.
- **0:10:24** "How many years between the Testaments? 400. Plato lived 400 years before Christ… a period of darkness governed by Platonic philosophy". This is an inference from SDP 183.2.
- **0:12:03** "Broad education" in the spirit-of-prophecy sense (well-rounded) against the world's sense ("you need more than God's truth"). This is a useful distinction.
- **0:27:53** Supermarket illustration: "new packaging, same great product". Satan repackages Greek philosophy as humanism. A good, plain illustration.
- **0:31:47** The book "Ellen White and Maria Montessori: Two Women Who Revolutionized the World of Education", reportedly suggesting they met. No author given; needs a primary source.
- **0:36:20** He makes the 8–10 point explicitly: "until the age of 8 to 10 the child should be with the parents", so the child learns to depend on God through depending on parents. He adds testimony: "people say it was a relief to finally realize I could depend upon the Lord".
- **0:37:27** He shows a picture (grades, honors, degrees) as lessons in self-dependence.
- **0:39:08** "We're kind of having this sort of debate in our church" about overcoming sin. This is denominational; leave it out.
- **0:41:24** "This science of sciences has not a shred of human devising" (NOT FOUND).
- **0:41:59** "We're going to go tomorrow into the lessons of learning to depend."

He does not cover the John the Baptist / Jesus "bright spots", MH 400.1, cry-it-out or the "180 years" rewrite.

**Cautions (new)**

- "Many Seventh-day Adventists" and "conservative Seventh-day Adventist homeschooling families" (Montessori, Mason).
- "Our church" debate.

**Series mapping (new)**

- **No. 43 / 44:** "grades, honors, degrees teach 'I can do it'" (his line; the existing EGW leads apply).
- **No. 10:** the 8–10 dependence reason, plus FE 21.2 already listed.
- **The supermarket "new package"** illustration works for the "Does your method teach dependence?" candidate.

### B5 · Our Greatest Need (uAo5lqxRXWE) · 34:17 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/05-our-greatest-need.txt` (ytt --json)

A morning devotional that opens a week-long morning series on why the Jews rejected Jesus. Education appears only as a frame ("false education conditions us not to see our need"). It is a talk; use sparingly.

**Thesis**

- Our greatest need is to see our need: Laodicea (Rev 3:17) is blind to its condition, and grace is given only to weakness (2 Cor 12:9).
- The world's "you can do anything" message, and sin itself, numb us to that need.

**Outline**

- 0:00:03 DA 27.1 applied to today's believers awaiting the Second Coming ("180 years").
- 0:03:31 Rev 3:14–17: a doctor who knows your case before diagnosing.
- 0:07:35 The need is to see the need; addressed to "conservatives" who think themselves right.
- 0:08:41 PP 248.2; 2 Cor 12:9: "sufficient" means enough, and strength is shown only in weakness.
- 0:12:41 COL 158.2: nobody visits the doctor when feeling fine; Satan as "master anesthetist" (cares, media, games).
- 0:16:42 Children taught "you can do it"; then "just be you" when they can't. Contrasts: John 15:5; Jer 17:9; Prov 28:26; "toxic relationship" vs the True Witness; Micaiah (1 Kgs 22:8); Israel preferred Aaron to Moses.
- 0:22:01 The Fall as misplaced trust (Ed 25.2); "all sin has its roots in distrust" (his gloss); Ed 28.3.
- 0:28:16 Rev 3:18 gold, raiment, eyesalve (7BC 965.1; 4T 88.2); Zech 3: taking off filthy garments is uncomfortable but necessary.

**Bible texts**

- 0:03:31 Rev 3:14–17 (quoted); 0:28:16 Rev 3:18 (alluded/explained).
- 0:02:23 John 1:11 (quoted inside DA 27.1).
- 0:10:24 2 Cor 12:9 (quoted).
- 0:16:42 John 15:5 (quoted "without me ye can do nothing"); 0:19:02 Jer 17:9 (alluded); Prov 28:26 (paraphrased as "those who follow their heart are a fool").
- 0:20:50 1 Kgs 22:8 (paraphrased); 0:18:26 Luke 4:16–29 (alluded); Isa 64:6 (alluded); 0:29:28 Zech 3:3–4 (alluded).

**EGW quotes**

- 0:00:37 "For more than a thousand years the Jewish people had awaited …" — DA 27.1 — VERIFIED.
- 0:08:41 "In order to receive God's help, man must realize his weakness and deficiency" — PP 248.2 — VERIFIED; context flag: the same sentence goes on "he must apply his own mind … determined endeavor", and the paragraph warns against waiting for God to do what he gave us power to do.
- 0:12:41 "It is only he who knows himself to be a sinner that Christ can save …" — COL 158.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:24:13 "nothing poisonous in the fruit … distrust of God's goodness" — Ed 25.2 — VERIFIED.
- 0:25:58 "Sin not only shuts us away from God …" — Ed 28.3 — VERIFIED.
- 0:28:16 gold of faith and love, white raiment, eyesalve — 7BC 965.1 (RH April 1, 1890) — VERIFIED. 0:28:54 "purity of character … garment of heavenly texture" — 4T 88.2 — VERIFIED.
- Allusions only: DA's Nazareth scene (0:18:26); "the people liked Aaron better than Moses" (0:21:28) — not located; don't use.

**Other sources as spoken**: none.

**Strongest arguments**

- 2 Cor 12:9: grace "sufficient" only where weakness is admitted; a short on "raising kids who can say 'I need help'".
- "You can do it" vs "without me ye can do nothing": self-esteem slogans that end in "just be you" (his diagnosis; Bible contrast is clean).
- The Fall began in distrust, not appetite (Ed 25.2).

**History / statistics claims**: none.

**Cautions for the series**

- Laodicea = "us", "180 years" of awaiting (since 1844), "conservatives" — denominational.
- "Toxic relationships" riff could be heard as dismissing real abuse; he concedes there are true ones.
- PP 248.2 used one-sidedly; its paragraph stresses human effort joined with divine power.
- Doctor/painkiller and "building" analogies are illustrations, not stories.

**Series mapping**

- **62 Pride can't say "I repent"**: COL 158.2 ("only he who knows himself to be a sinner"); Rev 3:17.
- **5 Can you be smart without God?** / **6 Knowledge that puffs up**: Ed 25.2 (sin began as distrust of God's word); Ed 28.3 (sin destroys the capacity for knowing him).
- **New: "You can do it?"** — what the self-esteem script teaches about needing God. KJV John 15:5; 2 Cor 12:9; Prov 28:26. EGW PP 248.2 (with its effort clause), COL 158.2, Ed 25.2.

### B6 · Training the Army (eLa0IVpFlSo) · 1:04:13 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `autumn-leaves-nz/06-training-the-army.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- True education has three pillars: character transformation for heaven (education = redemption), harmonious development (physical first, then mental and spiritual), and training for service (true education is missionary training).
- It is centered in the family; parents must first see the importance of their work, then be trained for it (Ed 276.3).

**Outline**

- 0:00:03 Three books: his booklet _10 Principles of True Education_; _Education_ ("written for parents"); _True Education from Birth and for Eternity_ by Jennifer White, his mother.
- 0:03:25 1MCP 53.2 urgency; true vs false science of education; false education stops at this earth; "algebra is not an entrance requirement for heaven."
- 0:06:10 Family the greatest educational agency (PUR 1910); Ed 276.3 parents' training ("no master's in parenting", "why not in our own church?"); never graduated (CT 51.1); "the training of a lifetime must be discarded" (CT 20.1). Moses ~10 years in Egypt's schools, 40 to unlearn; Paul unlearned a "church" education.
- 0:11:45 Pillar 1: Ed 30.2; DA 330.2 ("redemption is that process by which the soul is trained for heaven"); PP 595.2; CT 61.2. Prov 9:10: fear of the Lord = respect and obedience; "knowledge of the holy" = intimate relationship (Gen 4:1, Spanish _conocer_). House parable: paint and landscaping (recitals, memory verses) vs the unseen foundation.
- 0:20:41 Bible as the foundation (MH 401.4), but not a Bible-only curriculum (no math-from-the-Bible schemes; just no evolution in the science book). First lesson: know God's will (CG 293.3 = CT 447). "True education is religion."
- 0:23:31 Pillar 2: harmonious development (Ed 13.1); quartet image; three-volunteer demo (physical holding up mental and spiritual); Ed 195.1; CE 13.1; activity research (JAMA Pediatrics review; Ratey, _Spark_).
- 0:29:01 Youth: divide study time with practical work (FE 538.2); neglect books before work (YI 1898). Useful work beats sport (CT 308.2); "every man, woman, and child" (TDG 203.5); tortilla story; manual training and a trade (Ed 218.1, CT 307.1, Ed 218.2); Paul needed tents as a missionary. Agriculture (Ed 219.1; SpM 134.4 "A, B, and C"); gardening studies; _Mycobacterium vaccae_; warns that worldly education plus agriculture is still false education.
- 0:40:08 Mental culture: thinkers (Ed 17.2); unused studies train students to trust another's thought (CT 387.4); Jesuit memory method (Painter, via _Studies in Christian Education_); the "endless treadmill"; Satan is content with Bible study that is never applied; MH 402.2.
- 0:48:26 Spiritual: character is the only treasure we take (CG 161.1); CG 296.3.
- 0:48:59 Pillar 3: service (Ed 13.1; MH 395.1); time for missionary work (CT 545.3); "acquaintance awakens sympathy" (Ed 269.2) with the man-walking-past illustration; CT 549.1; GW92 279.2. Caution on youth mission trips; families serve together.
- 0:57:19 Restoration (Ed 15.2) begins in the home (MH 349.1). Gaebelein's "chocolate coating"; 1938 Japan story (Dr. Mizuno had read _Education_); a light that wants to be darkness.

**Bible texts**

- 0:05:06 Matt 6:33 (paraphrased).
- 0:14:33 Prov 9:10 (quoted, slightly misread as "beginning of understanding" then corrected); 0:15:07 Prov 8:13 / 16:6 (alluded, "depart from evil").
- 0:16:48 Gen 4:1 (alluded, "Adam knew Eve").
- 0:33:30 Acts 18:3 (alluded, tents).
- 0:45:06 2 Tim 3:7 (paraphrased); 0:46:13 Jas 2:19 (alluded); 1:01:56 Matt 5:14 (alluded).

**EGW quotes** (rows in `verification.md`; all VERIFIED except one NOT FOUND)

- 0:03:25 1MCP 53.2 (dup 0:58:28; also Ritsema Ep 1). 0:06:10 PUR Aug 18, 1910 par. 1 (family the greatest educational agency; dup 0:57:55). 0:06:44 Ed 276.3 (dup 0:57:55).
- 0:08:56 CT 51.1. 0:10:04 CT 20.1. 0:11:45 Ed 30.2. 0:12:52 DA 330.2. 0:13:26 PP 595.2. 0:14:00 CT 61.2.
- 0:20:41 MH 401.4. 0:21:16 CG 293.3 = CT 447. 0:24:35 Ed 13.1 (dup 0:48:59). 0:25:43 Ed 195.1. 0:27:22 CE 13.1.
- 0:29:01 FE 538.2 ("equal portions … four hours each" is his gloss). 0:29:34 YI April 7, 1898 par. 3. 0:31:51 CT 308.2. 0:32:24 TDG 203.5. 0:32:58 Ed 218.1. 0:33:30 CT 307.1. 0:34:04 Ed 218.2. 0:34:38 Ed 219.1.
- 0:38:29 "The Garden of Eden was not only Adam's dwelling but his schoolroom" — GCDB March 6, 1899 par. 36, VERIFIED on re-check (the corpus spells it "school-room"; attribution caution as at A1). 0:38:29 SpM 134.4 "A B and C of the educational work of our schools … very first work that must be entered upon" (6T 179.2 has a variant).
- 0:40:42 Ed 17.2. 0:41:15 CT 387.4. 0:47:53 MH 402.2. 0:48:26 CG 161.1 (= COL 332), CG 296.3 (= CT 84–85).
- 0:50:05 MH 395.1. 0:51:14 CT 545.3. 0:52:20 Ed 269.2 (source context: foreign-mission geography). 0:54:34 CT 549.1. 0:55:06 GW92 279.2.
- 0:57:19 Ed 15.2. 0:57:55 MH 349.1.
- Allusions only: "little lessons oft repeated" (0:14:33); "law of heaven is unselfish service"; "fellowship with Christ in service" (0:49:32); "fathers and mothers in Israel" (0:56:12).

**Other sources as spoken**

- His own booklet; Jennifer White's _True Education from Birth and for Eternity_ (ministry products).
- "One review from the Journal of the American Medical Association of Pediatrics" (0:28:28) — no title or year; needs a primary source.
- John Ratey, _Spark: The Revolutionary New Science of Exercise and the Brain_ (2008): "an unparalleled stimulus, creating an environment in which the brain is ready, willing, and able to learn" — named, checkable; not checked here.
- Gardening studies (self-esteem, patience; one study where gardeners outscored non-gardeners in reading, math, spelling) — needs a primary source.
- _Mycobacterium vaccae_ — real research exists (mood, immune effects in mice/humans); "fight cancer, treat arthritis, raise IQ" is overreach — needs a primary source; don't speak.
- Painter, _History of Education_ (1886), on Jesuit schools, read via E. A. Sutherland, _Studies in Christian Education_ (1915) — named; not in local corpus.
- "The papal system of education makes teachers content to repeat set lessons … endless treadmill" — Sutherland (_SICE_), not EGW; not in local corpus.
- Frank Gaebelein, "a chocolate coating of Christianity on a pagan education pill" — named; needs a primary source for the wording.
- Dr. Mizuno, Japan 1938 — needs a primary source (denominational anecdote).

**Strongest arguments**

- "Redemption is that process by which the soul is trained for heaven" (DA 330.2) + "the work of education and the work of redemption are one" (Ed 30.2): education is not a subject next to religion.
- The house with no foundation: grades, recitals and memory verses are paint and landscaping (parable; Luke 6:48).
- "Knowledge of the holy" (Prov 9:10) as relational knowing (Gen 4:1): fear of the Lord is step one, relationship is understanding.
- Unused knowledge trains reflectors: years of "when will I ever use this?" teach students to trust another's thought (CT 387.4; MH 402.2 "the use they make of knowledge").
- "Acquaintance awakens sympathy, and sympathy is the spring of effective ministry" (Ed 269.2) with the walking-man illustration.
- Paul's tents mattered when he became a missionary (CT 307.1; Acts 18:3).

**History / statistics claims**

- Physical-activity benefits list (mood, cognition, norepinephrine, synaptic plasticity) attributed to one JAMA Pediatrics review — needs a primary source.
- Gardening outcomes; _M. vaccae_ benefits — needs a primary source; several look overstated.
- "About seven or eight years in girls, nine or ten in boys" mostly physical activity — needs a primary source (same claim in B7).
- "Remarkable how many university graduates can't clean a bathroom" — anecdote.
- 1938 Japan school meeting — needs a primary source.

**Cautions for the series**

- Japan Union Mission, "Seventh-day Adventist leaders", "our own church", "Adventist schools bringing agriculture back" — denominational history and institutions.
- Jesuit/"papal system" passage and "we talk about the papacy in prophecy seminars" — anti-Catholic frame; Painter is a checkable source but the frame stays out.
- Health claims (above) — counsel, not brain science.
- "Why don't we have [parent training] in our own church?" — fine in spirit; no church names on camera.
- He nuances well: not a Bible-only curriculum; "worldly education plus agriculture is still false education"; "not criticizing the wonderful teachers". These match the series' every-Christian restraint.
- Stories: tortilla grandmother (second-hand, true as told), man walking past (hypothetical illustration), quartet and volunteer demo (staged illustrations), Japan (claimed true, unverified). No fiction.

**Series mapping**

- **1 What is school actually for?**: 1MCP 53.2; DA 330.2; PP 595.2; Ed 15.2.
- **4 Teaching and saving are one work**: Ed 30.2 + DA 330.2 (redemption as training); CT 61.2.
- **3 Head, hands, heart**: Ed 13.1; Ed 195.1 (physical as the base) with the three-person image.
- **5 Can you be smart without God?**: Prov 9:10 read relationally (Gen 4:1).
- **2 Thinkers, not echoes**: CT 387.4 (crowding the mind with unused studies); MH 402.2.
- **47 "Lord, what wilt thou have me to do?"**: CG 293.3 = CT 447 ("the first great lesson in all education is to know and understand the will of God").
- **31 Should every child learn a trade?**: CT 307.1; Ed 218.1–218.2; TDG 203.5 ("every man, woman, and child … some trade … tentmaking").
- **27 Paul the tentmaker**: Acts 18:3 — he needed the trade as a missionary, not on the Sanhedrin.
- **33 Sports or useful work?**: CT 308.2.
- **11 The garden is the best classroom**: Ed 219.1; SpM 134.4 ("A B and C").
- **39 The Bible as a schoolbook**: MH 401.4 + his caution that "foundation" is not "only source".
- **45 The older help the younger** / **86 You learn to swim in the water**: CT 545.3; Ed 269.2; GW92 279.2 ("lay some special responsibility upon them").
- **76 Heaven is a school**: CT 51.1 (never graduated); Ed 13.1 ("higher joy of wider service").
- **New: "The part of the house nobody sees"** — performance vs foundation in raising children. KJV Luke 6:48; Prov 9:10. EGW CT 61.2, MH 401.4, CG 161.1.
- **New: "Acquaintance before ministry"** — why children who never meet need never serve. KJV Luke 10:33–34; Mark 6:34. EGW Ed 269.2, CT 545.3.
