### C9 · Gather the Children (pAjI6IkO66k) · 1:38:22 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `trinidad/09-gather-the-children.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Sabbath school (or Sunday school) should be **age-integrated and family-based** and **Bible-study-based**. Both are needed: a family class that is not Bible-based "is not going to feed anything".
- Aim for _interest_ (the mind engaged and asking why), not _entertainment_ (diversion that ends when the show ends). The teacher is a facilitator who draws out the class around a table.

**Outline**

- 0:01:09–0:04:33 The source is his own book, _Gather the Children: A Biblical Model of Sabbath School for Spiritually Strong Families_. Five CSW quotes on the Sabbath school's purpose (CSW 9.1, 9.2, 10.2, 10.4, 115.2).
- 0:05:08–0:07:24 The Bible as a sword: use it rightly. Goals: attendance, interest in the Bible rather than in the class, new believers, the basics ("two by two" was only partly true).
- 0:07:24–0:10:10 His record: 15 years of starting these classes. Example: within three weeks only one adult couple stayed in the traditional class. Visitors call it "the best class"; people come for Sabbath school and leave before church.
- 0:10:10–0:14:47 Age-segregation came from compulsory schooling (covered Thursday). Assemblies in Scripture include the children: Deut 31:12–13, Josh 8:35, 2 Chr 20:13, Ezra 10:1, Joel 2:16.
- 0:14:47–0:20:27 Eight benefits: the parent–child bond; a shared subject for the week; it empowers parents to disciple; it builds a habit of spiritual engagement from childhood (no "transition phase"; "youth up to 35"); it trains leaders; cross-generational ties; consideration for all ages; church unity.
- 0:20:27–0:24:26 Bible-based means God's opinion, not yours. 1T 338.3; Christ's simplicity charmed the greatest minds (Ev 525.1, CH 498.4, Ev 56.2, RH Oct 19, 1897).
- 0:24:26–0:29:32 Interesting versus entertaining (dictionary definitions). CG 521.4 defines "intensely interesting": thought and preparation, a few verses, questions, short illustrations, a little song, short prayers. Ed 185.2: Bible stories.
- 0:29:32–0:42:12 Ten tips for teachers. Be a facilitator, not a lecturer. Lead the class through your own study rather than announcing conclusions. Engage everyone (call on drifters, have the verse read, go round the circle, include non-readers, watch for the timid). Show enthusiasm (CSW 182.2; Ed 233.2, Betterton). Don't just read the lesson; let the class tell the story. Use who/what/when/where/why questions. Make it relevant (New Jerusalem mileage; lining up 5,000 people; writing on a brick). The goal is interest, not covering the lesson (CSW 112.2). Value every comment and don't answer for your child. Correct gently. Give an assignment; rotate leaders.
- 0:42:12–0:52:18 Practical: the lessons are the Bible. Lesson series he names: My Bible First (junior level) and SonLight. Tie the class to family worship through the week; parents' role (Ed 187.1, CSW 19.2). Children from non-religious homes: CSW 57.1, so don't turn the class into entertainment. Little ones on a blanket. A round table; small groups of 2–3 families; be flexible about detours.
- 0:52:18–1:36:50 **Live sample class** ("Adam and Eve: obedience"). John 3:16 memory verse. Children under 15 read Isa 14:12–14 and count Lucifer's five "I will"s. Two children draw the two trees (Gen 2:16–17); PP 53.2 ("every angel in heaven"). Board list of what makes temptation harder to resist: location, association, media, diet, boundaries, idle time, talking with the tempter. Eve left Adam (PP 53.5, alluded). A child draws a snake; the serpent "had wings" (PP 53.4, alluded); a witch-doctor goat story from Africa. Gen 3:1–5: Eve added "neither shall ye touch". The "refrigerator and mango" analogy. PP 54.2 read by a volunteer, then a list of Satan's lies on the board. 5T 310.1: thoughts → feelings → actions → habits → character. Weeds are easiest to pull when small. He covered about a quarter of the slides "and that's okay".
- 1:36:50–1:38:22 Questions and answers: an hour suits most children. Start it as a trial with one other family.

**Bible texts**

- 0:05:41 Heb 4:12 (he says "Hebrews 12:4") and Eph 6:17, alluded.
- 0:11:24 Deut 31:12–13, quoted. 0:12:32 Josh 8:35, quoted. 0:13:06 2 Chr 20:13, quoted. 0:14:13 Ezra 10:1, quoted. 0:14:13 Joel 2:16, quoted. All CLI-printed.
- 0:55:30 John 3:16 (memory verse). 0:58:05 Isa 14:12–14, read. 1:02:06 Gen 2:16–17, read. 1:14:35 Gen 3:1–5, read.
- 0:35:01 Luke 2:46 (Jesus asking questions), alluded.

**EGW quotes**: 22 rows, all **VERIFIED** (see `verification.md`). Key ones:

- CSW 115.2 ("if rightly conducted"). The next sentence, "It is not the best plan for teachers to do all the talking, but they should draw out the class to tell what they know", carries his whole method, but he does not read it.
- CG 521.4 (family worship, applied by him to Sabbath school). Ed 185.2 (the page he gives is right). Ed 233.2 (Betterton). CSW 57.1 ("might better remain at home"; his use is wider than the source).

**Other sources as spoken**

- His own book, _Gather the Children_ (2023); the science of seating and "round table" engagement is cited "in my book". Needs a primary source.
- Dictionary definitions of "interesting" and "entertaining".
- Lesson series named: My Bible First (junior); SonLight lessons.
- The witch-doctor goat story ("my friend had a goat fly over his house"): unverifiable.

**Strongest arguments**

- **Five Bible assemblies that included the children**, down to "those that suck the breasts" (Joel 2:16). It is a clean, KJV-only evidence beat for no. 85.
- **Interesting is not entertaining.** Interest pulls the mind in ("why?"); entertainment holds it only while the show lasts. CG 521.4's recipe of a few verses, questions, a short illustration, a song and a short prayer.
- **Don't announce your discovery; let them make it.** The teacher got the "rush" from studying, so lead the class through the same steps. Pair with CSW 115.2.
- **Satan's five "I will"s against Eve's one added word.** Count the "I"s in Isa 14:13–14; Eve added "neither shall ye touch it" (Gen 3:3). A ready-made hook for a short.
- **Weeds are easiest pulled when they sprout** (5T 310.1 chain: thoughts, feelings, character). Fits no. 60.

**History / statistics claims**

- Age-segregation "came from compulsory education": a history claim that needs a primary source (as for no. 85).
- "Youth conferences now count 18–35": anecdotal.
- "In Bible times at 12 you were accountable": needs a source (it is a Jewish tradition, bar mitzvah at 13; not in the KJV).
- "Science really supports" small groups and round seating: needs a primary source.
- The New Jerusalem as 1,326 miles: his conversion of 12,000 furlongs (Rev 21:16). The arithmetic is fine; make no claim about its shape.

**Cautions for the series**

- Denominational framing: "Sabbath school", "Spirit of Prophecy readings", "the Conflict of the Ages series". The series would say "family Bible class".
- The witch-doctor flying goat (1:18:06): a spiritualism anecdote that cannot be checked. Steer around it.
- "The serpent had wings and could fly" (1:17:31, from PP 53.4) is an extra-biblical detail; never speak it as Scripture.
- CSW 57.1 ("children might better remain at home") is about parents preparing lessons at home. He uses it to argue against entertainment-based classes, which is wider than the source.
- **No disagreement with the series.** This talk supports no. 85 (age-grading) from the church side, without public-school rhetoric.

**Series mapping**

- **No. 85 Why is school sorted by age?** Adds Deut 31:12–13; Josh 8:35; 2 Chr 20:13; Ezra 10:1; Joel 2:16 (all CLI-printed 2026-10-12). Use them as "God's classroom had every age in it".
- **No. 45 The older help the younger.** Adds his benefits list (children learn to talk with adults; adults learn patience with a 3-year-old's answer).
- **No. 39 The Bible as a schoolbook.** Adds 1T 338.3 ("so elevated as to be admired by the greatest minds, and yet … the humblest, feeblest child … can comprehend it"); Ed 185.2.
- **No. 73 The teacher must be the lesson.** Adds CSW 182.2 ("thoroughly learned the lesson, and are intensely interested in it"; Christ "did not aim to attract attention by eloquence"); Ed 233.2 (Betterton's "things imaginary as if they were real", an anecdote EGW retells: credit it, as for the Ritsema stories).
- **No. 54 A hundred questions a day.** Adds CG 522.1 = Ed 186 ("let them ask questions").
- **No. 60 Never yield the first time.** Adds PP 53.2 ("If they steadfastly repelled his first insinuations, they would be as secure as the heavenly messengers"); Gen 3:3; 5T 310.1.
- **NEW (Q): Interesting or entertaining?** Promise: entertainment holds a child only while it lasts; interest makes them ask why. KJV: Luke 2:46–47; Neh 8:8; Deut 6:20. EGW: CG 521.4 (= ST Aug 7, 1884); CSW 115.2; CSW 182.2.
- **NEW (Q): Should teachers do all the talking?** Promise: the best class is the one where the students tell what they found. KJV: Luke 2:46; Matt 16:13–15; Acts 17:11. EGW: CSW 115.2 ("draw out the class to tell what they know"); CSW 112.2 (don't crowd too much at once). Credit the facilitator model to White.
- **NEW (Q): Family worship children look forward to.** Promise: a few verses, a question, a story, a song, a short prayer. KJV: Deut 6:7; Ps 78:4–6; Josh 24:15. EGW: CG 521.4; CG 522.1 = Ed 186.1.

### C10 · Daniel Chapter Zero (qaxUo91yNAA) · 1:23:41 · speaker: Joshua White (delta to B2)

Transcript: `trinidad/10-daniel-chapter-zero.txt` (ytt --json)

0:00:19–0:30:34 is not the talk, so skip it: music, an opening prayer, an offering appeal for the ministry by a local brother, and a children's story on Rom 12:20–21 told by a woman. White's talk runs 0:30:34–1:23:41 (~53 min). It covers the same arc as B2 with fewer Bible examples (Moses and Samuel only), the same Ed 33 / FE 95 / 2 Kings / Haskell readings, and the same imagined Josiah plea.

**New in C10**

- **0:31:46** The audience suggests the parallels between Daniel and the remnant, and one adds "preparation to stand before the king".
- **0:40:51** PP 244.1, beyond PP 243.4: "kept the boy as long as she could… about twelve… The lessons learned at his mother's side could not be forgotten. They were a shield…"
- **0:44:15** He gives the FE ref correctly ("Fundamentals of Christian Education… page 95").
  - 0:46:34 He reads "figures and symbols" as the sanctuary system.
  - 0:45:59 He adds sacred history: "we have nothing to fear for the future, except as we shall forget…" (a bare allusion to LS 196.2; it is not checked as a quotation).
- **0:49:56** A balancing caveat the series can use: "we're not leaving schools out of this… there is a need for schools for those parents who are either incapable or negligent… [but] the school… should be modeled after the home". This matches no. 79's "an aid to parents".
- **0:51:36** "Astronomers can find that [the sun going back] today… they can trace it back". **Flag: this looks wrong.** It is the "missing day" legend and has no source.
- **1:08:43** What Daniel's parents heard in Deuteronomy:
  - Deut 4:6–8 (obedience as Israel's wisdom before the nations);
  - Deut 8:3 (the word as bread);
  - Deut 28:13 ("head and not the tail");
  - captivity foretold, with return on repentance (Deut 30:1–5).
- **1:10:30** "Maybe Daniel and his three friends were actually… three brothers… maybe it was one faithful mother." This is conjecture.
- **1:15:43** "The very reason they were kept alive was… they were seen as well educated on arrival in Babylon." This is an inference from Dan 1:4 and SDP 20.2, and the "kept alive" part is unsupported.
- **1:16:18** He extends SDP 26.1 into SDP 26.2: "more young persons could be trusted as missionaries in responsible positions and in institutions of learning. Many will yet be called to stand before judges and kings."
  - **Context flag:** he leaves out the sentence just before it in SDP 26.2. There Haskell says these principles "would fit young men who are rooted and grounded in the Scriptures, to enter worldly universities… and… come forth unsullied", and he cites the Waldenses.
  - That sentence cuts against the talk's implied "not the schools of our time" (1:13:26). It belongs in no. 19 and in the series' balance on later schooling.
- **1:17:24** "It was Yale, Stanford and Harvard all rolled up into one." "Daniel had just completed his bachelor's degree" (Dan 2).
- **1:20:14** Ps 119:99–100 ("I have more understanding than all my teachers…") applied to Daniel and to Jesus at 12. This is useful for no. 21 and no. 39.
- **1:21:24** Ed 276.3: "Never will education accomplish all that it might and should accomplish until the importance of the parents' work is fully recognized, and they receive a training for its sacred responsibilities." He calls it "our keynote". It is useful for no. 9 and no. 73.
- **1:22:34** "Don't get me wrong, we can utilize other resources", a softening of the "word, nature, SoP only" line.

**Cautions (new)**

- The astronomy claim.
- The "Yale, Stanford, Harvard" line names institutions; that is harmless but dated.
- The rest are the same as B2.

**Series mapping (new)**

- **No. 79:** the "schools for parents incapable or negligent, modeled on the home" framing (his words; the EGW lead is Ed 46.1, already listed).
- **No. 9 / 73:** Ed 276.3.
- **No. 16:** PP 244.1.
- **No. 21 / 39:** Ps 119:99–100.
- **No. 19:** SDP 26.2 (a balance note: grounded youth in worldly universities).

### C11 · Studying the Bible (vxLJUOPY9Pc) · 1:01:11 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `trinidad/11-studying-the-bible.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Jesus answered the lawyer's "What shall I do to inherit eternal life?" with "What is written… how readest thou?" (Luke 10:26). He moved the responsibility from religious teachers to personal study.
- Personal Bible study is a core part of true education and a learnable method:
  - paper;
  - verse by verse;
  - effort;
  - avoiding what kills interest;
  - prayer;
  - the Spirit;
  - willingness to be wrong;
  - obedience;
  - letting Scripture interpret Scripture;
  - lining up the fence posts;
  - no assumptions.

  He demonstrates it with a 20-minute concordance study of "inherit".

**Outline**

- 0:00:01 Luke 10:25–26: the lawyer was set up by the Pharisees (an allusion to DA 497.2). "Jesus takes the responsibility from your religious teacher to your own personal study."
- 0:04:33 Bible study is hard, and thinking skills have been attacked. The Reformation: sola scriptura; Luther, Wycliffe, Tyndale ("the boy that driveth the plow").
- 0:07:17 Tip 1, "paper is better". The phone-as-hammer joke; reading research (paper beats screen for comprehension, retention and nonfiction; screens cause overconfidence; brain areas); censorship risk for Bible apps.
- 0:12:50 Tip 2, verse by verse: Isa 28:9–10; building a foundation block by block; Ed 189.4.
- 0:15:02 Tip 3, mental effort: John 5:39, Prov 2:2–6 (read by volunteers); Ed 189.3; FE 307.2; "a lot of dopamine when the dots connect".
- 0:18:29 Tip 4, avoid what destroys interest: media, devices, fiction (MYP 273.3).
- 0:19:33 Tips 5–6, pray and seek the Spirit: SC 91.1; Jer 33:3; James 1:5; John 16:13. Test impressions by the Bible (Isa 8:20). Ed 189.2.
- 0:22:47 Tip 7, be willing to be wrong (FE 307.3; Ed 189.1). Tip 8, obey (John 7:17; Ed 189.2).
- 0:25:07 Tip 9, the Bible interprets itself (Ed 123.3):
  - Rev 12:9 (the serpent);
  - Rev 1:16, 20 (stars = angels);
  - Dan 8 (ram and goat);
  - Ps 91:1, "shadow", traced through Gen 19:8, Judg 9:15, Ps 57:1 and Isa 30:2–3 to mean protection.
- 0:31:03 Tip 10, line up the fence posts: one odd text (the thief on the cross) does not move the rest.
- 0:33:22 Tip 11, beware assumptions: Miller understood the 2300 days but assumed what "the sanctuary" meant. 1 Pet 3:15.
- 0:34:28 Live demonstration, about 20 minutes. Key word "inherit" from a concordance, with volunteers reading. The list on the board:
  - by faith (Gen 15:6–8, 18);
  - because God promised;
  - through Joshua/Jesus (Deut 1:38);
  - rest from enemies = sin (Deut 12:10; 1 Cor 15:26, 56);
  - fear the Lord (Ps 25:12–13);
  - wait on the Lord, the meek, the righteous (Ps 37:9, 11, 29; Matt 5:5);
  - love His name (Ps 69:36);
  - co-heirs with Christ (Ps 82:8; Rom 8:16–17);
  - the wise and soul-winners (Prov 3:35; 11:30);
  - trust in the Lord (Isa 57:13; 60:21);
  - willing to sacrifice all (Matt 19:29);
  - faith and patience (Heb 6:12);
  - overcomers (Rev 21:7).
- 0:58:38 Recap: "20 minutes and what great discovery". Add 10–20 minutes a day; keep a notebook; get a paper concordance; look things up on the phone but read from the paper Bible; the MySword app.

**Bible texts**

All quoted/read unless marked; the demonstration verses are read by volunteers.

- Luke 10:25–26
- Isa 28:9–10
- John 5:39
- Prov 2:2–6
- Jer 33:3
- James 1:5
- John 16:13
- Isa 8:20
- John 7:17
- Rev 12:9 (Rev 12:12 read by mistake first)
- Rev 1:16, 20
- Ps 91:1
- Gen 19:8
- Judg 9:15, Ps 57:1, Isa 30:2–3 (cited)
- Luke 23:43 and John 20:17 (alluded)
- Dan 8:14 (alluded)
- 1 Pet 3:15
- Gen 15:7–8, 18
- Deut 1:38
- Deut 12:10
- 1 Cor 15:26, 56 (cited)
- Mic 7:8 (paraphrased)
- Ps 25:12–13
- Ps 37:9, 11, 29
- Ps 69:36
- Ps 82:8
- Rom 8:16–17
- Prov 3:35
- Prov 11:30
- Isa 57:13
- Isa 60:21
- Matt 5:5 (cited)
- Matt 19:29
- Heb 6:12
- Rev 21:7

**EGW quotes** (all in `verification.md`)

| Time    | Quote                                                                            | Ref       | Status   |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | -------- |
| 0:14:30 | "In daily study the verse-by-verse method is often most helpful…"                | Ed 189.4  | VERIFIED |
| 0:17:22 | "As the miner digs for the golden treasure…"                                     | Ed 189.3  | VERIFIED |
| 0:17:54 | "your duty does not end there… a casual reading of the Scriptures is not enough" | FE 307.2  | VERIFIED |
| 0:19:01 | "Those whose imagination has not become perverted by the reading of fiction…"    | MYP 273.3 | VERIFIED |
| 0:19:33 | "Never should the Bible be studied without prayer…"                              | SC 91.1   | VERIFIED |
| 0:22:47 | "A true knowledge of the Bible…" (read twice)                                    | Ed 189.2  | VERIFIED |
| 0:23:21 | "not to take your ideas to the Bible…"                                           | FE 307.3  | VERIFIED |
| 0:23:56 | "in the spirit of a learner"                                                     | Ed 189.1  | VERIFIED |
| 0:25:07 | "Each Gospel is a supplement to the others…"                                     | Ed 123.3  | VERIFIED |

**Other sources as spoken**

- Tyndale's retort to a "learned Catholic theologian". The corpus has it in GC 246.3 and Foxe (FBOM 115.9).
- Luther and Wycliffe, paraphrased with no source.
- Paper-versus-screen research: "they find that students… absorbed more by reading on paper" and similar claims. He names no study; needs a primary source. The likely leads are Delgado et al., _Educational Research Review_ (2018), and Ackerman & Goldsmith (2011) on overconfidence.
- The "MySword" Android app.

**Strongest arguments** (good shorts)

- **"How readest thou?"** The most important question ever asked, and Jesus answered with a question that sent the man to his own Bible (Luke 10:26). This fits no. 2 and no. 39 perfectly.
- **The 20-minute word study.** Pick one word, list every verse and write what each adds. "Inherit" yields a complete answer to "What shall I do to inherit eternal life?". It is a teachable family method and makes a strong demonstration episode.
- **Let Scripture explain Scripture.** Ps 91:1's "shadow" decoded by Gen 19:8, Judg 9:15, Ps 57:1 and Isa 30:2–3 is a clean visual short (Ed 123.3).
- **Line up the fence posts.** One odd post does not move the fence. A memorable picture of interpretive humility.
- **Be willing to be wrong:** "not for proof to sustain our opinions, but in order to know what God says" (Ed 189.1; FE 307.3).
- **Why the Bible feels boring:** "Those whose imagination has not become perverted by the reading of fiction will find the Bible the most interesting of books" (MYP 273.3). It fits no. 68.

**History / statistics claims**

- Tyndale:
  - "Translated the majority of the King James Version" — overstated. Studies credit Tyndale with most of the KJV _New Testament_ wording and part of the OT. Needs a primary source.
  - "Took courage to get his head chopped off" — **wrong**. Tyndale was strangled and burned at Vilvoorde in 1536.
  - The retort itself is sound (GC 246.3; Foxe).
- Wycliffe made "the first translation of the Bible into English". That is the conventional claim (the first complete English Bible, the 1380s), though earlier partial translations existed.
- Paper versus screen:
  - The comprehension and nonfiction claims and the overconfidence claim have real support (see leads above).
  - "Prefrontal and cingulate activation on paper, parietal on screens" needs a primary source.
  - "The parietal cortex… has more to do with emotional processing" is **wrong as stated**: the parietal cortex is mainly sensory and spatial integration.
  - "Dopamine when the dots connect" is pop science.
- "Censorship… someone could delete the Bible from your app" is speculation.

**Cautions for the series**

- **Church history and an anti-Catholic edge:** the Reformation heroes, the "pope and all his laws", the "learned Catholic". Under the no-denominational-history rule, a short could use the Tyndale "plowboy" line only as Reformation history with care, or skip it.
- **Denominational:** William Miller in the 1830s and the 2300 days/sanctuary; the thief-on-the-cross punctuation argument (the state of the dead). Both are doctrinal positions that divide Christian audiences. Use the fence-post principle with a neutral example.
- **Brain-science claims:** keep paper versus screen as a lead, with no neuroscience in speech.
- **Isa 28:9–10 as a study method:** in context the line is arguably the scoffers' mockery (Isa 28:13–14). Lean on Ed 189.4 and Acts 17:11 instead.
- "This is not exactly what we're talking about in an education conference": he frames personal Bible study as "a fundamental aspect of true education". The frame suits the series.

**Series mapping**

Existing topics:

- **No. 2:** Luke 10:26 ("how readest thou?"); Acts 17:11 (already listed); FE 307.2 (study "for yourselves… not… without mental effort"); Ed 189.1 ("not for proof to sustain our opinions").
- **No. 39:** Ed 189.2–189.4 (the verse-by-verse method; "as the miner digs"); Ed 123.3 (each Gospel supplements the others; every prophecy explains another); John 5:39; Prov 2:3–5.
- **No. 41:** a paper Bible, a notebook and a concordance at home (his method). The screen research is a lead only.
- **No. 68:** MYP 273.3 (= YI Oct 9, 1902 par. 8): fiction makes the Bible seem dull.
- **No. 69:** "look it up on the phone, read it from the page" (his practice). No statistics.
- **No. 83:** the same "harder reading builds the mind" theme. Ed 189.3 adds "diligent effort and persevering thought".

Candidate new topics:

- **"How readest thou?"** (Q). _Promise:_ asked life's biggest question, Jesus didn't answer from His own authority; He sent the man to his own reading.
  - KJV: Luke 10:25–28; Acts 17:11; John 5:39.
  - EGW: Ed 189.1; FE 307.2.
- **"One word, twenty minutes"** (E). _Promise:_ a family Bible study anyone can run with a concordance and a notebook, shown live with "inherit".
  - KJV: Gen 15:6–8; Ps 37:9, 11, 29; Matt 5:5; Rom 8:16–17; Rev 21:7.
  - EGW: Ed 189.4; Ed 123.3.
- **"Let the Bible explain itself"** (Q). _Promise:_ what does "the shadow of the Almighty" mean? Four other verses answer.
  - KJV: Ps 91:1; Gen 19:8; Judg 9:15; Ps 57:1; Isa 30:2–3.
  - EGW: Ed 123.3.
  - Speak "Almighty" only as the verse's own word.
- **"Line up the fence posts"** (Q). _Promise:_ what to do with the verse that doesn't seem to fit.
  - KJV: 2 Tim 2:15; Isa 28:10 (with care); 2 Pet 1:20.
  - EGW: Ed 123.3; FE 307.3.

### C12 · Time to Leave, but No One to Go (nlQBbeWt_HM) · 47:49 · speaker: Joshua White (delta to B8)

Transcript: `trinidad/12-time-to-leave.txt` (ytt --json)

C12 is a near-duplicate of B8 (about 90%): the same Haskell and PK readings, the same decree sequence, the same parallels chart, and the same "areas of Babylon" close. It drops the 1 Cor 10 opening, the Josiah countdown is shorter, and it omits the public-school, Battle Creek and Vodie Baucham segment entirely. That makes it the gentler version.

**New in C12**

- **0:00:33** Opens with Rev 18:4 ("Come out of her, my people…"): "who's this addressed to? My people."
- **0:04:33** A recap ties the captivity to education: "they had plenty of information but they neglected the relationship aspect… they were always relying on their leaders… not studying the word of God for themselves".
- **0:10:09** "Take 20 minutes a day and study your Bible" (links to C11).
- **0:10:44** "It might seem difficult to move to the country now… the price of land has just skyrocketed in the last couple years." This is an anecdote and needs a primary source.
- **0:14:40** "Babylon" means a lifestyle: "we're not to live like the world… educate like the world."
- **0:24:47** "Esther… man has yet to write a better movie plot."
- **0:28:12** Country living: "we live in the country because it's the right environment for our children… a really nice side effect is it's a safer place". He is clearer than B8 that the motive is the children.
- **0:29:58** "How many angels' messages are there? … two decrees, then the death decree, then a third." A prophetic numerology aside.
- **0:38:25** He reads Zech 2:6–9 and pairs it with Rev 18:2, 4: "repeated twice by two angels… something they're struggling with."
- **0:39:37** He cites PK 587 and 591 for Joshua and the angel and the sealing (refs correct; he does not read the text).
- **0:43:31** "Is there also the risk that we bring Babylon into the country? Where's that Babylon? Right here." He holds up a phone. A good visual.
- **0:44:37** "He let them put him through a course of indoctrination at the Babylonian University. That's exactly what it was." This is the Ritsema reading of Daniel 1, stated more strongly than in B8.
- **0:45:48** "Proper education will encompass the dress and the diet and the media and where we live… it's a lifestyle."

**Cautions (new)**

- The prophetic chart again: 1830s rejoicing, 2300 days (caption "January 14"), the latter rain, the Sunday-law death decree.
- "Course of indoctrination… exactly what it was" (see ritsema-review §4.1).
- The land-price claim.

**Series mapping (new)**

- **No. 69:** "we can bring Babylon into the country: it's right here (phone)".
- **No. 2:** "relying on their leaders… not studying the word for themselves" (bridges to C11).
- **No. 11:** the country as "the right environment for our children" (motive stated without fear).

### C13 · Not Recognizing Jesus (2bLkyO1Qvvo) · 1:00:06 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `trinidad/13-not-recognizing-jesus.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- Israel did not recognize Jesus because centuries of false education had taught them to trust their own wisdom. Greek learning, mixed into the rabbinic schools, left them seeking deliverance from Rome, not from sin.
- The test of true versus false education is whether it teaches dependence on God or on self, and the method can be "Greek" even when the content is Christian and the school is at home.

**Outline**

- 0:00:00 Recap: the second generation (Judges 2), Daniel's preparation, Israel's failure to leave Babylon.
- 0:02:16 Why did the Jews reject Jesus? DA 27.1 and DA 299.3: in the Sermon on the Mount Christ sought "to undo the work… wrought by false education".
- 0:05:39 Eden: the root of disobedience is distrust, self placed first (Ed 25.2; Ed 24.2, "she cast away faith, the key of knowledge"). Sin destroys "both the desire and the capacity for knowing Him" (Ed 28.3), so sin blinds us to our need (COL 158.2). Illustration: you only go to the doctor when you feel sick.
- 0:14:15 Two systems (Sutherland, _Living Fountains_): the Hebrew system "rested upon faith", while paganism "placed above God the study and worship of self". DA 35.2: the principle that man saves himself, "the principle of the Jewish religion".
- 0:16:36 Babylon enslaved bodies, Medo-Persia tried to kill, and Greece captured minds (SDP 184.1). Greek religion counterfeited the Hebrew one (sacrifices, priests, oracles, the games in place of the feasts). Plato (SDP 183.2). A truth-and-error mix says "God hasn't given me enough", which is "broad education".
- 0:21:52 Plato's "science of sciences… dialectic" against Ed 126.2's "science of redemption is the science of all sciences": one teaches dependence on man, the other on God.
- 0:24:43 Jews flocked to Alexandria (SDP 190.1). Gilbert (Ministry, 1933):
  - Greece courted the Jews.
  - Young men were sent to Alexandria to "influence the Greeks" and to gain "recognition".
  - The elders objected.
  - Degrees, gowns and caps followed, and "an educational aristocracy… the Sanhedrin".
  - The Bible was studied less as students advanced (Avot 5:21: Scripture at 5, Mishnah at 10, Gemara at 15).
- 0:31:27 Haskell: the nations should have sent their youth to Jerusalem's schools of the prophets (SDP 196.1–197.1). The 400 silent years mean "not a family in Judah to whom the education of a prophet could be intrusted" (SDP 190.2).
- 0:34:16 "School at home can still be false education", because the method can stay Greek even when the content changes. PK 709.2 and DA 29.4: they sought deliverance from the Romans, not from sin.
- 0:36:28 Greece still rules the world's learning (SDP 197.3). The repackaging is humanism: the AHA and Humanists International definitions; Dewey; Montessori; Charlotte Mason ("religious humanist"); "inherent good in the child"; "work with strengths, ignore weaknesses"; "broad"/classical education.
- 0:46:10 Methods that teach self-dependence:
  - sending a small child off to school;
  - "self-soothing" (cry it out);
  - competition;
  - curricula built on men's words.
- 0:49:32 Self-reliance leads to the conclusion that "sin can't be overcome". Choose your shrine (SDP 198.1).
- 0:51:45 Two bright spots. John the Baptist was not sent "to the teachers of theology" (DA 101.3). Jesus learned from "Heaven-appointed sources: useful work, Scriptures, nature, experiences of life" (MH 400.1).
- 0:54:34 He restates DA 27.1 for "180 years… the Seventh-day Adventist people". 1SM 407.3: minds "cast in such a mold". Transformation, not information; the parent first.

**Bible texts**

- 0:03:56 John 1:11, quoted.
- 0:05:39 Gen 3, paraphrased.
- 0:13:08 Rev 3:17–18 (Laodicea), alluded.
- 0:16:36 the Daniel 2/7 kingdoms (Babylon, Medo-Persia, Greece, Rome), alluded.
- 0:17:48 Esther, alluded.
- 0:20:05 Col 2:8 / 1 Tim 6:20 ("science falsely so called", inside SDP 183.2).
- 0:43:51 Rom 3:10–12 / Jer 17:9 ("there's nothing good", "the heart… bent to evil"), paraphrased.
- 0:45:37 Isa 8:20, quoted.
- 0:47:54 "When you cry I will answer" (Isa 58:9 / Ps 91:15 / Jer 33:3), paraphrased.
- 0:50:38 Josh 24:15, quoted (inside SDP 198.1).
- 0:52:19 Luke 10:26, quoted.
- 0:53:28 Luke 2:51–52 (Jesus at His mother's knee), alluded.

**EGW quotes** (all in `verification.md`)

**Other sources as spoken**

- E. A. Sutherland, _Living Fountains or Broken Cisterns_ (1900), read at 0:14:15. It is not in the local corpus, so the wording needs a primary-source check.
- F. C. Gilbert, "Why the Jews Rejected Jesus as the Messiah", _The Ministry_, 1933, read 0:26:24–0:31:27. Not in the corpus; needs the article.
- Haskell, SDP 183.2, 184.1, 190.1, 190.2, 196.1–197.1, 197.3, 198.1, all verified in the corpus.
  - At 0:31:27 he reads SDP 196.1–197.1 as if it continued Gilbert; it is Haskell. B4 attributes it correctly.
- Plato on dialectic as "the science of sciences". Needs a primary source; the wording is not Plato's.
- The American Humanist Association and Humanists International definitions. Checkable on their sites; the wording matches their published statements.
- The "Ethics of the Fathers" ages (Avot 5:21), via Gilbert.
- The John Dewey quote "Faith in the prayer-hearing God is an unproved and outmoded faith. There is no God and there is no soul…". Needs a primary source; this looks like a widely circulated misattribution and is not in Dewey's works as far as known.
- A book comparing Ellen White and Maria Montessori. He names no title or author; needs a primary source.

**Strongest arguments** (good shorts)

- **"Do you only go to the doctor when you feel sick?"** We must know our need (COL 158.2; Luke 5:31). An education that hides the need hides the Healer.
- **Two sciences of sciences.** Man's intellect judging true and false, against the science of redemption (Ed 126.2). Present it as a contrast of principles, without the unsourced Plato wording.
- **Why 400 silent years?** "Not a family in Judah to whom the education of a prophet could be intrusted" (SDP 190.2). Then God raised John in the desert (DA 101.3; Luke 1:80). This is a striking and unusual short.
- **Recognition from the world.** Israel sent its brightest youth to Alexandria "to influence the Greeks" and to gain standing, and came home with degrees and gowns (Gilbert, if the article checks out). Pair it with SDP 197.1 ("nations should have sent their youth to… Jerusalem").
- **"School at home can still be Greek"**: the method teaches as loudly as the content. His best non-polemical line, and it applies to every family.
- **Jesus' curriculum** (MH 400.1): useful work, Scripture, nature and life's experiences, all four available to every family.

**History / statistics claims**

- "The history of Greece fills the time between Malachi and John" (Haskell). This is approximate: the Persian period ran about 100 years past Malachi, and Rome ruled from 63 BC. Needs a primary source.
- Gilbert:
  - "Rabbi… was a Greek degree" — **wrong**. _Rabbi_ is Hebrew/Aramaic, "my master".
  - Greek "Sanhedrin" from _synedrion_ — correct.
  - "the Sanhedrin which crucified Jesus" — Rome crucified; the Sanhedrin condemned (Mark 14:64; John 19:6, 16).
  - Degrees, gowns and caps — needs a primary source.
- "The Greeks descend from Jacob son of Noah" is a caption garble for Javan, son of Japheth (Gen 10:2, 4). Greek games "replaced the national feasts" is interpretive.
- "Dewey, father of modern public education in the United States" is loose. The Dewey quote needs a primary source (see above).
- Montessori "was humanist" — needs a primary source.
- Mason "termed a religious humanist" with "inherent good in the child" — **looks wrong**. Mason was a practising Anglican, and her first principle is that children are "born persons… not born either good or bad, but with possibilities for good and for evil".

**Cautions for the series**

- **Denominational:**
  - 0:54:34 rewrites DA 27.1 for "the Seventh-day Adventist people" and "180 years";
  - "our church" debates (in B4);
  - "spirit of prophecy".
- **Named educators condemned:**
  - "I am appalled how many… look at Dewey";
  - Montessori and Charlotte Mason, whose curriculum "is used by a lot of conservative Seventh-day Adventists";
  - classical education ("read whatever you want").

  This is rhetoric that condemns families' curriculum choices, and two of the claims look wrong (Mason; the Dewey quote). Keep the principle (does the method teach dependence on God?) and drop the names.

- **Parenting/science:** "self-soothing… teaches the child they'll have to figure it out themselves… harder to trust in Jesus" (0:47:19). This is plausible pastoral counsel but carries a developmental claim that needs a source. Keep it to Scripture (Isa 49:15; 66:13) if used at all.
- **"Those taught to rely on self conclude sin cannot be overcome"** (0:49:32) ties pedagogy to an internal Adventist theological debate. Leave it out.
- **Overstated readings:** "Satan… invented 'don't worry about the weak points'" (0:44:24) is a caricature of strengths-based teaching.

**Series mapping**

Existing topics:

- **No. 2:** Ed 24.2 ("she distrusted His wisdom. She cast away faith, the key of knowledge"); SDP 198.1 ("seeking to find out by experiment and speculation what must be known by faith").
- **No. 5:** Ed 126.2 (the science of redemption, the highest study); Ed 28.3 (sin destroys "the capacity for knowing Him").
- **No. 6:** Ed 25.2–25.3 (the mingling of evil with good confused the mind); SDP 183.2 ("her literary minds worshiped the intellect"); SDP 184.1 ("Greece captured their minds").
- **No. 17:** MH 400.1 (already used).
- **No. 28:** DA 101.3 (John was not sent to "the teachers of theology"; "the training of the rabbinical schools would have unfitted him").
- **No. 42 / 43:** Gilbert's degrees, gowns and "recognition from the world's greatest nation". Gated on reading the 1933 article.
- **No. 44:** "competition… beat somebody else… we don't see our need when we've learned to prove ourselves" (his line). The EGW lead is already listed (Ed 225.4–226.1).
- **No. 10:** the dependence argument (a small child learns to depend on God through depending on parents). His reason; no EGW ref given.
- **No. 24:** SDP 197.1 (nations should have sent their youth to the schools of the prophets at Jerusalem).

Candidate new topics:

- **"Why didn't they recognize Him?"** (E). _Promise:_ a nation waited a thousand years for the Messiah and missed Him, and the reason was what they had been taught.
  - KJV: John 1:11; Luke 19:42, 44; Matt 5–7.
  - EGW: DA 27.1, 29.4, 299.3; PK 709.2; 1SM 407.3.
- **"Four hundred silent years"** (Q). _Promise:_ why heaven sent no prophet: there was no home fit to raise one.
  - KJV: Mal 4:5–6; Luke 1:15–17, 80.
  - EGW: DA 101.3; pioneer: SDP 190.2.
- **"Does your method teach dependence?"** (Q). _Promise:_ the same lesson can teach a child to lean on God or on themselves.
  - KJV: Prov 3:5–7; John 15:5; Jer 17:5, 7.
  - EGW: COL 158.2; 8T 316.1; Ed 24.2.
- **"Do you go to the doctor when you feel fine?"** (Q). _Promise:_ an education that hides our need hides the Healer.
  - KJV: Luke 5:31–32; Rev 3:17–18.
  - EGW: COL 158.2.

### C14 · A Safe Choice (QqRhQfMn1Xw) · 1:07:07 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `trinidad/14-a-safe-choice.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- God has one plan for each life, not a menu, and our own best judgment ends in death (Prov 16:25; Jer 10:23). Knowing God's will is "the first great lesson" of true education.
- Ed 267.3 gives three rules, and **the order matters**. (1) Do your best in the work that lies nearest. (2) Commit your ways to God, meaning obey what is already revealed. (3) Then watch for His providence. Open doors without steps 1–2 can be Satan's doors.
- God does not depend on degrees. Faithfulness, obedience and self-study can put a person where credentials would usually be needed (Joseph, Daniel, his own story).

**Outline**

- 0:00:34–0:04:03 "Follow your heart" and "plan A or B" rejected (Prov 16:25; Jer 10:23). FE 414.2.
- 0:04:36–0:06:16 Ed 267.3, the three rules, applied beyond occupation; sequence is key.
- 0:06:16–0:14:06 Step 1, the work that lies nearest: chores and opportunities to serve in our path. Luke 16:10; the talents (Matt 25), where the buried talent means "invested in the world"; Eccl 9:10. MYP 148.2, MH 472.3–4, MYP 148.3. "Restless modern Eves" (AH 115.2): motherhood is the greater work. Fathers: 1 Tim 5:8.
- 0:14:06–0:20:58 Step 2, commit your ways: obey revealed will before looking for signs. Daniel 1:8 needed no "sign". Prov 3:5–6; John 7:17. The oil-lamp illustration: light for one step at a time. Slide splice: GW92 388.1 + 1SM 172.2 + 1SM 401.2. Jer 29:13.
- 0:20:58–0:24:26 Step 3, providence: doors, circumstances, impressions. Satan opens doors too. A friend's stolen tool came back, he took it as "a sign", and he declined spiritually. Matt 12:39 (an evil generation seeks a sign). LS 132.1 ("keep the eye single … the path shall brighten").
- 0:24:26–0:28:55 Story: a young man with a single mother torn over university. He decided not to go, and within hours a way opened to support his mother.
- 0:28:55–0:43:38 Joseph as the model. Faithful in the nearest work (Ed 52.2). Caravan and grief (PP 213.1). Full surrender (PP 213.3–214.1). Given the dirtiest jobs, yet faithful. Dreams (3SG 138.2). Fidelity in palace and prison (Ed 51.3 + 52.4). **His reading of Potiphar's wife** (0:38:32–0:43:02): the bigger temptation was an "open door" out of slavery toward the leadership his dreams promised. In that culture the act was normal and even religiously accepted, so refusing cost him that door (PP 217.2). He says he is "postulating a little".
- 0:43:38–0:52:31 Applying it to careers: MH 395.1 ("every son and daughter … a missionary") weeds out careers. We seek God about the career but not about the preparation. University "extremely dangerous … even at one of our own schools" (with a "not always wrong" caveat). Moses unlearned for 40 years; Joseph had no degree; Daniel was placed "despite his Babylonian training". Jacob and Abraham tried to engineer God's will (Hagar); Joseph waited.
- 0:52:31–1:06:25 His own story. Top student and scholarships, aiming at a PhD in education. Stopped by a counsel he read as "students should not go into debt for schooling" (not found as stated; see verification). Farm work; summer savings vanished (Hag 1:6, "a bag with holes"). Self-study; Ed 263.2 and 271.2 ("rightly trained"). An upper-division paper on "why children shouldn't go to school until 8 or 10" won the professor's first 100% in 14 years. Seminars followed: "every continent except Antarctica"; advising schools; a ministry leader asking him, who has no PhD, for leadership. DA 250.4.

**Bible texts**

- 0:02:18 Prov 16:25, quoted. 0:03:29 Jer 10:23, quoted. 0:08:28 Luke 16:10, quoted. 0:09:04 Matt 25:14–30, retold. 0:10:47 Eccl 9:10, alluded. 0:12:27 2 Chr 31:21 (Hezekiah, via MYP 148.3). 0:14:06 1 Tim 5:8, paraphrased.
- 0:16:28 Dan 1:8. 0:17:01 Prov 3:5–6, quoted. 0:17:35 John 7:17, quoted. 0:18:07 Ps 119:105, alluded (the lamp). 0:20:22 Jer 29:13, quoted. 0:23:17 Matt 12:39 / 16:4, paraphrased.
- 0:28:55–0:43:38 Gen 37, 39 (39:9 "how then can I do this great wickedness"), retold. 0:48:09 Acts 7:22 (Moses), alluded. 0:50:04 Gen 25:23, 27 (Jacob), alluded; Gen 16 (Hagar), alluded. 0:57:48 Hag 1:6, alluded.

**EGW quotes**: 19 rows (16 VERIFIED, 2 DIFFERS, 1 NOT FOUND); see `verification.md`. Notes:

- Ed 267.3: the page he gives is right.
- The 0:19:49 slide splices three statements from 1890–1901 (**DIFFERS**, words accurate).
- 0:37:25 joins Ed 51.3's last line to Ed 52.4 (**DIFFERS**, adjacent text).
- LS 132.1 is an angel's word to EGW in a dream about leaving her own children for the work; his use is wider.
- "Students should not go into debt for their schooling" (0:53:40) is **NOT FOUND** as a rule. The nearest is CT 527.1 (better to earn as you study than to leave "under a burden of debt") and CT 69.1 (school debt).

**Other sources as spoken**

- "A friend … speaker": "our ability to understand God's will is directly linked to our willingness to follow it". Not EGW; unnamed.
- The oil-lamp illustration: his own (generic).
- His research paper (no title or venue) and the professor's "first 100% in 14 years": anecdote.

**Strongest arguments**

- **Three rules in order** (Ed 267.3). This is the single most usable item for no. 47: do the nearest work, obey what you already know, then read the doors. "Open doors" without the first two can mislead.
- **The lamp gives one step of light** (Ps 119:105): you see the next step only when you take the one you have. A strong visual hook. Credit the framing to White.
- **Joseph faithful as a slave, not just as a ruler** (Ed 52.4: "Faithfulness in every station … trained every power for highest service"). Sold at 17, he managed a house before he ran a nation.
- **Daniel 1:8 needed no sign**: when God has spoken, there is nothing to pray about. Good for no. 19 or no. 47.
- **"Every son and daughter of God is called to be a missionary"** (MH 395.1) as a filter for careers.

**History / statistics claims**

- Ancient travellers carried the oil lamp low, lighting one step: plausible, but needs a source if spoken as history.
- In the Egyptian setting, adultery was "essential practice … part of the pagan religions" and "religiously accepted": **needs a primary source**, and he flags it as postulating. Don't use it.
- His own career claims (seminars on six continents; advising schools; no PhD): autobiography.

**Cautions for the series**

- **University "extremely dangerous … even at one of our own schools"** (0:46:26–0:47:36). A sweeping judgment on families' choices and a denominational institutional reference. The series speaks to every family, so steer around it.
- **The debt "rule" is overstated.** He calls it "pretty clear", but the corpus counsel is a preference (CT 527.1). Never state it as a prohibition.
- **The reading of Potiphar's wife** (the temptation as a route to freedom; adultery culturally and religiously normal) is his own speculation beyond PP 217.2. If used, keep to PP 217.2's own framing: "His whole future life depended upon the decision of the moment".
- The "spirit of prophecy" label and "one of our schools" are denominational.
- Personal success stories (the 100%, the PhD-holders asking him for leadership) work as testimony, not evidence.
- The anti-degree tone overlaps with what the series leaves out of _Credits_ (no. 43). Ed 51–52 and DA 250.4 make the point without polemic.

**Series mapping**

- **No. 47 "Lord, what wilt thou have me to do?"** Adds Ed 267.3 (the three rules, verbatim); Prov 16:25; Jer 10:23; Prov 3:5–6; John 7:17; MH 395.1; GW92 388.1 ("conformity to God's revealed will always increases faith and knowledge"); FE 414.2 ("The first great lesson in all education is to know and understand the will of God").
- **No. 25 Elisha: faithful in little things.** Adds Luke 16:10; MYP 148.2 (waiting for "some great work", "their life passes away"); MYP 148.3; MH 472.3–4 ("it is to be accepted as God's choice for us").
- **No. 22 Joseph.** Adds PP 213.1 (the caravan; "from the tenderly cherished son to the despised and helpless slave"); PP 213.3–214.1 (he "then and there gave himself fully to the Lord"); Ed 51.3 ("The same fidelity was manifest in the palace of the Pharaohs as in the prisoner's cell"); Ed 52.2, 52.4; PP 217.2.
- **No. 28 "Unlearned and ignorant men" / no. 43 The diploma heaven signs.** Adds DA 250.4 ("no limit to the usefulness of one who, by putting self aside, makes room for the working of the Holy Spirit"). Also Joseph and Daniel placed above credentialed men, without the anti-degree polemic.
- **No. 86 You learn to swim in the water.** Adds Ed 263.2–264 ("He has put it in our power, through co-operation with Him, to bring this scene of misery to an end"); Ed 271.2 ("such an army of workers as our youth, rightly trained").
- **NEW (Q): A lamp for one step.** Promise: God rarely shows the whole road; He lights the next step of obedience. KJV: Ps 119:105; Prov 4:18; John 7:17; Prov 3:5–6. EGW: GW92 388.1; 1SM 401.2 ("not making the most of the light … already given"); Ed 267.3.
- **NEW (Q): Can an open door be the wrong door?** Promise: a sign never outranks what God has already said. KJV: Prov 16:25; Matt 12:39; Gen 39:9; Dan 1:8. EGW: Ed 267.3 (order of the rules); PP 217.2.

### C15 · Final Charge (WH3QrmDHQlg) · 23:22 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `trinidad/15-final-charge.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- True education in one word is **unselfishness**, "the principle of God's kingdom" that Satan denies exists (Ed 154.3). Education and redemption are one work.
- The world and the universe await a practical demonstration of unselfishness. In the last days it takes the form of restored families (Mal 4:5–6). That calls for a change of lifestyle and a change of mindset, **from information to transformation**.

**Outline**

- 0:00:33–0:02:17 Audience one-word summaries (relationship, faithfulness, obedience, love…). His pick: sacrifice, then unselfishness. "The plan of redemption begins and ends with a gift" (5T 730.4).
- 0:02:54–0:05:46 Ed 154.3 read twice (he says "page 54"). Matt 22:35–40: the two commandments sum to unselfish love. Isa 14:13–14: Satan's "I will"s are selfishness. "False education" prepares for Satan's kingdom; true education for God's.
- 0:05:46–0:11:46 Job 1:6–11: a council of representatives from the universe's dominions, and Satan claims Earth. "Hast thou considered" means "set your heart on", a deep reflection. "Doth Job fear God for nought?" God leaves the argument to Job's life. Our lives are the same test.
- 0:11:46–0:16:24 The sacrifices of true education: one income, a mother at home, country living, time with the children, pleading for wisdom rather than "following a book". Mal 4:5–6 applied to the second coming; the family as "the most powerful evidence … of Christianity" (AH 32.2).
- 0:16:24–0:21:13 Two changes. **Lifestyle** (country, the Deuteronomy method), because words are not enough. **Mindset**, from information to transformation. Two examples. "Wait until 8, then start academics" (information) versus "eight years to shape character" (transformation). Hours spent hunting the right curriculum while forgetting the relationship.
- 0:21:13–0:23:22 "You'll never arrive." Stepping stones, "pressing on the upward way". Closing prayer.

**Bible texts**

- 0:04:04 Matt 22:35–40, paraphrased. 0:05:11 Isa 14:13–14, alluded. 0:06:31 Job 1:6–11, read/paraphrased. 0:11:11 1 Cor 4:9 ("spectacle"), alluded. 0:14:10 Mal 4:5–6, quoted.

**EGW quotes**

- 0:02:17 "The plan of redemption begins and ends with a gift". **5T 730.4, VERIFIED**.
- 0:02:54 "Unselfishness, the principle of God's kingdom". **Ed 154.3, VERIFIED** (page 154, not 54). Ed 154.4–155.1 then turns to Job, the same move he makes.
- 0:15:49 "the most powerful evidence in favor of Christianity". **AH 32.2 (= 4T 304), VERIFIED** (the sentence is left unfinished; the source's answer is "a well-ordered, well-disciplined family").

**Other sources as spoken**: none. ("Pressing on the upward way" is the hymn "Higher Ground", alluded.)

**Strongest arguments**

- **The Job test.** Satan's charge was that no one serves God for nothing; God let Job's life answer it (Job 1:9; Ed 154.3–155.1). A family that gives up comfort for its children answers the same charge. Strong episode material.
- **Information versus transformation**: "eight years to shape character, not eight years to wait". A sharp corrective even for the series' own no. 10.
- **Curriculum versus relationship**: hours of curriculum research can crowd out time with the child.
- **"You'll never arrive"**: education as a lifelong, eternal process (fits no. 76).

**History / statistics claims**: none.

**Cautions for the series**

- The Job 1:6 "council of representatives of the dominions" and "Earth is my territory" are interpretive (EGW-derived), not in the KJV text; keep to the text.
- Mal 4:5–6 "specifically the second coming" is a prophecy application. Quote the verse and let it stand.
- His list of sacrifices (one income, country living) can sound like a checklist for every family. The series keeps the principle (unselfishness) and lets families apply it.

**Series mapping**

- **No. 1 What is school actually for? / no. 4 Teaching and saving are one work.** Adds Ed 154.3 ("Unselfishness, the principle of God's kingdom, is the principle that Satan hates"); 5T 730.4.
- **No. 10 When should kids start school?** Adds the "information versus transformation" turn: the years before 8–10 are character years, not waiting years.
- **No. 77 Taught of the Lord (finale).** Adds Mal 4:6 (CLI-printed); AH 32.2 = 4T 304 ("The greatest evidence of the power of Christianity that can be presented to the world is a well-ordered, well-disciplined family").
- **NEW (E): The Job test.** Promise: Satan said no one serves God for nothing; one man's life answered him, and a family's still can. KJV: Job 1:8–11; 2:3; Mal 4:6; 1 Cor 4:9. EGW: Ed 154.3–155.1; AH 32.2.

## D · True Education 3 Part Series (Autumn Leaves NZ camp meeting, January 2018)

https://www.youtube.com/playlist?list=PLLa98Guf3JC1v-NznPBufJXkrEsfzdN2l. Folder `true-education-3-part/`. Channel "Autumn Leaves NZ". Upload dates from `yt-dlp --print "%(upload_date)s"` (2026-10-13).

A three-part series given at one camp meeting: D1 on Friday afternoon ("as Sabbath draws near"), D2 on Sabbath morning, D3 on Sabbath afternoon. He calls them "three messages that are separate and yet … go together". This is the earliest dated recording of his material in the four playlists. Playlist B is a later Autumn Leaves visit (B6 was uploaded 2026-01-23), so D1 is the 2018 form of the talk B6 and C1 repeat.

| #   | Id          | Length | Uploaded   | Title                                           | Speaker      |

| D1  | BxXUUqt7hNw | 54:15  | 2018-01-19 | True Education P1 - A Foundation                | Joshua White |
| D2  | a8qrE2KB1Co | 41:32  | 2018-01-19 | True Education P2 - In Pity For Our Weakness    | Joshua White |
| D3  | KeU3-w8iFw4 | 59:18  | 2018-01-20 | True Education P3 - Respectable Conventionality | Joshua White |

**Speakers.** Joshua White in all three. Each opens with his own prayer ("speak through me"), and he speaks of "Michigan where I live" (D2) and of his own Africa work (D1). D3 mentions "Dr. Ramsey's appeal this morning", another camp-meeting speaker, who is not recorded here.

**Dedupe.** D1 is a delta to B6 (and C1, A1): the same three pillars and the same evidence chain. D2 is new as a whole talk; only its Eden-to-Sinai reading (Ed 33–35) overlaps B1. D3 is new and is given in full. The agent for playlist F writes its two "Respectable Conventionality" talks as deltas against D3.

**EGW check.** 95 rows: 94 VERIFIED, 1 DIFFERS, 0 NOT FOUND (`verification.md`). Two of D's hits correct NOT FOUND rows in batch C1 (see the end of `verification.md`).

### D1 · True Education P1 - A Foundation (BxXUUqt7hNw) · 54:15 · speaker: Joshua White (delta to B6)

Transcript: `true-education-3-part/01-a-foundation.txt` (ytt --json)

About 75% of D1 repeats B6/C1. It has the same three goals (education = redemption, Ed 30.2; harmonious development, Ed 13.1; service, Ed 13.1 / MH 395.1), the same house-without-a-foundation, quartet and three-trees images, and the same evidence chain: Ratey's "unparalleled stimulus", the activity-benefits list, "nobody asks if history class makes money", the three kinds of physical training, the garden test-score studies, _M. vaccae_ and "dirt is the new Prozac", the Jefferson line, Sutherland's Eden passage, Eden "his schoolroom" (GCDB 1899), "A, B and C" (SpM 134.4), CT 545.3, CT 547.2, CT 549.1 and GW92 279.2. It has no live leaning demo, no Jesuit/Painter passage and no Gaebelein.

**New in BxXUUqt7hNw**

- **0:00:00 Japan 1938 as the frame**, opening and closing the talk: Mizuno's "If you follow your plan, you have no reason to worry. If you do not follow your plan, you have no reason to exist." He widens it at once to "a plan of education, a plan of health, a plan of evangelism … or we may as well be just another denomination". Denominational lore; needs a primary source (B6 and C1 close with it).
- **0:12:02 A run of four FE/CG definitions**, all VERIFIED, read back to back without refs. "True education is religion" (CG 493.5); "the power and soul of true education is a knowledge of God, and of Jesus Christ whom He hath sent" (FE 392.1); "the science of true education is the truth … so deeply impressed … that it cannot be obliterated" (FE 542.1); "True education is the inculcation of those ideas … renew the mind and transform the character … fortify the mind against the deceptive whisperings … fit the learned to become a co-worker with Christ" (FE 543.2). This is the cleanest one-minute definition block in his material for no. 1 and no. 4.
- **0:13:05** SpM 245.4: "a place where our children can study the Bible, which is the foundation of all true education". He adds "the home or the school, either one". **Context:** the source is an appeal to finish building a schoolhouse (Ms 100, 1902, the same manuscript flagged in `verification.md`).
- **0:14:37** COL 330.3: "the preparation of the physical, mental, and moral powers for the performance of every duty … training of body, mind, and soul for divine service". He notes the pair said twice. Then CT 64.1.
- **0:15:08 "Combined" = blended (rice and beans).** CG 295.2 / CT 149.2, "practical and literary training should be combined". He reads it as teaching academics _through_ gardening and trades, which he says he trains teachers to do "at the school I've worked with in Africa". **Context:** CT 149.2 is about children sharing domestic duties alongside their studies. The blended-lesson method is his application, not the text.
- **0:16:43 Hippocampus study** (higher-fit children, larger hippocampal volumes, better relational memory). This is Chaddock et al. 2010 (_Brain Research_) by its wording; a lead. Ed 207.2 ("the whole body is designed for action") and FE 146.3 (no studies that crowd out "muscular exertion in the open air"; "stated hours … manual labor").
- **0:20:24** AH 508.3–508.4 (Avondale: "the time of study must be divided"; students cleared land and built houses). He names Australia correctly.
- **0:32:21 Country living** (see Cautions): "This is one of the incredible benefits of country living. We have been counseled to move our families to the country." Benefits: awareness of the world, the power of attention, responsibility, cause-to-effect reasoning, industry, economy, diligence. "There is science to back up every one of them" (none named). "Country living is the powerful learning environment that God has designed for our children."
- **0:33:24** Ed 196.2 (physiology and hygiene "begun by the mother in the home"); he wants children trained to treat sickness, as "medical missionaries".
- **0:34:27 CE 51.1** read in pieces. Teachers need "no less of piety" and "a thorough knowledge of the sciences". "He is a Christian who aims to reach the highest attainments for the purpose of doing others good." "Education balanced by a solid religious experience fits the child of God to do his appointed work steadily, firmly, understandingly." The middle sentence was NOT FOUND in batch C1; it is CE 51.1 / CT 505.3.
- **0:35:58** CE 24.1 (character "fitted … to discharge the duties of the present life, and to enter at last upon the future, immortal life"); CG 296.3; CG 293.3 with "go to Education page 267" for the three ways to know God's will (Ed 267.3, page right; C14 develops it); CG 161.1; Ed 225.3.
- **0:42:16** FE 541.3: "The necessity of establishing Christian schools is urged upon me very strongly … a wrong education often starts the mind on the road to infidelity." VERIFIED, with two middle sentences dropped.
- **0:43:18–0:51:41 Kibidula, Tanzania.** He opens with UN figures: primary attendance 92–96%, secondary 32% of boys and 24% of girls, 71% of the population under 30, one in six girls aged 15–19 pregnant each year, 5–8,000 girls expelled yearly for pregnancy, about a million child domestic workers. The Kibidula mission with Reach Switzerland (2004) runs a farm school for primary failures and dropouts: three units, about 60 students, each with a garden, orchard and field. They press their own oil and live with their teachers as "a family school". After about two hours a week of academics they pass the national exams they once failed. "99% … have left baptized Seventh-day Adventist Christians." All of it needs a primary source. It is a vivid true-story candidate, but denominational as told.
- **0:51:41** "Why do we not have a thousand such schools…?" Luther and Melanchthon "transformed the nation of Germany in one generation" (the Sutherland frame; see B7).
- **0:52:43** Ed 83.5 close ("what worse than folly is it to seek an education apart from Him … hew out broken cisterns").

**Cautions (new)**

- "No reason to exist … just another denomination" (0:02:36) is denominational identity rhetoric. So are "leave not Seventh-day Adventists" (0:50:39) and the 99% baptism figure.
- Country living is presented as God's learning environment and as counsel to follow. The motive is the children's learning, with no fear or crisis framing. The "science behind every one" claim is unsourced. SpM 134.4, read just after it, is about siting the Avondale school "at a distance from the city"; he does not read that half.
- Tanzania statistics and the 99% baptism figure: leads only.
- The _M. vaccae_ claims are overstated again, plus new advice: "don't scrub" the vegetables, and soaking hands in dirt "cures the depression often". Don't speak.
- "Early school start" is folded into the Tanzania failure ("they start school very early, which we've seen…"). That is his inference.

**Series mapping (new)**

- **No. 1 / no. 4:** the FE 392.1 + FE 542.1 + FE 543.2 + CG 493.5 definition block; COL 330.3.
- **No. 3:** COL 330.3 ("physical, mental, and moral … body, mind, and soul"); Ed 207.2; FE 146.3.
- **No. 37 / no. 31:** CT 149.2 / CG 295.2, practical and literary training combined, read in context (domestic duties beside study).
- **No. 95 (Where you live is part of the lesson):** his list of what country living trains (attention, responsibility, cause and effect, industry, economy), with no fear framing. Needs EGW support of its own; he reads none here.
- **No. 74 (The greatest want of the world):** CE 51.1 / CT 505.3 ("aims to reach the highest attainments for the purpose of doing others good").
- **NEW candidate: "A school for the ones who failed."** Promise: children written off as slow can learn when hands, mind and faith are trained together. KJV: 1 Cor 1:27–28; Ps 113:7; Prov 22:29. EGW: CT 307.3; Ed 218.1; CT 153.1 ("must be saved if possible"). The Kibidula story needs a primary source first.

### D2 · True Education P2 - In Pity For Our Weakness (a8qrE2KB1Co) · 41:32 · speaker: Joshua White

Transcript: `true-education-3-part/02-in-pity-for-our-weakness.txt` (ytt --json)

**Thesis**

- God has a Plan A. When sin spoils it, He mercifully brings in a Plan B that keeps the same goal and changes only the method. His examples: the plan of salvation; Christ as the Father's representative; the tabernacle; the written word; the schools.
- The family is Plan A. Schools are Plan B: "necessary … because of sin, because of our weakness", ordained by God, but "an aid to parents", never their replacement. A counterfeit Plan B replaces the family, and a true Plan B can turn into the goal, as the temple did.

**Outline**

- 0:00:00–0:03:44 Daniel in 605 BC as an imagined interior monologue ("as Daniel pondered these things…"). Isa 43:10 "ye are my witnesses". PK 705.3: "Not by any temporary failure of Israel … was the plan of the ages … to be frustrated."
- 0:03:44–0:06:21 Plan A / Plan B defined: "God doesn't change his mind; we change our circumstances through sin." "You may have heard it said that school is plan B, but God's plan B is just as important often as his plan A."
- 0:06:21–0:08:57 **Plan B 1, the plan of salvation:** Ed 15.1–15.2. The goal (the image restored in body, mind and soul) is unchanged.
- 0:08:57–0:11:03 **Plan B 2, God's connection to man:** Ed 20.3, Ed 21.1 (the Father "personally directed their education … face to face held communion with the Eternal"). After the Fall, Christ stands as the Father's representative (Ed 33.2); Ed 30.3 ("we behold the light of the knowledge of His glory in the face of Christ").
- 0:11:03–0:13:40 **Plan B 3, the tabernacle:** Ed 34.3 (Sinai's training) and Ed 35.1, "In pity for their weakness, God gave them a symbol of His presence" (Ex 25:8). The title comes from here.
- 0:14:14–0:15:49 **Plan B 4, the written word:** Ed 16.3. Nature still speaks, "but these revelations are partial and imperfect … we need the fuller revelation … in His written word."
- 0:15:49–0:18:53 **Plan B 5, the schools.** Every Plan B was redemptive and therefore educational (Ed 15.2, Ed 30.2, CT 49.3 "the great purpose of all the education and discipline of life").
- 0:18:53–0:24:38 Plan A was the family school: Ed 33.1–33.3, Ed 34.1, Ed 40.3 with Deut 6:6–7, FE 95.3 ("the home and the school were one … in the place of stranger lips"), Ed 44.3 ("the education imparted by God to the parents, and by them to be given to their children"). "Plan A was so much better that the Lord was hanging on to it as long as he could." Ed 45.1: the plan was "but partially and imperfectly fulfilled".
- 0:24:38–0:26:42 **The ellipsis he restores.** First he shows Ed 46.1 cut down to "God provided other agencies … Samuel … established the schools of the prophets" and asks, "Plan B: schools train the children instead of the parents?" Then he restores the cut text: "as an aid to parents in the work of education … for the training of such a class of teachers". He reads it as schools that trained teachers who would instruct the people (and so the parents). Ed 46.2 + FE 96.2 read as one (DIFFERS, a splice).
- 0:26:42–0:31:22 Does Plan B replace Plan A? No more than the tabernacle replaced God's wish to dwell in hearts, or the Bible replaced nature. "Is plan B necessary? It absolutely is." The home is the best place: CT 107.1, CT 109.1, CT 130.1, CT 158.2, CT 162.3 ("Neither the church school nor the college affords the opportunities…"). Schools are needed "because of the cruel neglect of many parents" (CT 204.4) and for children "parents have neglected to educate" (CT 153.1). He points back to Kibidula.
- 0:31:22–0:33:28 **The stuck car.** A friend pushes; you don't shut off the engine, set the brake and fold your arms. "The church school is an aid, the extra push" (CT 167.1). Home missionary training (MYP 225.2), schools as "the Lord's special instrumentality" (CT 149.1), "let fathers and mothers co-operate with the teacher" (CT 161.1), church schools "ordained by God" (6T 203.1).
- 0:33:28–0:36:38 **Lesson 1, beware counterfeit Plan Bs.** Salvation by works ("very exemplified in the Catholic Church"), saints and Mary as mediators, shrines and images, "man's words and writings about God". Counterfeit schools are ones that do not teach students to know God: no Bible at the foundation, mental-only, selfish, aimed at worldly success, academics before 8–10, "preschool, daycare, kindergarten … instead of the mother", reflectors, unsupervised associations.
- 0:36:38–0:39:46 **Lesson 2, don't let the method become the goal.** Israel trusted the temple services instead of the One they pointed to, "so when Jesus came they did not recognize him". "Has this become our pride? … Is it helping the family or replacing the family?" Parental-neglect texts: CG 234.4, CT 204.4 (Satan beside neglected children), ST Nov 10, 1881 par. 13 (Eli), 1T 219.1 ("the curse of God will surely rest upon unfaithful parents").
- 0:39:46–0:41:32 Close: CT 107.1 ("If the child is not instructed aright here, Satan will educate him through agencies of his choosing. How important, then, is the school in the home!"). "It worked for Moses … David … Elijah … Jesus."

**Bible texts**

- 0:00:32 Isa 43:10 / 44:8 (alluded: "ye are my witnesses"); Isa 49:6 / 60:3 (alluded: a light to the nations).
- 0:09:28 Gen 2:8–9 (inside Ed 20.3). 0:09:28 Gen 3:8 (alluded: "cool of the day", inside Ed 21.1). 0:10:00 Luke 3:38 (inside Ed 33.1).
- 0:11:03 Isa 40:12 (inside Ed 34.3). 0:13:09 Ex 25:8 (quoted, inside Ed 35.1). 0:10:32 2 Cor 4:6 (inside Ed 30.3).
- 0:22:04 Deut 6:6–7 (quoted inside Ed 40.3; "in the evening after work" is his joke). 0:28:47 Eph 6:4 (inside CT 109.1).
- 0:36:38 John 1:29 (alluded: "the lamb of God"). 0:38:44 1 Sam 2–3 (Eli, alluded via ST 1881).
- 0:04:46 Gentile mission after Israel's failure (Acts 13:46, alluded).

**EGW quotes** (35 rows: 34 VERIFIED, 1 DIFFERS; detail in `verification.md`)

- 0:03:12 PK 705.3 (**context:** Malachi's day and the Gentiles; he applies it to Daniel's captivity, and the principle holds). 0:06:52 Ed 15.1. 0:07:54 Ed 15.2. 0:09:28 Ed 20.3, Ed 21.1. 0:10:00 Ed 33.1. 0:10:32 Ed 33.2 (rest read at 0:19:58), Ed 30.3. 0:11:03 Ed 34.3. 0:12:38 Ed 35.1. 0:14:14 Ed 16.3 (dup 0:18:22). 0:18:53 CT 49.3 (= CCh 202.4).
- 0:20:30 Ed 33.3 (**he starts at "the men who held fast"**, so the cities sentence is not read). 0:21:00 Ed 34.1. 0:22:04 Ed 40.3. 0:23:06 FE 95.3, Ed 44.3. 0:23:37 Ed 45.1. 0:24:38 Ed 46.1 (read twice, shortened and then whole).
- 0:26:10 Ed 46.2 + FE 96.2: **DIFFERS** (splice; the "If a youth was eager…" sentence is FE 96.2 = RH Oct 30, 1900, not Ed).
- 0:28:16 CT 107.1 (dup 0:39:46). 0:28:47 CT 109.1, CT 130.1. 0:29:18 CT 158.2 (**context:** children of "small companies of Sabbathkeepers" are "needed in their homes"; it is about not sending them away to school, so it fits his use), CT 162.3. 0:29:50 CT 204.4 (dup 0:38:44; CT 205.1 goes on to "send them to schools … Scriptural foundation", as `verification.md` notes). 0:30:21 CT 153.1. 0:31:22 CT 167.1. 0:32:25 MYP 225.2, CT 149.1. 0:32:58 CT 161.1, 6T 203.1.
- 0:38:14 CG 234.4 (first sentence; the source's subject is "blind parental affection"). 0:38:44 ST Nov 10, 1881 par. 13 (= 2BC 1009.3). 0:39:15 1T 219.1 (**cut:** the skipped sentence names the cause, "false sympathy and blind love … excuse the faults").
- Allusion, not counted: 0:00:00 "Acts of the Apostles … we don't expect and ask for more of the outpouring" (nearest AA 50.2).

**Science and history**

- 605 BC for Daniel's captivity: standard. The "Plan B" scheme is his own frame and makes no historical claim.
- No studies cited. "Conventional education encourages selfishness" (0:35:35) is his assertion.

**Cautions**

- **The Catholic counterfeit list** (0:34:00–0:35:03: works salvation "very exemplified in the Catholic Church", saints, Mary) is anti-Catholic framing. Keep it out.
- **"Preschool, daycare, kindergarten" as Satan's counterfeit** (0:35:35) condemns families' choices. The series keeps the start-age counsel (no. 10) without that rhetoric.
- **Fear and curse language** at the close (CT 204.4's Satan beside the child; Eli's curse; "the blood of their souls"): VERIFIED, but heavy. The sources put the cause in indulgence and "not restraining", and the series' no. 108 (Eli's house) carries it gently.
- **His reading of Ed 46.1.** "Schools … to help train teachers to teach the parents how to teach their children" goes a step past the text. Ed 46.1–46.2 says the teachers were to "instruct the people in the works and ways of God", and the schools were also for "the mental and spiritual welfare of the youth". "An aid to parents" is in the text; "teachers of parents" is his inference.
- Daniel's 605 BC thoughts are an imagined monologue: a dramatized reconstruction, not fiction proper. Flag it if reused.
- "The visions of the night" (CT 204.4) and "the prophet of the Lord" are insider framing.
- Strengths: he restores the cut text of Ed 46.1 himself, and the whole talk is a careful corrective to "school replaces the family" _and_ to "school is not needed". It is the balanced position the series takes (ritsema-review §4.2).

**Series mapping**

- **No. 79 Was school God's first plan?** The Plan A / Plan B frame with Ed 33.1–33.3, 34.1, 44.3, 45.1, 46.1 (whole); "is plan B necessary? It absolutely is … not to replace the family". Best single source for no. 79's balance.
- **No. 24 Israel's colleges:** Ed 46.1–46.2; FE 96.2 (the open door for a youth "eager to obtain a better knowledge of the Scriptures … that he might become a teacher in Israel").
- **No. 9 / no. 92:** CT 107.1 (whole, including "If the child is not instructed aright here, Satan will educate him through agencies of his choosing"); CT 162.3; CT 158.2 ("the well-ordered Christian home … is the best place for them"); Ed 44.3 ("imparted by God to the parents, and by them … to their children").
- **No. 14 The generation that forgot:** Ed 45.1 ("Fathers and mothers in Israel became indifferent…"); Ed 40.3.
- **No. 108 Eli's house:** ST Nov 10, 1881 par. 13; 1T 219.1 (with the cut sentence restored); CG 234.4.
- **No. 100 Why didn't they recognize Him?** The method became the goal: temple services trusted in place of the One they pictured.
- **NEW candidate: "Plan B is mercy."** Promise: when we fail God's first plan, He gives a second way to the same goal and never lowers the goal. KJV: Ex 25:8; Isa 40:12; Gen 3:15; 2 Cor 4:6. EGW: Ed 35.1 ("In pity for their weakness"); Ed 30.3; Ed 16.3; Ed 46.1. A strong E-episode frame for parents who feel they started late or must use a school.
- **NEW candidate: "The push from behind."** Promise: a good school helps a family that keeps its own engine running; it cannot replace one that has switched off. KJV: Deut 6:6–7; Eph 6:4; Prov 22:6. EGW: CT 167.1 ("an aid to the parents"); CT 161.1 ("co-operate with the teacher"); CT 162.3. The stuck-car picture is the visual.
